# Tiruppavai

## 1. Margali tingal

*mārgaḻit tingaḷ madi niṟainda nannāḷāl nīrādap pōduvīr pōduminō nēr iḻaiyīr sīr malugum āyppādic celvac cirumīrgāḷ kūrvēl kodun-toḻilan nandagōpan kumaran ērārnda kaṇṇi yasōdai iḷam singam kār mēnic cengaṇ kadir madiyam pōl mugattān nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān parōr pugaḻap padindelōr empāvāi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

sīr malgum āyppādi – (From the) wealthy place of āyppādi; selvac cirumīrgāḷ – O, the young gōpikās who have got the best wealth (which is association with, and kainkaryam to, kriṣṇan); nēr iḻaiyīr – who are wearing wonderful ornaments (keeping kriṣṇan in mind); mārgaḻi tingaḷ – we got the best of all months – the mārgaḻi month, and the good full moon day for doing the nōnbu (ceremony to pray for the rains)); kūr vēl – (Standing with a) sharp spear; kodum toḻilan – if any enemies come near his dear kriṣṇan he would be a person doing cruel deeds (to those enemies) – that person is; nandagōpan – nandagōpan, whose; kumaran – dear son is kriṣṇan; ēr ārnda kaṇṇi yasōdai – the one who has got beautiful eyes; iḷam singam – her young lion-cub is kriṣṇan; kār mēni, sengaṇ – (his) body is the color of dark (kind) clouds, he has got eyes lotus-like; kadir madiyam pōl mugattān – and his face is the bright light of the moon; nārāyaṇanē – and who is none other than srīman nārāyaṇan; namakkē paṟai taruvān – only srīman nārāyaṇan can give us (us who depend only on him) the opportunity to do kainkaryam to Him; Al – So; padindēlōr – those who want to take a good (padindu) bath (enjoyment with kriṣṇan), please go with us; pārōr pugaḻa – and the people of this world would celebrate that

**Перевод:**

**This first pāsuram is briefly indicating that what will be achieved (i.e., prāpakam/upēyam) is parai (kainkaryam to perumāḷ).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 1st pāsuram of tiruppāvai, the gōpikās are celebrating that they got the month of mārgaḻi for doing their nōnbu. First 3 lines of the pāsuram are about prāpyam (means), \[being together with bhāgavatās\], and rest are about prāpakam (destiny/puruśārtam) \[kriṣṇan\].*

***mārgaḻit tingaḷ*****:**

They are celebrating that they got the good mārgaḻi month for them to do kainkaryams to [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/);  like His lotus feet, mārgaḻi is also not too hot or too cold; kriṣṇan also said in gītā that of all the months He is like mārgaḻi, so this is the best month for Him too; mārgaḻi is the brāhma muhūrtam (serene/tranquil time) of the year (not only for us but also for dēvas because, since our 1 year is 1 day for them, our mārgaḻi month is their 4-6am) – so it is a good month in that sense too *\[1\]*; the old people of āyarpādi would not come out early due to the mārgaḻi month being cold, which means enjoyment with kriṣṇan as they pleased (*தங்களுக்கு கிருஷ்ணனோடே யதேஷ்டம் விநியோகிக்கலாம் என்றபடி*); people are happy that this is the good month to do kainkaryams to [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is happy that he got an opportunity to take care of the people (‘ayana’ of the word [nārāyaṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as occurs in the pāsuram).

***madi niṟainda:***

madi: moon; nirainda: full (Full moon);  Full moon being sukla pakśam, is it a good day for doing a good deed? When a person is doing a kainkaryam to [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), vidi/sāstram will follow what s/he is doing and help her/him, so it is a good day; they are happy about it being a full moon day also because the girls can see each other’s’ happy faces and can go together to wake up kriṣṇan; vouldn’t it be better to go on a dark day to be with kriṣṇan? Since the people of āyarpādi themselves agreed for the gōpikās to do nōnbu with help from kriṣṇan, gōpikās are okay with the place being bright due to full moon; also madi (another meaning: gnyānam/wisdom) is being complete for the people because this is the day they came along to go to kriṣṇan.

***nan-nāḷāl:***

What a day, like a central piece of a month and fortnight! They are celebrating that it is a ‘good day’. How is it a good day? First, because it is a bright day, it brings more happiness to the occasion; the gōpars had restricted them from seeing kriṣṇan, and today those *same* people have completely approved them to (do nōnbu and) be with kriṣṇan; a day marked for getting the experience of kriṣṇan is a good day;  for a person who thought s/he was independent (svatantran) till yesterday the good day dawns only on the day s/he realizes that [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is our only saviour (and helps us do kainkaryam to Him); day we do *panca samskāram* is our birth day; day we leave this *samsāram* is our wedding day (with Him);

What a desire in celebrating the month, fortnight, day, etc.?! This flow of affection for these gōpikās is because of their desire for Him (prāpyam).

***nīrāda***

To reduce their *viraha tāpam* (because of not meeting kriṣṇan for many days) they want to take a dip (in connecting with kriṣṇan); ‘*neerada*’ also means கலவி (union) with kriṣṇan (in olden days, *tamiḻ* people also used the word ‘(neer) *sunai aadal*‘ to mean கலவி),

Among friends why did they not use the casual word ‘*kuḷikka*‘ for bath? It is due to the bhagavat sambandam among them. They give such respect even when it is their son or siśya – ***how srī*** [***parāsara bhattar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/)***‘s mother (named āṇḍāḷ) would take the sri pāda tīrtam (water that was used for washing the feet of a noble person) of*** [***bhattar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) ***without thinking of him as a son – because of respect for*** [***bhattar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) ***as an ācāryan and because he is in the siśya paramparai of*** [***rāmānujar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)***;    and this respect is also similar to how*** [***periya nambi***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/periya-nambi/) ***who is an ācāryan of*** [***rāmānujar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) ***would prostrate upon seeing*** [***rāmānujar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) ***with his huge gōśti of disciples because*** [***periya nambi***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/periya-nambi/) ***saw*** [***rāmānujar***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) ***as the person blessed by his own ācāryan*** [***āḷavandār***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) ***for addressing his (***[***āḷavandār***](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)***‘s) wishes. These are similar because all of them are about a higher level deed related to kainkaryam / bhagavat sambandam..***

***pōduvīr pōduminō***

For low-level (materialistic, etc.,) goals itself one would have to gather and use a lot of money/strength/bravery/good-luck, etc. If that is the case, would it require a lot more of these and then some more, if one wants to enjoy kriṣṇan?? No, only requirement is the “Desire” to do so. By ‘pōduvīr’ they are calling the people who have that wish to go with them to serve kriṣṇan. The former ones are undesirable goals, so they required us to gain eligibility;

As a cētanan (subservient to [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) only the wish to do kainkaryam is required. Parents and other elders, [āḻvārs](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), [ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) have already taken care of other things required to qualify us to do the kainkaryam. *All these days, only our wish was missing.* Now even that is present. So they are saying ‘those who have got the wish, let us go and see kriṣṇan’. For other low-level wishes one would want to go alone; since these gōpikās are going for bhagavat kainkaryam, they are calling their friends also to join them, by saying ‘*pōduvīr*‘.

Since it is clear that the girls are already interested, what is the need for saying pōdimin**O**, that is, pressing them to go? Like [tāyār (pirātti)](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) it is their nature to correct/advise; it also means – ‘we want to live well so please move right now and quickly let us go and meet kriṣṇan’; also they want their friends to go in the front and they want to walk behind them and enjoy that company.

**nēr iḻaiyīr**

“O girls who are wearing (neatly worn?) ornaments”. As they said *pōduminō* the ones invited became like new.

When they are in a hurry to go and take bath, would they have taken time to decorate themselves with ornaments??! They must have decorated themselves in a hurry and must have worn ornaments wrongly, that is, meant for some other part of the body, so it must have looked different and new, so they were addressed as ‘*nēr iḻaiyīr*‘;  or, since they are always thinking of kriṣṇan, and since they don’t know when kriṣṇan might come and fall on them (*அவன் எப்போது வந்து மேல்விழும்*?), they are in the habit of always wearing ornaments, so that is why they were addressed as ‘nēr iḻaiyīr’; or, because of the happiness that they have about their previous encounters with kriṣṇan they always feel and look great;

**sīr malgum āyppādi –** wealthy place that is āyppādi

Because of kriṣṇan’s presence in āyppādi, it is having the real wealth; kriṣṇan’s *sovlabhyam* (being easy to approach, being simple) is so wealthy that its waves are going out from āyppādi and reaching into srī vaikuntam.  *perumān does not have any need or way to show one of his kalyāṇa guṇas – sovlabyam – when he is sitting in srī vaikuṇṭam. That is because the* [*ātmā*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)*s there are all mukta ātmās full of jñānam etc., and perumān is all available to them; so there is no opportunity to show his sovlabhyam to them; So* [*perumāḷ*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) *is more happy to be in āyppādi among the common people so that he can put that character of His in to good use.*

Only in darkness the light would seem very bright, so [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is very happy to come to this world to exercise the full power of one of his *kalyāṇa guṇas* (divine qualities), that is *sovlabhyam*. Because of His presence, *āyppādi* is wealthy of his *guṇas*; and his *guṇas* are wealthy in *āyppādi*. He, who is bigger than the three worlds, has made himself available for the commands of *gōpikās* in this place – *āyppādi*.

*āyppādi* has been content and hence wealthy with cows that provided enough milk, so they were all happy with getting the milk and making the butter (*கறப்பன, கடைவனவற்றால் குறைவற்றிருக்கை*).

What is so special about *āyppādi*? īsn’t it just some small place? It is the divine place where one can go to without having to leave this body (which has to be done to go to srī vaikuṇṭam); *āyppādi* is not the place like *ayōdya* where great saints/jñānis like *vaśiśtar* lived – it is just a place where *gōpikās* live who are so innocent that they can’t differentiate between their left and right arms; it is not a place that enjoyed the wonderful characters of [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (rāmar), but the place that enjoys looking at all the mischief of kriṣṇan.

**selva cirumīrgāḷ! –** O, wealthy girls!

What is the wealth this is referring to? It is the association that all the gōpikās are having with *perumān*. When *lakśmaṇan* went behind *rāman* to the forest empty-handed, he was referred by vālmīki as ‘*lakśmaṇo lakshmi (wealth) sampanna:*‘, because *lakśmaṇan* got the real wealth that is *rāma kainkaryam*. Similarly when *vibhīśaṇāḻvān* left *lankai* empty handed and approached to do saranāgaty to [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), he was referred to as ‘*srīmān*‘ (wealthy), because only now *vibhīśaṇan* got the real wealth – that is association with rāman;   *gajēndran*, the elephant – when he decided that there is no own effort that can save his life, and then called  *perumān* for help, *gajēndrāḻvār* was referred to as ‘*srīmān*‘ (wealthy) – again it is the association / surrender to *emperumān* that is shown as the real wealth for us. (This association is called ‘*bhagavat sambhandam*‘).

Difference among the above three association examples:

–         lakśmaṇan from birth did Not have any *svātantriyam* (any feeling of being independent from perumān), or *anya śēśatvam* (being subservient to someone other than [srīman nārāyāṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)). So he was fully subservient to rāman from birth.

–         *vibhīśaṇan* did not have any *svātantriyam* either but he did have ‘*anya śēśatvam*‘ that is, he was subservient to *rāvaṇan*. When that was also lost, he became wealthy because of attaining *ananyārha śēśatvam* towards rāman.

–         *gajēndrāḻvān* had *svātantriyam* at first (when he thought he could save by Only on own efforts), but he did not have any *anya śēśatvam* – he served garlands only to *emperumān*. But when *gajēndrāḻvān* shed his svātantriyam by calling only [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to help, he became wealthy too.

The *gōpikās* of *āyppādi* are wealthy because of their association with *kriṣṇan* – what with kriṣṇan not tolerating the separation of any of the 5 ḷakhs gōpikas of āyppādi, their playing together, and kriṣṇan showing his *kāruṇyam* (mercy/affection) when the gōpikās are not able to tolerate their separation from kriṣṇan.

What is meant by ‘*sirumeergāḷ*‘ – it is that all the gōpikās are a match to kriṣṇan in age and ability to play (kriṣṇan is also an *iḷam singam* (lion-cub)); kriṣṇan, when seeing the males and those who have become women, will stay away from them, similar to how he would stay away from those who do poojas to other *devatas* (than [nārāyaṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) and from those who possess *svātantriyam* (feeling independent from perumān and so doing things forbidden by Him or not doing things required by Him). This is to show that He is so much fond of those young *gōpikās* who are a good match to his age and ability to play. It is also to show how *gōpikās* are so innocent, so devoted to kriṣṇan and his *cēśtitams* (actions/mischief), and making sure that without *gōvindan* getting it first, they don’t put anything into their mouth.

**===\> kūr vēl kodum toḻilan nandagōpan :**

Earlier nandagōpan would be so careful to not even step on a fresh grass; like [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/)’s siśya *siriyāttān* he was also very kind; after getting these children, thinking ‘what danger might come to them’, nandagōpan started standing with a well-sharpened spear (சாணை பிடித்து).

***(kodum toḻilan***) – If nandagōpan sees an ant down there (near kriṣṇan) , he would jump on it with so much anger and vigour that is fit for attacking a lion; and get blood-stains on his hands (from the blood flowing down from the spear!).

What about vēdas saying not to hurt any living being? vēdas say ‘sāmānya dharma’ – that which one should usually follow, and ‘visēśa dharma’ – like to save a cow one could tell lies. In this case, *nandagōpan* is doing this with fear of danger to kriṣṇan’s head (what with so many rakśasās going into the one-way path that is to āyppādi to try to hurt kriṣṇan).

(**nandagōpan**) – Why is [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) using this word in the pāsuram instead of just saying *‘kūr vēl koduntoḻilan kumaran*‘?  It is to imply that *nandagōpan* is happy seeing his son. Also, like how [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) would be affectionate to Her, She is using this name to imply that kriṣṇan’s father also will be affectionate to **her** and friends and will let kriṣṇan be with them. That is the same reason for waking him up (in pasuram 17) by saying ‘*nandagōpalā eḻundirāi* (nandagōpa! please wake up)’, to imply that he would help the girls meet kriṣṇan.

**===\> kumaran**

When the people of āyppādi go and complain to nandagōpar, saying, ‘He stole our butter, He stole our girls, (made mess everywhere?)’, then nandagōpar would say ‘If kriṣṇan comes anywhere near the vicinity of my eye sight I will set him straight (*என் கண் வட்டத்திலே வந்து தோற்றினானாகில் நியமிக்கக் கடவேன்*).  But when kriṣṇan does appear in front of nandagōpan, seeing kriṣṇan’s disciplined and innocent behaviour like a little lion cub (hence, ‘kumaran’), nandagōpar would say “My blunder! Did you say all these bad things about **such** a sweet boy?” – getting back at those who complained. (*கெட்டேன்! இவனையோ இங்ஙனே பழியிட்டது*?).

**===\> āer ārnda kaṇṇi yaśōdai**

Beauty is resident in yaśōdai pirātti’s eyes; since she is always looking at her son, it is reflecting in her eyes as beauty (krishna darśan makes our eyes beautiful) ;  aren’t they the eyes that saw the little face which pretended to cry, look at her mother as if scared, and with hands together plead her to untie him?

(yaśōdai) – she is amicable to them to wake her up ‘*yasōdāi aṟivurāi*’ and get what they wanted.

**===\> iḷam singam**

Since nandagōpar tends to do corrections for making Him good (as fathers do), kriṣṇan behaves like a little cub (hence [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) says ‘*nandagōpan kumaran*‘). But since yaśōdai will treat him with affection only, kriśnan, in front of her, behaves like a lion, and with a lot of pride (hence, ‘*yaśōdai’s iḷam singam*‘); yaśōdai would not make Him afraid by scolding (shouting) – she will let him do His mischief, going along with whatever He did; if anyone comes and complains to her about Him, (and only when it becomes extreme) she might scold;  iḷam singam – age that matches those of the little girls of āyppādi; *parāsara bhattar* says He is a *சிங்கக் குருகு (‘மேலெழப் பறந்து பாயும் சிங்கக் குட்டி’*). kurugu refers to small birds; kutty refers to small. singak Kurugu implies that kriṣṇan is like a lion cub that jumps on you as if flying in the air.

**===\>** **kār mēni**

kār implies both black, and beautiful.  He who was prayed by [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) to come and give darśan like a black cloud full of clean water (helps people), is the same boy who is visible to one and all in āyppādi.   As much as the gōpikas’ parents tried to keep them away from Him, he is so beautiful that it is impossible to be away from Him.

**===\> sengaṇ**

– Beautiful eyes matching the beautiful *tirumēni* (divine body of kriṣṇan);

– His affection from inside is showing through His eyes – His affection for we people, without becoming angry at our mistakes which we make as human beings.

– He is the king.  As He has got all that is wealth, it reflects in His eyes.

– He comforts us with His beauty, and looks at us with affection.

**kadir madiyam pōl mugattān**

– Face that is the moon that got its brightness from the sun and with its own nicety; kadir indicates that He provides brightness that helps and be accessible and enjoyable to those who want to befriend kriṣṇan; at the same time it would punish those who want to oppose Him (kamsan, the asuras, ..).

–  To emphasize about kriṣṇan’s face not being comparable to anything, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) says ‘*mugattān*‘ – uses a metaphor instead of a simile.

**nārāyaṇanē** (all the descriptions above are about kriṣṇan who is nārāyaṇan)

**– How do gōpikās know that kriṣṇan is** [**nārāyaṇan**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)**? This avataram, by lifting the gōvardana mountain with its little finger and with other mischief which humans cannot do, has made it easy for gōpikās to know that He is the sarvēśvaran (god for all, that is** [**nārāyaṇan**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)**);** also the people of āyppādi do not know other gods, they would use only this word as part of the nōnbu, as they know it is kriṣṇan that is nārāyaṇan. Her ([āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)’s) fathers ([āḻvārs](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)) also used this word only in many pāsurams (*nān kaṇḍu koṇḍēn nārayaṇā ennum nāmam, tiṇṇam naraṇam, nāraṇanai nangu arindēn*); like how riśis also used this word (*nārāyana śabhdamātram, ēśa nārāyaṇa: srīmān, nārāyaṇa parabrahma*).

**nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān**

nārāyaṇanē implies that He by Himself would show His love to us, even when we don’t realize/accept that He is *antaryāmi* (He is inside our ātmā); this is because of His affection towards us.

**namakkē** – only to us – us who have shed our *svātantryam* (shedding any thought that we are independent from [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) – shedding by *deciding* that it is against nature to do something thinking that we did it all by ourselves (even if we have the wherewithal to do something) (ākincanyam); and the ‘us’ who do not have any power are looking for only His helping hands  (ananya-gaditvam (no other place/devatai/strength to go to for help except Him, for enabling to do kainkaryam to Him)).

**paṟai taruvān** – While the instrument ‘paṟai’ is to be obtained from kriṣṇan to be used in their *nōnbu*, they are actually talking about the ‘*paṟai*‘ that is *pāratantryam* (ability to be subservient to Him; kainkaryam). How do we know that that is what they are actually talking about – they themselves say it in pāsuram 29 – *iṟṟai paṟai koḷvān anṟu kāṇ, gōvindā, eṟṟaikkum ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum* … (No, not this instrument paṟai, hey gōvinda, but the subservience to you in all the births);

==\> It says paṟai “*taruvān*” (he will give) instead of ‘*koḷvōm*‘ (we will ~take), because they understand that even if they are eligible, or even if it is possible, only He should give it out of His will and then they should get that (paṟai).

**pārōr pugaḻa –** for the people to appreciate/celebrate.

Those who had not allowed us and kriṣṇan to be together, and also those who had allowed, are now appreciating and celebrating about kriṣṇan’s help of giving us the tools for doing the nōnbu, and about us girls getting involved in the nōnbu (for praying to get rains).

Even though they (the elders) don’t fully understand the experience of us girls being together with kriṣṇan, they still celebrate that togetherness. They would celebrate that the whole āyppādi got the rains and became fertile because of our nōnbu.

**padindu** (this goes with ‘*padindu nīrāda pōduvīr* – that is, take bath **well**.(or, **fully** enjoy kriṣṇan)

**ēl** – Listen (and)

**ōr** – keep it in your mind (become determined/ committed),

**ēm pāvāi** – our dear girl

-ēl ōr ēmpāvāi is also part of nonbu’s phrase (thought) which does not have a specific meaning).

-keep it in mind and follow.

-Since this is about getting together and enjoying kriṣṇan, ‘*pāvāi*‘ addresses rati dēvi (love goddess).

The first pāsuram celebrates the good time that became available, talks about the people who are eligible for doing kainkaryam (anybody who have got that ‘wish’), explains the beauty of kriṣṇan and how nandagōpan and yasōdai cannot hide kriṣṇan’s beauty from others, talks about upāyam/upēyam (Him and kainkaryam respectively), and ended with saying that even those who did not want them to be with kriṣṇan are also celebrating their union with kriṣṇan.

This pāsuram referred to ‘para svarūpam’ of emperumān (nārāyaṇan).

————–

\[1\] kriṣṇa also says in rutu He is vasanta rutu – they are the months of citra, and vaikāsi – they are the tiru avatāra months of [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) and [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) respectively – kānci svāmi.

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 2. Vaiyattu

*vaiyattu vāḻvīrgāḷ nāmum nam pāvaikku ceiyum kirisaigaḷ kēḷīrō pāṟkadaluḷ paiya tuyinṟa paraman adi pādi ney uṇṇōm pāl uṇṇōm nātkālē nīrādi mai ittu eḻudōm malarittu nām mudiyōm seyyādana seyyōm tīkkuraḷai sendru ōdōm aiyamum piccaiyum āndanaiyum kai kātti uyyumāru eṇṇi ugandēlōr empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāḻvīrgāḷ – O you who are born to have fulfilling life; vaiyattu – in this world; nāmum – we, who live thinking that we achieve our destiny because of Him; uyyum āṟu eṇṇi – we realize the means for achieving the destiny; ugandu – so with happiness; kēḷīrō – listen (to us about); seyyum kirisaigaḷ – the tasks that we do; nam pāvaikku – for our nōnbu; adi pādi – we sing praises of the lotus feet of; paiya tuyinṟa paraman – the almighty who is lying down with scheming thoughts; pāṟkadaluḷ – in the milky ocean; aiyamum – we give things to appropriate persons, and; piccaiyum – give bikśa (alms) which are given to brahmacāris and sanyāsis; āndanaiyum – till the level they are able to receive; kai kātti – we give that much; nei uṇṇōm – we would not eat ghee; pāl uṇṇōm – would not consume milk; nīrādi – will bathe; nāt kālē – early in the morning; mai ittu eḻudōm – we won’t decorate our eyes; malar ittu nām mudiyōm – won’t decorate our hair with flowers; seyyādana seyyōm – would not do what our pūrvācāryas did not do; senṟu ōdōm – would not go and tell emperumān; tīkkuraḷai – any gossip that creates problems for others

**Перевод:**

**vaiyattu vāḻvīrgāḷ**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the matter of materialistic wishes, what is liked today won’t be liked tomorrow, what is not liked today will be liked tomorrow – this is due to one’s own karma – so it is hard to tell the difference between what is good and what is bad (since what is desired keeps changing). But in the case of wishing to do kainkaryam to emperumān, it is very clear as to what is good and what is to be avoided. So, for the girls who (in the previous pāsuram) made up their mind to devote to do kainkaryams, it would be easy to identify good and bad and follow accordingly. Previous pāsuram talked about nature of prāpyam (goal), prāpakam (means), and that of the adikāri (person who wants to achieve the goal). In this pāsuram, it talks about do’s and don’ts for such a person.*

– You the most fortunate people who are living in this world, the world to which the sarvēsvaran (god for all) has come to live among you as kriṣṇan (as if He is a layman).

– Specifically saying ‘*vaiyattu*‘ – to show that this place is better in many respects compared to even *srī vaikuṇṭam* – because [perumāḷ](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is able to show many of his *kalyāṇa guṇas* (divine characteristics) only in this world and not in srī vaikuṇṭam because in there every one is mukta ātma with no pāpams /mistakes etc., so it is more exciting to see [perumāḷ](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) showing his guṇas in this bhū lōkam; isn’t that why nitya sūris come to this world, to enjoy [emperumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s guṇas?

*hanuman* also did not desire to go to srī vaikuṇṭam where he won’t be able to see srī rāman with all his glorious guṇas that He had the opportunity to show us in this world only; only in this world could we see kriṣṇan praying – to his Mother to untie him – we won’t get that experience in srī vaikuṇṭam.

[āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) also have sung that they prefer less of srī vaikuṇṭam! (*accuvai peṟinum vēṇḍēn*, *kaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ maṟṟonṟinai kāṇāvē*, and more).

Like water in a desert, in this world people drowned in samsāram got the wonder that is kriṣṇan and so they are fortunate to live in this world.

(Speaking of going to *śrī vaikuṇṭam*, once [nanjeeyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/) was thinking loud, how come dasaratan went only to svargam (heaven) and not to *srī vaikuṇṭam* (mōkśam).  [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/)‘s comment was “he should have actually gone to *narakam* (hell) – *dasaratan* thought that his promise (*samānya darmam*) was more important; he did not think about how *perumāḷ* would walk in the forest with his soft lotus feet getting hurt (*visēśa darmam*). He might have gotten *svargam* because of the cakravarty’s bhājñam to have that *tiru magan*“.

**vāḻvīrgāḷ** – the gōpikās of āyppādi are the most fortunate because: they are of the same age and so got the friendship of kriṣṇan; they were born during the same time period of kriṣṇan (and not before or after His avatār); and that too these gōpikās were born in the same āyppādi where kriṣṇan was born (and not in some other place during the same time); how wonderful that all these good things came together for these gōpikās! Even being in the same place as His presence would be a blessing. Being there, and fulfillment of life are same.

While the gōpikās who are saying *vāḻvīrgāḷ* are themselves enjoying this opportunity, why are they celebrating only others (*vāḻvīrgāḷ*) as having the opportunity – they are not satisfied with just their enjoying it, so they are appreciating the others who are joining together and providing them the great kriṣṇa anubhavam (experience/enjoyment).

**nāmum** – we who are living with the thought the He is everything (upāyam (means), upēyam (destiny)) for us

**nam pāvaikku** – *nam*: our;  *pāvai*: nōnbu;

Our nōnbu is special because it is for creating good for the people and the place, and for those who are associated with them; with that goal we are waking up all the girls and kriṣṇan also in order to be with Him; asking to do this nōnbu (being with kriṣṇan on the pretext of doing the nōnbu) is like we are being paid to eat the sweet sugarcane;

**seyyum kirisaigaḷ –** as part of nōnbu we will follow all these ten do’s and don’ts (as part of being associated with [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)); though we look for His hand for everything, we do these for dēha yātirai/kālakśēpam – until we get what we want.

**kēḷīrō –** would you listen

They are so happy when the people were previously saying No to kriṣṇa and No to gōpikās for meeting each other, and now the gōpikās are able to meet and enjoy kriṣṇan – so they are telling everyone to listen to their happiness.

**==\>** Listening about [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is wonderful; whoever is talking about [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is not doing that for feeling like an ācāryan (teacher/greater soul);  who are listening are doing that not because they don’t know about it. It is just to talk and listen about [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) that they are coming together for the conversation. (*bōdayanta: parasparam (gītā)).*

*When the girls asked her to tell them about good things to follow:*

**pāṟkadaluḷ paiya tuyinṟa** ((kśīrābhdi nātan who is) sleeping in the ocean)

From srī vaikuṇṭam, [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) came down and is lying down on ādi sēśan, and thinking deeply about how to help us.

**paiya tuyinṟa** – Without enjoying with tāyārs, He with *kaḷḷam* is lying down with closed eyes as He is mainly concerned about the well being of us samsāris.

Why are they referring to kriṣṇan with the name *kśīrābhdi nātan*? As they have been suffering all these days because of not being able to meet kriṣṇan, now they are thinking that what god has set up for them (meeting with kriṣṇan as part of nōnbu) might be disturbed somehow – so they are saying the name of the god (in *sayanam* lying down on ādi śēśan in the ocean) so that the elders would be committed for them to do this *nōnbu* (with kriṣṇan).  They don’t want elders to say that they just sang only about kriṣṇan when going for nōnbu; so they sing the equivalent of Him in *kśeerābhdi*.

**paraman**

Referring to His beauty when seen lying down on ādi śēśan (tiru anantāḻvān); since He is present in the milky ocean like this for thinking about the well-being of people, *paraman* refers to his greatness also; great in every respect.

**adi pādi** – will sing about his lotus feet

As [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is thinking about the gōpikās’ well being, all that the gōpikās can think of is His *tiruvadigaḷ* (lotus feet) only, as that is where one goes to for getting [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s *anugraham* (blessing).

**==\> pādi nei uṇṇōm pāl uṇṇom –** Will not eat Ghee, Milk etc.

They are saying they would not eat such rich food, as they want to focus on the nōnbu and on being together with kriṣṇan; after eating kriṣṇan’s friendship, they don’t feel like eating anything else; why are they saying will not ‘eat’ milk/ghee, instead of saying ‘drink’ milk/ghee. It may be the way the covherd girls speak – we also refer to him as ‘veṇṇai **uṇḍa** vāyan’ ([āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) automatically got that, similar to how her body odor became that of covherd girls!);

**==\>** Another reason for saying ‘eat’ instead of ‘drink’ is that from the time kriṣṇan was born, others do not know how milk or ghee or butter would look like – because kriṣṇan consumes all those things as and when they are made – (so the gōpikās thought that it is some (dessert) item to eat).

[nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) also said ‘ellām kaṇṇan’, emphasizing that when kriṣṇan is there, rich food, etc., are not important;

If they don’t eat (they fast), it is kriṣṇan who will look like He was fasting, due to the care He has got for the girls.

**nātkālē nīrādi**  (take bath very early)

Take bath early, in case He shows up; take bath early so that we can go and do kainkaryam soon; take bath to quench the feeling of separation from Him; bharatan too, separated from perumāḷ, took bath very early to quench the feeling of separation.

**mai ittu eḻudōm** (won’t apply *mai* (*kājal*) to the eyes)

**malar ittu nām mudiyōm** (won’t wear flowers)

If they don’t apply *mai* in their eyes, kriṣṇan will not tolerate, and will feel that he should take care of them. He would give us flowers to wear before, but now we won’t wear during this nōnbu.

–\> **mai** gives brightness to eyes; jñāna yōgam gives brightness to ātmā; she is saying we will not follow jñāna yōgam for attaining perumān. (They say – we have told you that we are not capable of doing jñāna yōgam, that is the reason we surrendered ([samāsrayaṇam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html)) to you through our ācaryān, asking you to help us, we don’t have a way to reach you by our own efforts).

–\> **malar** – bhagavat anubhavam – can imply bhakti yōgam – we will not follow bhakti yōgam either, for reaching your lotus feet. Same reason as above, as we are saraṇāgatas.

If He comes and insists on putting the flowers on us, we would not be able to resist; we would not wear them on our own.

**seyyādana seyyōm** (Won’t do what our ancestors did not do (even if acceptable to vēdas))

Will not do kainkaryams for other low benefits; will not go to kriṣṇan without waking up all the gōpikās and getting together; bharatan also said I cannot do what our elders have not done (to become a king when one is not the eldest son); **even if vēdas allow us something, we will not do it if it is not followed by our** [**purvācaryas**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/)**. That is, even though we could, we would not go to Him directly, but only through bhāgavatas as support.**

**tīkkuraḷai cenṟu ōdōm** (will not go and gossip or wrongly complain (about others)); sīta dēvi also hid from tiruvadi (hanuman) about rākśasis giving so much trouble to Her; she did not say anything about that to srī rāman also even when they were alone comfortable in their bed;

if we girls said something bad to each other due to circumstances/out of friendship, we will not let [perumān](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) know about it;  on the same note,

**we will avoid letting the deficiencies of others that we see in front of our eyes to reach our heart –** [**perumān**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) **is antaryāmi – our thinking is equivalent to telling him;**

**aiyamum picaiyum** – we will give to the deserving/respected people, and to brahmacāris (another meaning – we will help people understand about kriṣṇa anubhavam (experience) and bhāgavata (devotees) anubhavam (repectively).

**āndanaiyum –** will help as much as they can take

**kai kātti –** but without showing any sign of having helped, and without having any thoughts that we did such help.

Also: aiyyam – knowledge about His nature and characteristics; picai – knowledge about nature of ātmā. letting others know about these as much as we are able to. kai kātti – even if we share our knowledge, we don’t feel that it is we who did it.

**uyyumāṟu eṇṇi** – by thinking that by following the above do’s and don’ts we will reach our goal (kriṣṇan), (uyyum āṟu – 6 vārtaigaḷ by dēva perumāḷ to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/); eṇṇi – think of that).

**eṇṇi ugandu** – thinking about reaching this great goal and becoming happy (and thinking about that goal we are happily doing the steps required for nōnbu, would you listen about that? (that is, ‘eṇṇi ugandu seyyum kirisaigaḷ kēḷīro?’)

**ēl ōr em pāvāi.**

In this pāsuram, the gōpikās talk about do’s and don’ts as part of nōnbu/kainkaryam, and in general how we view srīvaiṣṇavas.

This pāsuram talks about vyūha avatāram of emperumān *(kśeerābhdi nātan)*.

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 3. Ongi ulagalanda

*ōngi ulagaḷanda uttaman pēr pādi nāngaḷ nam pāvaikku cāṟṟi nīrādināl tīnginṟi nādēllām tingaḷ mummāri peidu ōngu perum sennel ūdu kayal ugaḷa pūnkuvaḷaip pōdil poṟi vaṇḍu kaṇ paduppa tēngadē pukkirundu sīrtta mulai paṟṟi vāngak kudam niraikkum vaḷḷal perum pasukkaḷ nīngāda selvam niṟaindēlorempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēr – the names; uttaman – of puruśōtaman; ōngi – the one who grew tall; aḷanda – and measured (with His lotus feet); ulagu – all the three worlds; nīradināl – if we bathed; nam pāvaikku cāṟṟi – with the front/pretense of a nōnbu; nādu ellām – the whole country; tīngu inṟi – without any bad; mummāri peidu – would properly rain; tingaḷ – every month, and; kayal ugaḷa – fish would jump around; (so) ōngu perum sennel ūdu – between the tall and healthy grass; poṟi vaṇḍu – and beautiful bees; kaṇ paduppa – would sleep; pūm-kuvaḷai pōdil – in beautiful flowers; tēngadē – without hesitation; pukku irundu – get to and try to; vānga – pull; sīrta mulai paṟṟi – by holding with both the hands the big nipples of; perum pasukkaḷ – healthily grown cows; kudam niṟaikkum – they would fill the containers; vaḷḷal – with generosity; nīngāda selvam – (such is the ) wealth that can be sustained; niṟaindu – and stay complete

**Перевод:**

**Note: Inner meaning about ācāryan/siśyan is interspersed in this text.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*This pāsuram is listing the benefits to the people who allowed the gōpikas to meet kriṣṇan (for doing nōnbu). Even if one devotee is present in a place the whole place would live well; all the side benefits would happen by themselves; with so many (5 lakhs) gōpikās being allowed to meet kriṣṇan, there is no limit to how much good will happen to this world.*

**ōngi ulagaḷanda**

In previous pāsurams, they started with saying nārāyaṇan (paratvam), then they said about his next place for avatāra ideas to save us people, as kśīrabhdi nātan in the milky ocean and how gōpikās were fond of his divine feet (vyūham); and now they are looking at the lotus feet of trivikraman (vibhavam).

ōngi – as He was in sayanam (lying down posture) in the milky ocean, on ādi sēśan, thinking about His next avatāram for saving people, when dēvas prayed to Him about mahābali’s troubles, He got up and grew as trivikraman; ‘*uvanda uḷḷattanāi ulagam aḷandu* \[amalanādipirān – 3\]’ because of happiness to do the work for other people’s benefit;  It is not ‘*vaḷarndu ulagaḷandu*‘ but ‘*ōngi ulagaḷandu*‘ – it is not like He has to grow now – *ōngi* implies He with affection and kindness is getting up so fast, to help the dēvas;

When a *samsāri*, one with *ahankāram*, *svātantriyam*, etc., one day wonders who will be the savior for him,  [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) takes that as a call for help and immediately rushes to his or her heart to help further to reach near His lotus feet for doing kainkaryam.

When He measured all the worlds (with His 3 steps’ land), His divine foot touched all the people’s heads as He was fond of giving blessings to one and all. Even when some of them were not understanding His love for them, He was still fond of touching everyone like how a mother would hug her child sleeping next to her without thinking whether the child will be aware of her hug or not.

Since He is similar in beauty and behavior to [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), they are enjoying trivikrama avatāram also.

**uttaman**

He lives thinking that helping these dēvas is His pleasure/destiny; An *adaman* is one who troubles others to gain some benefits; *madyaman* thinks others should live as well as he should live; but *uttaman* would even reduce himself for the benefit of others. Such a great [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) reduced Himself (not only in size, as vāmanan), in order to help the dēvas.

**pēr pādi**

We do nāma sankīrtanam of such an uttaman. (saying his names (nāmam)).

The difference between His nāmam and Himself is like the difference between Him and others. Such is the wonder of his nāmam. If He is a gold bar, His nāmam is like gold jewels. Even if those who are not His bhaktās say His name it would benefit them; there is no qualification needed to say His name – saying His name would be one’s all the qualifications.

**utaman pēr** – refers to tirumantram; in 1st pāsuram it says ‘nārāyaṇan’, in the 2nd, ‘paraman’ that is His *guṇā* as implied by the mantram, In this pāsuram, they refer to trivikraman which refers to omnipresence (*vyāpti*) which the tirumantram reveals, and is represented by vāmana.

[kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in gītā talked about 4 ways to reach him in srī vaikuntam – karma yōgam (actions), jñāna yōgam (wisdom/knowledge), bhakti yōgam (devotion) and śaraṇāgati (surrender). The first three require so much effort on the person’s part, that kriṣṇan suggested the last option that is śaraṇāgati (surrender; samāsrayaṇam) for people like us.

**ōngiyavan** *pēr pādi* – is telling the great people following the karma yōgam to recite the names (pēr pādi) of the [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who got up and grew fast (action).

**ulagaḷandavan** *pēr pādi* – when ones *learns* more about how He covered so many worlds with His divine foot, the *jñānam* will grow – reciting His names in this way is for those great people following the jñāna yōgam

**uttaman** *pēr pādi* – when thinking how He is an *uttaman*, the person would get more *bhakti* – this is for the great people following the bhakti yōgam to recite His names (pēr pādi)

so, what about the śaranāgatās (prapannars, mumukśus) who have done samāśrayaṇam through her/his ācāryan? Which words combine in the pāsuram for them to talk about reciting his names? . . . . It is just ‘*pēr pādi*‘! – saying His nāmams is *svayam puruśārtam* (that itself is the goal), because, for the goal of reaching Him we have already surrendered to His lotus feet with the help of ācāryan (and [rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)).

**nāngaḷ**  (‘we’) **nam pāvaikku** (For Our nōnbu)

‘We’ who won’t be able to bear it if we don’t say your nāmam;

they are happy about them being able to talk about and also meet [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as till yesterday the elders were policing even their hearts;

**pāvaikku** – The nōnbu that we are doing while at present when we are not able to be with the prāpyam (kriṣṇan);

**pāvaikku cāṟṟi nīrādināl**

With nōnbu as the front/reason, if we take bath to quench the feeling due to our separation from [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); with nōnbu as the front/reason, if we meet kriṣṇan and enjoy with Him;

**pēr pādi – nīrādināl** – if we mingle with kriṣṇan with the knowledge of our (ever-present) relationship with Him (*sambanda jñāna pūrvakam*).

**tīngu inṟi nādellām**

(if we take bath – merge with [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) this place would get rid of all its problems, all will live well with their people (as a side-benefit (where not the focus is), of doing kainkaryam (where the focus is)); like how in rāma rājyam people lived well not because of their puṇyas (good karma), but with the bhājñam of [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (rāman),

**ellām**: not only this place, but all the places associated to this place also would live well.

**tīngu inṟi**: feeling of svātantriyam (feeling independent of perumān), giving too much importance to body and material comforts, etc., will all be averted with His blessings.

**tingaḷ  mummāri peidu**

Without drought or flood – three cycles in a month – of nine-days’ sunlight and 1-day’s rain which would benefit the place;

we who are having the three characteristics of 1) ananyārha sēśatvam, 2) ananya śaraṇatvam, and 3) ananyabhōjñatvam**;** whereas it might suffice to have goodness with just one such person, here we are so many of us with these characteristics making all of us very happy.

**ōngu perum sennel ūdu kayalugaḷa**

(because of good rains) the paddy crops would also quickly grow like the ulagaḷanda [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/);

**ūdu kayal ugaḷa** – The fish having grown big like baby elephants would not be able jump around easily as they are restricted in between the well-grown paddy; like how the devotees who see the sarvēśvaran (god of all) the ulagaḷanda [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), would be awe-struck by His beauty and won’t move.

Like how *mārīcā* out of fear saw *rāmā* in every tree after he was thrown far away by *rāmā*, here the gōpikās see *vāmana* when they see tall grass.

The paddy without the grain in its tip would stand straight; as it grows the grain, it would bow down its head; when a *siśyan* first goes to ācāryan, his head would be straight as he does not know what he does not know. As he gains *bhagavat jñānam* from his *ācāryan*, he would conclude that he does not know anything (reality/modesty), and his head would bow down (to his acāryan).

**pūnkuvaḷai pōdil poṟi vaṇḍu kaṇ paduppa**

With the lillies growing beautifully, the bees would happily drink the honey and lie down on the bed in the flowers and sleep elatedly; as the big fish try to go in-between the grass, the flowers would nicely shake along providing a comfortable bed for the bees; as they got into the ‘*pori*‘ (trap) of the flowers during their sleep as the sun comes up, the male and female bees will complain to each other saying ‘you did not wake me up’, ‘no you did not wake me up’;

perumān loves to stay and relax in the hearts of his sēśans.

siśyan feels safe under ācāryan’s blessings after having done samāśryaṇam.

(after describing about the richness of the paddy, now it is describing about the well-being of āyppādi)

**tēnagadē pukkirundu**

Like how tiruvadi (hanuman) jumped across the ocean without any hesitation, the people even after seeing that the cows are giving too much of milk, would go and milk the cow without any hesitation (the cows are so much healthy that an ordinary person might hesitate to go near it for fear of not catching all the milk).

**irundu** –  milk is always available with the cows

**tēngādē** – without any hesitation a siśyan would

**pukkirundu** – go inside the ācāryan’s tirumāḷigai (and learn)

**sīrtta mulai paṟṟi**

The cows are so healthy in āyppādi that it is not possible for the people to milk the cows just with their fingers – they have to use both their hands.

**paṟṟi vānga**

Those cows are so healthy, they would give so much milk just with touching and removing of the hands.

**vāngak kudam niraikkum**

Just by touching, the cows would fill the containers so fast, that one has to keep changing those containers to catch all the milk; if they don’t change them fast it won’t be the fault of the cows – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is also pouring his love, it is for the people to catch it as quickly and eagerly as they can.

ācāryan, like cow’s udder, gives us the jñānam of the lotus feet of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**vaḷḷal**  (kind / generous)

– because of the above (giving milk)

– these cows are like [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); how the gōpikās would tie a letter around His neck and ask Him to go as messenger, the cows also are standing amicable for the boys to tie it, direct it, etc.

– ācāryan is kind, who connects the siśyan with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)

**perum pasukkaḷ**

They are the great cows – they live not based on just the grass and water, but based on kind and friendly touch of [kriṣṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). They have listened to so much of kriṣṇan’s flute while casually chewing and digesting their food; this is how S*atrunjayan* the elephant of [cakravarti tirumagan (rāman)](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) also grew up.

**nīngāda selvam niṟaindu**

(if we did the nōnbu), this place will get all the wealth, unlimited wealth, the wealth that will always stay with the people; because it accumulated with the blessings of [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/). (selvam: kainkarya selvam).

Unlike the limits placed by [sarvēsvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) on our good and bad based on our puṇya and pāpā, here we would get ever-lasting goodness through these gōpikās, similar to [sīta pirāttiyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) saying ‘*pāpānām vā subhānām vā*‘ (will be kind whether person has done good or bad).

May we get the ever-lasting wealth that is ācāryan’s tiruvadi.

**ēl ōr em pāvāi** – think about this and follow our path.

In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) says that as they do nōnbu, the whole place will become rich with paddy crops, and other good things would happen for the people. She invokes trivikraman as having similar characteristics of kriṣṇan, and emphasizes reciting his Names as the sole purpose by itself.

In this pāsuram āṇḍāḷ talks about vibhava avatāram (trivikraman).

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 4. Ali malai

*āḻi maḻaik kaṇṇā! onṟu(m) nī kai karavēl āḻiyuḷ pukku mugandu kōdu ārttu ēṟi ūḻi mudalvan uruvam pōl mei karuttu pāḻiyam toḷudaiya paṟpanāban kaiyil āḻi pōl minni valampuri pōl ninṟu adirndu tāḻādē sārngam udaitta sara maḻai pōl vāḻa ulaginil peididāi nāngaḷum mārgaḻi nīrāda maghiḻndēlor empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

maḻaik kaṇṇā – O varuṇa dēvā the head of rain gods; āḻi – who is majestic like the ocean; kai karavēl – without holding back; onṟu(m) – anything; nī – you; āḻiyuḷ pukku – enter the ocean; mugandu kodu – drink entire water; ēri – rise in the sky; ārtu – with thunders; mei karutu – become dark; uruvam pōl – like the divine body of; ūḻi mudalvan – emperumān who is the root cause of time and everything; āḻi pōl minni – do lightning like the discus (in the right hand); ninṟu adirndu – stay and be thunderous; valam-puri pōl – like the conch (in the left hand); pāḻi-am-tōḷ-udai – of the one with great and beautiful shoulders; paṟpanābhan kaiyil – of the one with beautiful navel; tāḻadē – without any delay; sārngam udaita sara maḻai pōl – like how the arrows poured from the bow (of srī rāma); peididāi – pour the rain; vāḻa – for all to live; nāngaḷum – so that we too (we who are doing nōnbu); mārgaḻi nīrāda – pleasantly bathe as part of mārgaḻi; magiḻndu – with happiness

**Перевод:**

**āḻi maḻaik kaṇṇā**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The dēvatai of rain (parjanyan) comes and asks the gōpikās “How do I do my part in this togetherness so that my life would be meaningful and fit my nature of being subservient to emperumān; please give me orders to do anything you desire”. The gōpikās reply, ‘People should say ‘these girls did nōnbu, and all the rains showered’ ‘; and they give him their list of how he should rain for the well-being of the people. This is also related to people who have done samāsryaṇam (surrender) through their ācāryan. When someone asked āḻvān (kūrattāḻvān), ‘what will you do when you see dēvatāntarams (other devatās)’? āḻvān replied, “Your question is wrong and against the words of vēdās. Ask instead what the devatās should do when they see us who are saraṇāgatās (done samāsrayaṇam).”*

emperumān gave the work of creating and destroying to brahma rudras, and kept the saving work to Himself; O! the parjanyan, you are also similar to Him in helping the country as He has given this good work to you of pouring the rains after considering your goodness.

O! the parjanyan, to whom [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) has given the job of pouring rains on this world, and who has got a lot of rain in you, please, without thinking that we have done pāpams, pour the rain on us so that the elders are happy about the result of our nōnbu. Be of big heart and be kind and take care of these ordinary people’s requests – that is what kriṣṇan did by walking all the way to kauravās for negotiating a land on behalf of pāṇḍavas.

(It is such a low-level task for kriṣṇan, but He did that out of kindness towards these people; not only that, He wants others to know about it too! – where did kriṣṇan let others know about it? – go to [tiruvallikkēṇi](http://thiruvallikkeni.koyil.org/) and he is proudly standing there saying that I went as a messenger for the *pāṇḍavas*, I drove the chariot for them, and I got a lot of wounds in my face when helping them in *kurukśētra*).

**onṟu nī kai karavēl**

They are asking the *dēvatai* of rain to pour without holding anything back, without a changing generosity; nee – you who will become white when you don’t rain. Unlike emperumān gōśti that do good based on good deeds, pour without any partiality like how we accept everyone even considering their bad deeds as a good reason for mercy, as part of the group of [pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

Like [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) said to emperuman that His eyes cannot match pirātti’s always-kind eyes, here the varuṇa dēvatai is not comparable to emperumān’s ever generous nature.

**āḻi uḷ pukku mugandu  kodu**

Enter the middle of salty ocean all the way to the bottom and drink all the water and take them and pour them as rain – we want it for the well-being of our āyppādi. (and not into the small holes dug by the sons of king sakara who sent his sons in search of the horse used in asvamēda yāgā).

**ārttu –**

When her son gets angry with her and goes to a coultry at the street-end and doesn’t come home in the night, the mother would send food for him without letting him know about it, and later when he returns she would not show any expression about having given that food – [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is also like this – He protects us but doesn’t show it in His face.  But we want  you (parjanyan) to show it all – with all eagerness and happiness, and with a lot of thunder and lightning – like how tiruvadi (hanuman) came back with such an enthusiasm after locating and meeting sītā pirātti, and like how the other *vānaras* were showing the enthusiasm upon seeing how tiruvadi was returning.

**ēṟi** – We and this whole world should see you coming with thunder and lightning like a bow; should cover the whole sky with your dark clouds; like it is said in ‘*viṇṇīla mēlāppu virindārpōl*‘ (nāciār tirumoḻi 8-1) and ‘*mada yānai pōl eḻunda māmugil-kāḷ’* (nāciār tirumoḻi 8-9).

**āḻi uḷ pukku mugandukodu ārttu ēri** – people who have surrendered to Him get deep in to kainkaryam and get to ācāryan’s tiruvadi.

**ārtu** – fully into;  **ēṟi** – ācāryan’s position; **mugandu kodu** – acāryan due to his abhimānam/kindness towards even those who have done lots of pāpams, by making emperumān be kind to them.

**ūḻi mudalvan**

Like how [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) at the time before creation of beings (that time is called ūḻi; mudalvan: [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), sees all of us who have not yet gained a body or life, and feels sorry for us and with so much kindness starts his creation process with just His sankalpam (with a thought in His mind), and then He would get bright with happiness – You, the rain dēvatai, also should possess similar kindness towards us. “*jñālam padaitta em mugil vaṇṇanē*” (O the one who created the worlds, whose color is like dark clouds) is what [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) also said.

**uruvam pōl mei karuttu**

When [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) decides to create the worlds due to His kindness filling His heart like how a grown plant would be, the rain devatai, you also come with your body getting dark (clouds).

*‘mei’ ‘karuttu’* – they are saying only the body would get dark for the rain dēvatai, because unlike [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s kindness which towards us comes from His inside, the clouds will become white after giving rains; [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is always having kindness towards us.

**pāḻi-am-tōḷ**

*pāḻi* – lot of space available (in the shoulder); [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s shoulder is so big that when people wanting to reach Him who go under the protection of His big shoulders, and will look small compared to the shadow of His shoulders; that is, His longing for us is more compared to all of our longing for Him;

*am-tōḷ* – His shoulder’s protection is so sweet like the milk that is boiled off of its water (சுண்டக் காய்ச்சின பால்). He is to be protected by us, and is also our enjoyment.

**paṟpa nābhan**

[emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with the beautiful nābhi (navel; belly button); navel like a lotus flower; from where He created brahmā (vyaśti sruśti) and is waiting, with His large and kind shoulder, for us to be born and go to Him;

**paṟpanābhan kaiyil āḻi pōl minni**

If a king gets a child, because of his status he may not fully show all his happiness outside; but all his people would enjoy the birth celebrating by throwing colors at each other (like *hōli*).

Similarly, although [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is not showing his happiness outside after creating brahmā, since now brahmā would create all us (his children), His happiness is reflected outside by the *āḻi* (cakram / discus) in his hand which is becoming bright and dazzling. This is the reason for mentioning together in this pāsuram about ‘padmanābhan’ (who made brahma to create us) and His dazzling āḻi (which is reflecting [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s happiness for getting His children).

**valampuri pōl ninṟadirndu**

Like how in kurukśētra the conch sounded by kriṣṇan made the kauravas dead scared and pāndavas very happy, (what appears as deadly weapon for the enemies looks like an ornament for us); O the rain dēvatai make thunderous noise to raise and pour the rain on us.

**valampuri** – the conch is very close to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gets to put it in his wonderful lips. It has done bhājñam. ==\> [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) implies that they should get so much rain, not like how much the people who pray other dēvatais would get but like those who get the sambandam of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (like the conch got the relationship with [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), that is us who have done samāśrayaṇam.

**‘ninṟru’  adirundu** – unlike in kurukśētram where the conch was sounded only once, we want you (the rain dēvatai) to stay put and pour a lot of rain.

The kindness of emperumān as sounded by the praṇavam which announces about śēśa-śēśi relationship, **vāḻa** – for lovkīkas (samsāris) to be corrected with *svarūpa jñānam* (of śēśa-śēśi bhāvam); **ulaginil** – where samsāris live; **peididāi** – pour the nectar that is of bhagavat guṇa anubhavam.

**tāḻādē**

We should not get less (rain) like what they will get who go to other dēvatais; we should get like how those who have done bhagavat samāsryaṇam where perumān would rush to help us.

**sārngam udaitta**

*sārngam*: the bow of cakravarty tirumagan (srī rāman). Like a snake which did not get any prey, the bow would be idle with [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (srī rāman), but once [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gives it the permission it would automatically pour arrows on the enemies; once it leaves the bow even [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) cannot stop the arrow;

**sara-maḻai pōl –** like how [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s bow rains (the arrows) ..

**vāḻa ulaginil peididāy**

Pour your rain, O the rain dēvatai.

With those arrows of [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) the rākśasas were not saved; but you pour the rain for the whole world / everyone to live well.

**nāngaḷum mārgaḻi nīrāda, magiḻndu**

Then varuna dēvatai (rain) asks “அழகிது! (nice!), I wanted to do something for you, but you are asking me to rain which I have been already doing, as [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) assigned me that job. So what is new?”

For this the gōpikās answer, “Well, we and kriṣṇan will bathe, you see that and become happy; that will also get you to see kriṣṇan.”

After seeing your big clouds and the sound of thunder, we are taking bath to reduce our angst due to separation from kriṣṇan.  For the elders we have to bathe just as part of nōnbu only; it is not necessary to do it happily; for you, we will happily bathe and be with kriṣṇan. This is how you are helping us.

**ēlōrēm-pāvai** – commit to your mind and follow us; colloquial phrase with no specific meaning.

In this pāsuram, similar to how gōpikās ask varuṇan to do the task which he already does as part of his work/kainkaryam, prappanas also do the nitya/naimitika tasks (like sandya vandanam, srārtam) as part of following what is *required* of them.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 5. Mayanai

*māyanai mannu vada madurai maindanai tūya peru nīr yamunai tuṟaivanai āyar kulattinil tōnrum aṇi viḷakkai tāyaik kudal viḷakkam seida dāmōdaranai tūyōmāi vandu nām tū malar tūvit toḻudu vāyināl pādi manattināl sindikka pōya piḻaiyum pugu taruvān ninṟanavum tīyinil tūsāgum seppēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

māyanai – one who performs wonderful deeds; mannu vada-madurai maindanai – king of vadamadurai that is having ever present bhagavat sambandam; tūya peru nīr – one having divine and ever deep water; yamunai turaivanai – who plays in the bank of river yamunā; āyar kulattinil tōnṟum – who was born in the covherd group; aṇi viḷakkai – who is like a divine deepam (lamp); tāyaik kudal viḷakkam seida – who made his mother yasodā’s womb meaningful; dāmōdaranai – emperumān who let himself be tied by a small rope; nām – we who are open to Him approaching us; tūyōmāi vandu – \[to such emperumān\] (we) came with purity; tū malar tūvi – submit good flowers; toḻudu – and prostrate; vāyināl pādi – sing his names to our mouth’s content; manattināl sindikka – meditate in our mind; pōya piḻaiyum – and the pāpams that we committed (before getting bhagavat sambandam); pugu-taruvān ninṟanavum – (after such sambandam, the) pāpams that we commit without realizing; tīyinil tūsāgum – will all burn away like the cotton placed in fire; seppu – so, sing His names

**Перевод:**

**māyanai**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*This pāsuram has the inner meaning related to samāsrayaṇam.*

Continuing with previous pāsurams in which they sang about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in different lōkams/avatārams/[divya dēsams](https://koyil.org/), here they are singing about the māyan that is referring to srīvaikuṇṭanātan in paramapadam. This is paratvam (his Supremacy, one of his kalyāṇa guṇas (auspcious qualities)).

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who happily lives in srīvaikuṇṭam, with so much of aiśvaryam that the dēvās find it hard to see Him directly, it is hard to explain or think about, He is such a māyan; He has now come, and stayed here in dēvaki’s womb for 12 months, and as a boy He is playing with all gōpikās and cows – that is the paramapadanātan’s simplicity (*sovlabhyam*).

He was born here as kriṣṇan with his cangu and cakram.

(To avoid being scared of His paratvam, one thinks of His sovlabhyam; in order to avoid belittling His simplicity, one contemplates His paratvam).

**mannu vada-madurai**

*mannu* – maturāpurī, has *always* had [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s sambhandam. srī vāmana avatāram did a long penance in this place of siddāśramam; catrugnāḻvān took care ruling this place after winning lavaṇāsura and other asurās, and so [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is fond of this place; (now kriṣṇan – for butter, ghee, milk, and, the gopikās).

*vada-madurai*: like paramapadam this place is also famous because of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s association; since He came here out of love, they celebrate this place; this place is a means (prāpakam) to see Him; this place itself can be the goal/prāpyam (because of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)’s presence).

**maindanai**  – (say the name of the) ‘son’ (kriṣṇan) who is having majesty/strength.

(talk/sing the glory of) kriṣṇa (avatāram) who had the strength by which He removed the chains on His parents’ legs; He is the king; showed His beauty and pulled everyone to His side; smart. Becoming younger every day because of happily helping the gōpars.

**tūya peru nīr yamunai tuṟaivanai**

This great yamunai river can even let us forget about the virajā river (which is the river one crosses near srī vaikuntam in order to go into srī vaikuntam); when vasudēvar wanted to cross the yamunai river to go to tiru-āyppādi, she after touching kriṣṇan’s lotus feet gave way for vaśudēvar;

With kriṣṇan and gōpikās doing *rāsakrīdai* (play in the water) in the yamunai river, playing near it, gurgling and spitting in it, this river has become clean/pristine as well. (tūya).

(This is unlike river gōdāvari which due to fear of rāvaṇā, did not reveal that sītā pirātti was taken by rāvaṇā, even though tāyār had requested the river to inform rāma).

***yamunait tuṟaivan*** – rāsakrīdai happened in this place; this is where gōpikās get into trouble by kriṣṇan; His identity is associated with the banks of yamunā.

***tuṟaivan*** – the lead (kriṣṇan) of that land; leader of the āyppādi girls;

==\> ācāryan [srī parāsara bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) said: They are referring to kriṣṇan indirectly by saying *yamunait tuṟaivanai* because the tiru-āyppādi gōpikās are afraid that if they said the name kriṣṇan then kamsan might hear it and cause danger to Him.

**āyar kulattinil tōnṟum (m)aṇi viḷakkai**

Having mentioned ‘māyan’ above in this pāsuram to imply [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in paramapatam, now [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is implying that kriṣṇan in their covherd crowd is better in displaying His guṇams – like the brightness of a lamp in a dark room; this also matches with ‘*vada madurai maindanai*‘ of this pāsuram;

(*tōnṟum*) He does not have the limit of being born out of a womb.

(*tōnṟum*) – he was born in maturāpuri but He came here to tiruvāyppādi (so *āyar kulattinil tōnṟum*).

*(m)aṇi viḷakkai* – he is a mangaḷa dīpam (auspicious lamp) in tiurvāippādi.

āyar kulam – all the bhagavata group who consider only Him as their means (to attain Him).

**tāyaik kudal viḷakkam seyda dāmōdaranai**

People appreciate his mother yasōdai for giving birth from her womb to such a wonderful child; the child got that good name for his mother;

Women would have lines in their stomach after giving birth – here yasōdai is having such lines which is a great mark for having given birth to kriṣṇan.

dāmōdaranai: tied by the தாம்புக் கயிறு –  by letting her mother tie him with the rope, he got the people to appreciate yasōdai for giving birth to such a wonderful obedient child.

Like how we cannot become independent of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) too cannot untie our relationship with Him. If we follow this relationship with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), then we will be untied from samsāram.

dāmōdaranai: because of tying the rope in his hip, the marks are always present on kriṣṇan. When he jumps from one branch to another while playing, his dress moves aside and the gōpikās look at those marks and make fun of him; that is why [namperumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (utsavar, srīrangam, who is kaṇṇan himself) is always wearing dōti around his hip (to hide the lines/marks) – said ācāryan [nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/).

If we think of this simplicity of him, then the ties of our samsāram would be removed.

The marks that sēśans (who have done [bhaghavat samāsrayaṇam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html)) have their marks on their shoulders (*கோயிற் பொறியாலே ஒற்றுண்டு*); the sēśi ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to whom we are sēśans) has got his marks from the rope as he allows himself to be tied by the sēśans’ bhakti;  he shows his marks and gets us to have and show our marks (in our shoulders) – said [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/).

**tūyōmāi vandu nām**

The gōpikai with the vēda jñānam says this: Do not see whether we are eligible/good enough to go and do the nōnbu, to be with kriṣṇan, to do samāsrayaṇam through ācāryan; Willing to come as you are is good enough, no need to search for any purity than that, because bhagavat sambandam (association with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) does not concern your purity.

āyppādi people don’t know how to fully bathe and keep themselves clean; they are purer with kriṣṇan’s friendship.

vibīśaṇāḻvān did not bathe in the sea before going to see cakravarty tirumagan to do saraṇāgati; arjunan in kurukśētra did not bathe before getting carama slōkam (sarva darmān…) from kriṣṇan in the middle of the battleground; draupaty did not bathe (she was in her monthly periods) when doing saraṇāgati to kriṣṇan; For doing saraṇāgati these are not important, nor one has to earn any qualification – only the acceptance that we are ever dependent on [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), that we are his sēśans is important.

(vandu nām) – the norm is for Him (kriṣṇan) to come to us and save us from this samsāram, but he will see us coming to him.

[perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) told guhan that He himself should have come to meet him instead of guhan coming to Him; he told that he feels ashamed for letting the dandakāraṇya riśis (in the forest) come see Him instead of him readily going t them and taking care of their problems;

so why should the gōpikās go to him instead of waiting for him to come?  When being confident about upāyam (the means – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); he is the means, so he should try to come and save us) it makes one think to stay put and wait for him;  the desperation about reaching upēyam (goal/destiny – that is also [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) makes us to go to Him if he has not yet showed up.  
**tū(ya) malar – (**tūya: pure/noble**;**  malar: flower**) –** The flowers given for kainkaryam without expecting anything in return (ananya-prayōjanam); not bought from the store in a plantain leaf, but by getting the flowers and putting some small effort to make it;

Like mālākārar’s flowers that he gave to kriṣṇan in vada-madurai.

Like [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) said ‘*sūttu nan mālaigaḷ* ***tūyana*** *ēndi*’, this is also given without expecting anything in return.

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) also does not see what we give, but sees the affection in our heart for him.

patram puśpam…   He does not see the size of our kainkaryam because there is nothing that is too big in this world for him, and mainly because no kainkaryam is small for him. He only sees our affection/bhakti/care.

**tūvi** – sprinkle/throw the flower in his lotus feet (without worrying about the procedure?).  \[**kāyika kainkaryam** (one of the 3 types of kainkaryams)\]. Can they throw the flowers like that instead of properly submitting them to Him? Their love for Him makes them not consider these formalities;

like how [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) also said that we do anjali just once with our hands without even properly putting them together (கோணல் மாணலாக ஒரு முறை பண்ணிய அஞ்சலி), and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) accepts even that as an anjali and his heart melts for us.

**toḻudu –** do anjali (joining both the hands, kai kooppi) – offering ourselves – such that He would melt by seeing our affection. The reason for / use of, being born with our arms and hands (is to do anjali).

draupadi’s anjali made kriṣṇan feel so much indebted that he was never satisfied even after helping her so much;

Because he is such lover of us heroines, he would want to do anjali to us; or if He does ānjali and we the gōpikās don’t reciprocate, then the gōpars would fear that this nōnbu is not happening appropriately; so for their sake the gōpikās do the anjali.

**vāyināl pādi** – The use of being born with our mouth and voice is to say his tiru nāmams ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s names). \[**vākku kainkaryam**\].

In addition to going to you, we also sang your names;

Everything starts with thinking in the manas, but we praise you with all three apparatus, hands, mouth, and manas; our hands and mouth are going to you ahead of manas – such is our eagerness.

**manattināl sindikka** – The use of having our manas (mind/thoughts). \[**mānasa kainkaryam**\]. It is important to use the mind when singing His names.

So it is talking about using all the three (hands, mouth, and mind) equipment and falling on him.

**pōya piḻaium(,) pugu taruvān ninranavum**

The pāpams of the past (before [samāsryaṇam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html)), and the pāpams of the future (from the time of [samāsrayanam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html)) …..

**tīyinil toosu āgum – (** tūsu: cotton (ball) )

….. (pāpams) will vanish like how the cotton (ball) will burn in the fire and vanish.

If we regularly drink milk containing all the required medicinal items we will get rid of ailments. In the same way, as we keep doing bhagavat anubhavam, all our pāpams would vanish.

**seppu**: say.  (connects to :  tāyai kudal viḷakkam seida dāmōdaranai **seppu**). Say the name of dāmōdaran. Relates to kāyikam, vāk, and manas kainkaryams, and his sovlabhyam (simplicity).

English translation ṟaghuram srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 6. Pullum silambina kan

*puḷḷum silambina-kāṇ puḷḷaraiyan koyilil veḷḷai viḷi-sangin pēr-aravam kēttilaiyō piḷḷāi eḻundirāi pēi mulai nanjuṇḍu kaḷḷa cakatam kalakkaḻiya kālōcci veḷḷattaravil tuyil amarnda vittinai uḷḷattu kōṇḍu munivargaḷum yōgigaḷum meḷḷa eḻundu ari enṟa pēr aravam uḷḷam pugundu kuḷirndēlō rempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**
