puḷḷum silambina-kāṇ – birds are calling while going (after waking up), and; kēttilaiyō – are you not hearing; pēr-aravam – the big sound; veḷḷai viḷi-sangin – of white conch which is doing tirupaḷḷi-eḻucci (waking up emperumān); puḷḷaraiyan koyilil – in the temple of perumān who is garudan‘s leader, \[garudan who is the head of birds\]; piḷḷāi – you young gōpikā who is new to bhagavat viśayam; eḻundirāi – get rid of sleep and get up; munivargaḷum – adiyārs who meditate him; yōgigaḷum – and adiyārs who do yogābhyāsam; meḷḷa eḻundu – (they) get up slowly without disturbing the emperumān in their hearts; uḷḷattu koṇḍu – they have placed in their hearts through thoughts about; pēi mulai nanju-uṇḍu – perumān who drank phūtanai’s poisonous milk from her nipples, (and killed her); kaḷḷac cakatam – (who made the) the cunning cart; kalakku aḻiya – go out of shape and get destroyed; kāl ōcci – by kicking with tiruvadi (and finish it); tuyil amarnda – (who is) gracefully lying down; veḷḷattu – in the liquid of milky ocean; aravil – on tiruvanantāḻvān (ādi śēśan); vittinai – one who is root of the world; ari enṟa pēr aravam – (munis and yōgis are thinking about such emperumān and) making big sound, chanting ‘hari’; uḷḷam pugundu kuḷirndu – (please get up so that) we get that sound into our minds and become happy

**Перевод:**

**The first gōpikā that āṇḍāḷ is waking up in this pāsuram is new to bhagavat viśayam (matter related to perumān kainkaryam) and does not know any details about doing nōnbu.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*==\> The inner meaning in these 10 pāsurams – she is referring to other āḻvārs as those 10 gōpikās whom she is calling to go and get kriṣṇan’s tiruvadi, and to do kainkaryam to Him.*

In the first 4 pāsurams she talked about para, vyūha, vibhava, and antaryamitvams. 5th was out of pattern; and in this 6th pāsuram they sing about arcāvataram (in statue form) as in ‘puḷ araiyan kōil’

**puḷḷum silambina-kāṇ**

The gōpikās who have woken up early go to the first gōpikā’s house to wake her up. They tell her to wake up.

The girl from inside asks, “ṣouldn’t the day first dawn for waking up?”.

They reply, “The day has dawned, so please get up”.

The girl asks, “What is the sign that the day has already dawned?”.

“We are here, isn’t that enough to tell that it is dawn?”.

“If someone has slept the previous night then you can say that their getting up is the sign of dawn. You gōpikas did not sleep at all, so I cannot take it as a sign”.

Then they reply with the first words of this pāsuram, “puḷḷum silambina kāṇ” – the birds have woken up and they are going for gathering food (you come and see that)”.

She replies, “Is that a sign that the day has dawned?”,

“Is it not?”

“No” (ஓம், அல்லவீ)

“How come?”

“You did not let those birds sleep, so they are also going without sleep. In the place where you are born, even the birds are thinking about kriṣṇan and they don’t sleep. So, that is also not a sign of dawn. Tell me some other sign”. She keeps lying down on her bed.

In this context, *piḷḷai piḷḷaiāḻvān* would say that for this ātmā there is no good time other than when we hear the bird that marks us the amicable time to do bhagavat samāsrayaṇam. Here the sound of bird implies ācāryan’s word, and his waking up those sleeping in ignorance.

Inner meaning for puḷ (bird) – the bhāgavata bhaktas who have woken up early in the morning, wanting to get darśan of blackness of kriṣṇan.

In the place where āṇḍāḷ was born, would there be sleep for the birds, said *nālūrācān piḷḷai* – based on āṇḍāḷ’s ‘*kālai eḻundirundu kuruvik kaṇangaḷ mālin varavu solli maruḷ pādudal meimmai kolō* \[nāciyar tirumoḻi 9-8\]’.

**puḷ-araiyan kōyilil**

The king of birds periya tiruvadi (garudāḻvān) is there, his “kō” (king) sarvēśvaran is there, ‘il’ His house (that is, kō’s house, that is, temple), koyilil – in the temple of garudan’s king (that is, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)).

(When kriṣṇan is here, and when everyone is immersed in His beauty and behave as His bhaktas, can there be a perumāḷ temple in tiruvāyyppādi? yes, similar to how cakravarty tiurmagan (rāman), along with his dharma patni (as wife who helps rāmar conduct the darmas of living) and family had done pūjās to periya perumāḷ (srīranganātan) when [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was in ayōdḍhi; similarly, for kriṣṇan also there is a perumāḷ kōyil here.)

puḷ-araiyan – His identity is based on those people who depend on Him;  *lakśmaṇa pūrvajam ca* – sīta pirātti also said that Her being was in danger by yama in the form of a deer as soon as She sent away lakśmaṇan and *his elder brother* cakravarty tirumagan. She refers to srī rāman as related to lakśmaṇan.

They are referring to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) through the name of periya tiruvadi (garudan) – one generally goes to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) through the support of tiruvadigaḷ (ānjanēyar, garudan), ācārayan, [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/), and tāyār.

**veḷḷai viḷi sangin pēr aravam**

The gōpikās are saying that the sangu (conch) is white. The gōpikā inside says, ‘Conch is always white, it is not a good argument for saying that the day has dawned’.

She continues, ‘Every sāmam (every certain number of hours) they sound the conch in the temple, so the sound of conch is not a sign of dawn.’

The gōpikās reply, ‘pēr aravam’ (bigger sound) – the conch is being sounded with higher volume, which they would do only at dawn.

viḷi – calling. Calling at the auspicious time of *brāhma muhuūrtam* to do kainkaryam to perumāḷ.

Inner meaning: the sound of praṇavam coming from the temple, calling (viḷi) the ‘உ’ (devotees) of the praṇavam to come there.

pēr aravam – loud enough for you to get up;  when talking about lakśmaṇan going to mahārājar’s (sukrīvan) place and making loud sound with his bow (because mahārājar was delaying in joining rāma lakśmaṇā for finding sīta),  [anantāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/ananthazhwan/) said ‘mahārājar who was holding on to the breast of tārai, his blindness (to the need of rāmā) was lost when lakśmaṇan made the sound.

**kēttilaiyō –** are you not hearing that sound (of conch). Are you going to lose the bhājñam of hearing that sound that is calling you to go to kriṣṇan?

The gōpikā inside is not convinced.

**piḷḷāi eḻundirāi:**  piḷḷāi – ignorant

The gōpikās ask, “O girl, you who are new in bhagavat viśayam (related to perumāḷ), who are new to the sweetness of doing kainkaryam to perumāḷ, without continued experience in bhagavat viśayam you have not reached the level to understand the beauty of being with other bhāgavatās (devotees); should you not be interested in seeing us like how we are interested in seeing you?”

==\> The inner meaning of the 10 gōpikās woken up in this and the next total 10 pāsurams – the gōpikās being woken up refer to [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/). In this pāsuram, ‘*piḷḷāi*‘ refers to ‘[poigai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/)‘.  In his prabhandam, *mudal tiruvantādi*, [poigai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) says ‘*onṟum adanai uṇarēn nān*‘ as if like the girl who is new to *bhagavat viśayam*; in various pāsurams of *mudal tiruvantādi* he uses words similar to ‘*pēi mulai nanju uṇḍu*‘, ‘*kaḷḷa cakatam kāl ōcci*‘, ‘*veḷḷattaravil tuyil amarda vittu*‘, to enjoy kriṣṇan’s *amānuśa seśtitangaḷ* (actions beyond human). [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is using similar words in this pāsuram, and referring to a similar gōpikā.

==\> Another way this is seen is as follows: This pāsuram is about periyāḻvār her beloved father and ācāryan, and then the seven pāsurams including this go in reverse order of āḻvārs from periyāḻvār to poigai āḻvār, them being elders; then remaining three of ten pāsurams are about future āḻvārs – toṇḍaraippodi āḻvar, tiruppāṇāḻvār, and seerk kaliyan.

So, as this pāsuram talks about garudan, periyāḻvār is amsam (characteristic) of garudan. So this pāsuram indicates periyāḻvār.

[piḷḷāi](http://pillai.koyil.org/) – new in these matters;  [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) said ‘cintayanti was new to krishna gānam, otherwise she would have been used to the in-laws’ trying to stop her and she would have gone ahead out of the house anyway’.

The gōpikā inside asks back, “Who woke you up, you who know all the sweetness of kainkaryam?”

They reply, “Nobody woke us up; we woke up hearing others doing their own chores” as follows:

**pēi mulai nanju uṇḍu**

When He was alone when even His mother could not help, that phūtanai came near kriṣṇan to kill Him, but she got killed.

Inner meaning: ahankāram, mamakāram are related to svātantriyam – that is, our (wrong) feeling that we are independent of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); because of our ātmā getting into our body (*pēi uṇḍu*) we get the ahankāram and mamakāram (*mulai uṇḍu*) which are poisonous (*nanju*) for our ātmā, and we pray [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to get rid of these things from us.

**kaḷḷa cakatam**

If it is someone like phūtanai, then it may be possible to identify as a devil and protect oneself; but His mother had kept a toy as protection, into which the asuran got into for killing kriṣṇan. (so ‘*kaḷḷa*‘ *cakatam*).

**kalakkaḻiya kāl ōcci**

He kicked His legs because of delay in getting milk from His mother, and His lotus feet hit the toy and killed the asuran who went inside that toy;

==\> The girls are talking about *phūtanai* and the *cakatāsuran* so that the girl who is sleeping will get a jolt thinking about the dangers that came to her kriṣṇan, and so would wake up.

Inner meaning: pray [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to remove from us the *kāmam* (our interest in non-living things (that is materials, body, fame, gadgets,..) and *krōdam* (the anger when we don’t get those things).

**veḷḷatu aravil tuyil amarnda vittinai**

They are happy that without all such dangers (from bhūtanai and others), [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is safely lying on tiru anantāḻvān (ādi śēśan) in tiru pārkadal (milky ocean).

veḷḷam – to indicate that perumāḷ with his gentle tirumēni is comfortably sleeping on ādi śēśan

aravil – on the snake – tiru anantāḻvān (ādi śēśan); soft; his breath killed the madukaidavas, so there is no need to worry about any danger for [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/);

tuyil – sleep – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) lying down on ādi śēśan and deeply thinking about what avatāram to take next for saving us;  one who can come and comfortably live in a bhakta’s heart.

amarnda – as He wants to deeply think about ways of helping and saving us, He is not getting distracted even if tāyārs hug him tightly on their divine chests.

vittinai – vittu – the source/reason for everything;  kśīrābdi nātan ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in the ocean) getting ready for the next avatāram, as if all the avatārams so far are not enough.

**uḷḷattu-k koṇḍu**

Keeping them in their minds and hearts (them = [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) lying down on ādi sēśan in the ocean, and along with the tāyārs), where there is no danger of asurās;

**munivargaḷum yōgigaḷum**

the munis (who do deep thinking about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and yōgis (who do physical kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) \[similar to the gōpikā inside who is lying down thinking about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and the gōpikās outside who are wanting to do kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)\],

**meḷḷa eḻundu –** the munis and yōgs get up very slowly

the munis and yōgis are getting up from their bed very slowly, like how a pregnant woman would get up slowly and carefully, they are getting up without even disturbing the garland in their heads; they get up very carefully so that the [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) inside them (antarātma) would not be disturbed; (are there munis and yōgis in tiruvāippādi? – after kriṣṇan has done the avatāram here, the munis and yōgis come to this place that is full of cows, and labor themselves to enjoy kriṣṇan).

**ari enṟa**

ari – hari – nārāyaṇan.

The munis and yōgis then say hari 7 times as soon as they get up; (when we wake up we could say hari 7 times, and take about 4 steps in the direction towards srīrangam, before carrying on with our morning activities).

Saying ‘ari’ would get rid of all the hindrances in doing perumāḷ kainkaryam. It would get rid of all our fears – what is our fear? that some danger would happen to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). So they pray that He himself should get rid of such dangers, that He saves Himself for us. Once He does that, we are again afraid that someone will see that greatness and He will get *kaṇ eccil* (கண் பட்டு விடும்) and we will start singing *pallāṇḍu* for Him.

Before one gets the *jñānam* of *bhagavat sambandam* one is afraid about dangers to self; after getting the *jñānam* of *bhagavat sambandam* one is afraid of dangers to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**pēr aravam** (big sound (of chanting hari))

Not only the munis and yōgis, but all the 5 lakhs houses are saying ‘hari’.

**uḷḷam pugundu kuḷirndu –**

“As we are feeling so much *viraha tāpam* (suffering due to missing kriṣṇan), the sound of hari nāmam went into our minds and hearts and helped us cool it down, so we are feeling wonderful, so you wake up now.”.

That gōpikā finally gets up and joins other gōpikās to go to the next house to wake up another gōpikā.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 7. Kicu kicu

*kīcu kīcenṟu engum ānaiccāttan kalandu pēsina pēccaravam kēttilaiyō pēippēnnē! kāsum piṟappum kalakalappa kai pērttu vāsa naṟum kuḻal āicciyar mattināl ōsai padutta tayir aravam kēttilaiyō nāyagap pēṇ piḷḷāi nārāyaṇan mūrti kēsavanaip pādavum nī kēttē kidattiyō tēsam udaiyāy tiṟavēlor empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

pēi peṇṇē! – you, the one without proper mind (even though you know the taste of bhagavat viśayam); ānai-ch-cāttan – bharatvāja birds named valiyan (king crows); kīcu kīcu enṟu – (birds) making kīc sounds; engum – in all the directions; kalandu pēsina – and are talking among themselves; kēttilaiyō – did you not hear that; pēccu aravam – sound of their talking?; āycciar – (also) gōpikās; vāsam narum kuḻal – (with) hair in their head with very good fragrance, (and with their); kāsum – garland made with the shapes of kāsu (round coins-like) (அச்சுத்தாலி); piṟappum – and long garland made with the shapes of flower buds (முளைத்தாலி); kalakalappa – these are making sound touching each other; kai pērtu – (as the gōpikās) move their hands; ōsai paduta – and also noise is made; mattināl – when using the churn stick; tayir aravam – and the sound of curd; kēttilaiyō – did you not hear (them)?; nāyaga peṇ piḷḷāi – You who is the head of us gōpikās!; pādavum – as we sing; nārāyaṇan mūrty kēsavanai – about kaṇṇan who is the avatāram of nārāyānan; nī kēttē kidattiyō – are you still lying down?; tēsam udaiyāy – Oh the one with great tējas; tiṟa – open your door

**Перевод:**

**The gōpikās said “The day is dawning, so you wake up”; this gōpikā inside also asked “How do you say it is dawn. I don’t see any signs”. Now they reply,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*==\> This pāsuram refers to pēyāḻvār. (At the end of this text, there is a detail about how this pāsuram refers also to kulasēkara āḻvār (reverse order from periyāḻvār)*

**kīcu kīcu enṟu engum ānai-ch-cāttan**

“The ānai sāttan bird (sparrow) is making the ‘kīcu kīcu’ sound” (which sounds like a baby’s sweet talk (மழலைப் பேச்சு (அநக்ஷர-ரஸமாயிருக்கை))

“Just one bird making noise doesn’t make it a dawn. It may be your house’s bird.”

“They are everywhere” (engum)

“You would have triggered them from their sleep”

“No, not because of us, they got up by themselves and they are making sound by themselves”

“How do you say that”,

**kalandu pēsina pēccu aravam kēttilaiyō**

“They are talking together, that is how we know. There are many birds and they are talking to each other”

“They were together the whole night, why are they talking to each other now”

“They would be separated during the day, they are talking to each other so that they could use that to sustain the day. Their sound makes it clear that they are not feeling too good about the separation; like how someone going away for 6 months into the sea would want to spend more time with his wife, these birds are also talking and being with each other. Have you not heard them? Dwelling in the thoughts of kriṣṇan, you have not heard the birds”

Inner meaning: If a devotee were to go somewhere from the group of devotees, then these people would talk to each other to reduce their angst about that separation, and will think about it until that devotee returns; have you not heard them talking to each other?

**pēip peṇṇē**

She does not get up, saying **“**You would always talk like this”.

They say “Knowing about bhagavat viśayam and bhāgavata viśayam, you are still not getting up? You don’t have the jñānam?”

“Instead of talking about what I lack, talk about the signs of dawning”

==\> āḻvār: In this pāsurm, *pēi pēnnē* refers to ‘[pēi āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/)‘.  With his ‘*tiruk kaṇḍēn* \[mūnṟām tiruvantādi\]’ he talked about seeing the *nāyagan* ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)); his other pāsurams talk about kriṣṇan’s *sēśtitangaḷ* (activites), and about kriṣṇan marrying nappinnai pirātti after fighting with the bulls.

**kāsum piṟappum** (chain/garland made of coins, chain/garland made of those shaped like flower buds)

The coins, etc., from the chains worn in the women’s necks;

Inner meaning: tirumantram, dvayam and caramaslōkam (which we get from our ācāryan during samāśrayaṇam) are heard together, and people are realizing their nature of being properties of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**kalakalappa**

(gōpikās) are making noise together when they are churning curd using the churning rods.

**kai pērttu**

and they are getting tired after churning. Tired because they are so rich with a lot of curd; due to their gentleness, it feels like churning a mountain; tired in their mind may be because kriṣṇan is not around; actually may be because kriṣṇan says that He would help them churn, and He sits opposite a gōpikā, and while the gōpikā tries to churn He holds her hands and does not allow her to churn freely because He does not want her to make curd into buttermilk (he likes curd; *mōrār kudam urutti* \[siriya tirumadal\]), so her hands get tired.

**vāsa naṟum kuḻal āicciyar**

because of exhaustion, their hair locks get undone and that sends sweet fragrance to fill everywhere.

**mattināl ōsai padutta tayir aravam kēttilaiyō**

like how the ocean was churned making huge noise, these gōpikās are churning curd and you are not hearing that great sound? The gōpikās are losing themselves in the beautiful eyes of kriṣṇan and are singing in praise of that, and they are churning this curd with all their efforts; their chains are making the clinging  noise, and all these sounds are becoming greater and greater and hitting the other worlds, and you are saying you don’t hear any sound!

*Inner meaning* for the above two phrases: srīvaiṣṇavās who are ananyārha sēśabūtars (they know that they belong to nārāyaṇan [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) only), who understand the wonderful fragrance of bhagavat viśayam (about perumāḷ), wish well for the samsāris who are the cētanars (beings with jñānam that they belong to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) to recite the [4000 divya prabhandam](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/) – are you not hearing them reciting the [prabandams](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)?

The gōpikā still does not get up, and says, “In this tiruvāyppādi the people’s nature is to churn butter all the time, especially with kriṣṇan doing avatāram here, there is no dearth of cows or milk, so I don’t think this is dawn”.

**nāyagap peṇ piḷḷāi**

First they called her as ‘pēip peṇṇē’, now they are calling her as ‘nāyagap pēn Piḷḷāi’ – both of which imply the same, that she is loved by all these gōpikās;

a rich girl of high status who is talking against all their responses, and not getting up from the bed, even though she is the center-piece of all these gōpikās.

She still does not say anything, so they say:

**nārāyaṇan mūrty kēsavanaip pādavum nī kēttē kidattiyō**

even after our singing praises (*pādavum*) about nārayanan ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who without showing himself is helping us and saving us),

who is born here due to his simplicity (*mūrty*),

who being visible to us here in āyppādi, who is getting rid of enemies (anything in us that prevents us from doing kainkaryam to him) \[*kēsavanai*\],

and listening to (*kēttē*) all the greatness of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/),  you are enjoying our songs (like patting in the lap when enjoying some songs),  by lying down in bed (*kidattiyō*)?

kēttē kidattiyō – You are lying down as if our words are what would sustain your life;

They are using the word ‘*kēsavan*‘ to get her to quickly get up in alarm, as ‘*kēsavan*‘ would remind her of the danger that came to kriṣṇan in the form of the asuran *kēsi*. But she still does not get up, thinking that kriṣṇan has killed that kēsi, so there is no danger for Him.

“We thought that if we sang about kēsavan, you would get up immediately, and how janakan tirumagaḷ (jānaki) went quickly and hugged cakravarty tirumagan (srī rāman)\* we thought you would also quickly go with us to hug kriṣṇan. But you are feeling better because of realizing that there is no fear of kēsi, and sleeping with your hands on your chest (that is, without any worry).

(\* When living in the forest, when srī rāman killed kara-dūśaṇās and 14,000 rakśasas and came back to their place in the forest, sītā pirātti upon seeing śrī rāman back with blood/wounds all over His body, she quickly went and hugged Him with love and affection).

(People who are following karma, jñāna, or bhakti yōgas to reach srī vaikuntam have to be very careful (so, they are worried) to make sure that they are following all the procedures for the yōgas properly. If they are not followed properly, then they would have to take more births to complete it properly and then only could they attain mōkśam. But in the case of śaraṇagatās (those who have done [samāsrayaṇam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html%20) / surrender), they have left all the efforts to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) Himself (through their ācāryan) to take care of getting them mōkśam, so they don’t have any worries whatsoever because they are assured of mōkśam – so they sleep with their hands on their chest (no worries)). \[மார்பிலே கை வைத்து உறங்கப் ப்ராப்தி\]

**tēsam udaiyāy tiṟa**

O pretty and beautiful girl, O the girl who looks so bright due to thinking about kriṣṇan, please open the door for us to see that beauty and get the same jñānam about kriṣṇan, and quickly join us. Open the door and let the flood of your happiness (of tnking about kriṣṇan) hit us.

(That gōpikā finally gets up and joins other gōpikās to go to the next house to wake up another gōpikā.)

How this pāsuram is **also** addressing [kulasēkara āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/):

This [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) was a king, with great devotion to srī rāman – when he listens to the story of śrī  
rāman getting ready for the war against rāvaṇan, he would get up and enter into the sea thinking that he should go and help rāman in the war. In his palace there were a lot of srīvaiṣṇavas taken care of by [kulasēkaran](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/). The ministers were jealous and they stole some gold coins and put the blame on those srīvaiṣṇavas; [kulasēkaran](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) asked to bring a pot with a snake in it and put his hand inside the pot without any fear to prove that the srīvaiṣṇavas would have never done such a thing.

kalandu pēsina pēccu-aravam kēttilaiyō pēipeṇṇē (Hey [kulasēkarā](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/), the ministers are talking among each other scheming to get these srīvaiṣṇavas, do you hear that?)  
kāsum piṟappum kalakalappa kai pērtu (kudap pāmbil kai ittu) \[you had put your hands inside the pot that had the coins making noise and with the snake in it.

aravam kettilaiyō – aravam – snake

nāyagap peṇ piḷḷāi – the leader or the central person – [kulasēkara āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) is in the middle of the list of āḻvārs.

kēsavanaip pādavum ([kulasēkarāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/)‘s first pāsuram was about kēsi as well)

pādavum nī kēttē kidattiyō ([kulasēkarāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) wanted to go join the fight to help rāman). You would not sit idle after hearing about rāman.)

pēippeṇṇē – āḻvar has sung ‘*pēyanē enakku yāvarum yānumōr pēyarē evarkkum.* ‘ in nāyikā bhāvam

tēsamudaiyāi – you who wants a tēsu (tējas/ form) – [kulasēkara āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) wanted to be in any form which would be related to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – ūnēṟu selvattu udan piṟavi yān vēṇḍēn … [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) pon malaiyil ēdēnum āvēnē.

————

In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) and other gōpikās wake up a girl who knows a bit of bhagavat viśayam, but has forgotten about it and lying in bed; gōpikās come there to wake her up and remind her about bh**A**gavata viśayam; some gōpikas did not get early as they were lying in bed and enjoying on their thoughts on kriṣṇan.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 8. Kil vanam

*kīḻvānam veḷḷenṟu erumai ciṟuvīdu mēivān parandana kāṇ mikkuḷḷa piḷḷaigaḷum pōvān pōginṟārai pōgāmal kāttu unnaik kūvuvān vandu ninṟōm kōdukalam udaiya pāvai! eḻundirāi pādipparai kōṇḍu māvāi piḷandānai mallarai māttiya dēvādi dēvanai cenṟu nām sēvittāl āvāvenṟu ārāindu aruḷēlor empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōdukalam udaiya pāvai – O the girl who is of interest (to kriṣṇan); kīḻ vānam – the sky on the east side; veḷḷenṟu – has dawned (became white); erumai – buffaloes; siṟuvīdu – released out for some time (in the early morning); parandana kāṇ – spread out (in the fields); mēivān – to grace (the grass with early morning devs); mikkuḷḷa piḷḷaigaḷum – (regarding) all other girls; pōgāmal kāttu – (we) stopped them from going; pōvān pōginṟārai – (who were) going, with going itself as the goal; vandu ninṟōm – and came and stood steadily (by your door step); unnaik kūvuvān – to call you; eḻundirāi – please get up; pādi – (and) sing (kaṇṇan’s guṇās); paṟai koṇḍu – and get paṟai (instrument/mōkśam) (from Him); mā vāi piḷandānai – who killed horse shaped kēsi; mallarai māttiya – and who destroyed the wrestlers (cāṇūra-muśtika); dēvādi dēvanai – and who is the head of nitya-sūris; nām cenṟu sēvittāl – if we went (to Him) and give our respects to Him; ārāyndu – (He would) inquire (our needs); A A enṟu aruḷ – and He will say O!, and he would help us

**Перевод:**

**kīḻ vānam veḷḷenṟu**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The gōpikās who got up first, those gōpikas too, instead of thinking that it is enough if a few woke up, want to wake up all of their friends to join them and go to kriṣṇan. This is related to bhāgavata (devotees of perumān) viśayam.*

“It is getting bright in the East, and you are still sleeping?” so saying, the gōpikās are waking up this gōpikā.

“East has not become bright. You gōpikās are looking in that direction, and the brightness in your faces is reflected in there – you are *‘tingaḷ tiru mugattu cēi iḻaiyār* (pāsuram 30) (faces are like the moon)’. So it is *anyatā-jñānam* (mistaking one attribute for another); Is there some other sign of dawn?”

**erumai ciruvīdu mēivān parandana kāṇ**

“The buffaloes have spread out to eat grass with devs in the early morning; srī nandagōpar has got goats, cows, buffaloes etc., (if we delayed,) kriṣṇan would go behind them – then whom will we see? Please get up”.

Her father (all the [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) are considered [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)’s father (*anju kudikku oru santadiyāy* \[upadēsa rattinamālai\]), here it is referring to [toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/08/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)) who sang to [periya perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (srī ranga nātan) ‘*mēttiḷa mēdigaḷ taḷai vidum āyargaḷ*‘ (the covherd boys are taking care of the young cows), trying to wake Him up in [tirupaḷḷiyeḻucci](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=374). The daughter is also saying that the cows are already up, but she is trying to wake up a bhāgavata (kriṣṇan’s devotee); (better than [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/08/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) waking up kriṣṇan (periya perumāḷ) (*āḻvārgaḷ tam seyalai vinji niṟkum tanmaiyaḷāi* \[upadēsa rattina mālai\]).

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) being a Brahmin girl is supposed to know about hōmam, etc., but she is talking about the early morning dew-grass (*nunip pul*) eating habits of buffaloes – similar to how her walk, talk and odour changed due to pretending like a gōpikai, she with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s grace is also having the knowledge about these animals’ life.

The sleepy gōpikā replies, “It is the darkness that is spreading and drowning because of the brightness of your faces. They are not buffaloes; you are seeing that darkness and thinking that they are buffaloes. This is your *viparīta-jñānam* (mistaking one thing for another); darkness would not be there in front of you who have *sātvīka guṇa*. So, this is not a sign of dawn”.

The gōpikās ask, “We accept that all the signs we told so far are because of our misunderstanding; would you tell us why this is not yet dawn?”

“The gōpikās from all of five lakh houses have not come here; only about a thousand gōpikās have shown up. The rest have to wake up and come here, and that would be the sign of dawn of the day”.

In response,

**mikkuḷḷa piḷḷaigaḷum** (all remaining gōpikās of āyppādi)

“Everyone except you, have been going”

“How come they did not call me”

“Because there were too many people, they did not know that you were not present; they are all going fast as they missed kriṣṇan”

**pōvān pōginṟārai** (those who are going eagerly, and enjoying the journey)

“The srīvaiṣṇavās who after their life here would go in ‘*arcirāti mārgam*‘; *akrūrar* going to kriṣṇa (for taking him to kamsa), and the people who go on tiruvēnKata (tiruppati) yāttirai (journey), like how they think that the going (journey) itself is the *prayōjanam* (destiny), these gōpikās are also going to kriṣṇan.

“Where do I go if they all have already gone?”

**pōgāmal kāttu**  (we stopped them from going (without you))

all we had to tell all the gōpikās was that You have not yet joined – they just stopped in their tracks (consistent with what they said in the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram: *sēyyādana seyyōm* – won’t do what our [purvācāriās](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) have not done – will follow their ways), and they are waiting for you

**unnaikkūvuvān** (to call you)

We stopped them, and to follow the norm, we came in front of your door and called you.

**vandu ninṟōm –** we came here, and we stood by your bed; like how *vibīśaṇan* came and stood steadily in the sky (to see śrī rāmān)

“When so many gōpikās are waiting ready to go, why did you come and stand in front of my house? You are Not ready to go Without me, but here I am, Not getting up from my bed or showing my face (not entertaining your idea of waking me up); are you not upset with me because of this?”

They reply,

**kōdukalam udaiya pāvāi**

kōdukalam: *kudūkalam* (motivated/joyful/bhakti about kriṣṇan)

It is good for us to come to your house and go with You to see kriṣṇan; you are one of his favorite gōpikās, so if we are with kriṣṇan when you are also beside Him, that would make our being with kriṣṇan meaningful; we will also be in the good books of kriṣṇan if He knows that we did not leave a devotee when going to Him;

pāvāi – we do these to that gōpikā who understands and also has got her mind accepted to *pāratantriyam* (subservience to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)).

==\> āḻvār: In this 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram related to referring to gōpikā being woken up as āḻvār – [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) saying ‘*kōdukalam udaiya pāvai*‘ is referring to [bhūdattāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/). In his iraṇḍām tiruvandādi, [bhūdattāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) sang ‘*dēvādi dēvan enap paduvān mun oru nāḷ māvāi piḷanda magan*‘, also ‘*endan aḷavanṟāl yānudaiya anbu*‘. In tiruvāimoḻi 5-2-2, [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) sang ‘ *mādavan bhūtam*‘ which too refers to *kōdukalam*.

**eḻundirāi**

Please give us the greatness that it is we who came and woke you up, and then you woke up because of that.

Unlike that ‘*peṇṇin varuttam aṟiyāda*van \[nāciār tirumoḻi 13-1\] (He who does not understand woman’s sadness), you who understand it, would you still be lying down?

**“**What is it that we would do if I woke up?”

**pādip paṟai kōṇḍu**

The wealth that we are going to get now (going to kriṣṇan) is nothing like any other wealth that we got till now; earlier the elders had said no to kriṣṇa and no to gōpikās for meeting each other; we all had to hide within our minds and hearts our affection for kriṣṇan, and were very careful and afraid to show it out; but now all the people themselves have given us permission and also have requested kriṣṇan to help us in this nōnbu. This is like opening the dam from our hearts – we are so thrilled and we openly sing and praise about kriṣṇan – there is no stopping;

paṟai – the instrument that is used as part of nōnbu – as far as the people are concerned; for us it is the kriṣṇā anubhavam.

**“**What would you sing about?”

**mā vāi piḷandānai**

“We will sing about kriṣṇan who split the mouth of asuran kēsi and saved us and saved Himself to give to us; the One who made Himself available to us to make fun of Him and to enjoy Him.

**mallarai māttiya**

In maturāpuri He killed the two big fighters (wrestlers) for all the gōpikās there to see and celebrate; we will also celebrate that here.

Inner meaning: the two fighters – our kāmam (our wants of materialistic things) and krōdam (anger when we don’t get what we wanted). kriṣṇan will help kill these two.

kriṣṇan cleverly moved away and let the two wrestlers fall against each other and die; srī rāmānujar also would let the other sects argue against each other so that they would decide their own fate, and let the final winner come to him and lose; this is so that he would not have to waste too much time debating with each and every one).

**dēvādi dēvanai**

“He is the god for dēvas, people, all beings, and non-beings; He went to maturāpuri and killed kuvalaiyāpīda, cāṇūra muśitka, and got friends with the wealthy gōpikās of maturāpuri – would He come and befriend us poor covherd gōpikās who cannot even tell the difference between their right and left hands?”

**cenṟu**

We will go to Him; He should have come to us and saved us (as a [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), but we will go and show Him how our *viraham* (missing Him) has eaten our body; He will feel bad for us and He will bless us;

cakravarty tirumagan – only He went to all the sages, to mahārājar (sukrīvan), and to vibīśanāḻvān to bless them; in fact He felt bad that He did not go all the way to lankai to bless vibīhśanāḻvān as they met only at the shore near [tiruppullāṇi](http://thiruppullani.koyil.org/) (divya dēsam).

kriṣṇan too, only He went to *mālākārar* in maturāpuri; we as the *śaraṇāgatās* are His property – only the owner worries about and cares for the property; the property does not worry; when [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gets us, He the owner feels happy, the property does not; in our case, since we are given *jñānam*, we would feel happy when/that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is feeling happy.

āḻvārs sent birds etc., as messengers to ask [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to come and see them; with a very high level of bhakti towards [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) they even did *ūdal* (showing their being upset as part of being in love) with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) asking Him, the One who came to them, to go away.

Given all this, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) says, if We Go there to Him, He will be so thrilled. He would not reject us thinking that His *dēvādi dēvan*. (Also, our going there to Him is the first stage, which we all will be in for a long time; as bhakti increases and we get the stage of a mature *śaraṇāgatar*, we will be like this sleepy gōpikā saying that let [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) come and bless me; crossing even that is the aḻvārs’ stage).

**nām sēvittāl**

“If we go to Him (out of turn), will He avoid seeing us?”

“He would not (because of Him feeling bad for us). But if He did, we shall suffer like *bharatāḻvān* did”.

(bharatan begged śrī rāman to go back to *ayōḍhya* and become the king – but śrī rāman did not agree; rāman felt bad for a very long time that He did not agree to bharatan even when it is bharatan who **came** and saw Him).

“He will be kind like how He was to the crow”

(the crow – kākāsuran did apacāram (mistake) by hurting sītā pirātti. śrī rāman wanted to kill him, but pirātti made him do saraṇāgati and rāman calmed down and let him go – out of his kindness).

He should bow His head for us because of His love for us, but if we did that to Him (*அத்தலை இத்தலையானால் /attalai ittalai ānāl*) He would melt and accept us.

**āvā enṟu ārāindu aruḷ**

He would think “Aha! Aha! These gōpikās live with the subservience that I would provide them mōkśam, and so they have been waiting for me to go and meet them – it is my mistake that I let them come here to see me instead!” and He would be so kind to us and would feel so happy to see us, and would inquire (*ārāindu*) about our well-being. So there is not anything wrong if we went to Him; only we have to hurry up. So please get up and quickly join us.

(also read as – *āvā enṟu ārāindu aruḷuvān, kōdukalam udaiya pāvai* eḻudirāi).

(That gōpikā also finally gets up and joins other gōpikās; they go to the next house to wake up another gōpikā).

In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) wakes up yet another gōpikā and explains to her that they stopped many of the gōpikās and came to her house so that they would be happy to see her joining them for going to kriṣṇan; and kriṣṇan would be happy to understand their eagerness that they came to see him, instead of the norm of waiting for Him to come to them.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 9. Tumani madattu

*tūmaṇi mādattu cuṟṟum viḷakku eriya dūbham kamaḻat tuyil aṇai mēl kaṇ vaḷarum māmān magaḷē! maṇik kadavam tāḷ tiṟavāi māmīr! avaḷai eḻuppīrō! un magaḷ tān ūmaiyō? anṟic cevidō? anantalō? ēmapperum tuyil mandirappattāḷō māmāyan mādavan vaikuntan enṟu enṟu nāmam palavum navinṟēlōr empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

tū maṇi mādattu – In the house with naturally good ornamental stones embedded in them; viḷakku eriya – with sacred lamps glowing; cuṟṟum – in all the places; dūpam kamaḻa – and with scented smoke spreading around; māmān magaḷē – O daughter of (my) uncle; kaṇ vaḷarum – who is sleeping; tuyil aṇai mēl – on the bed (that makes one who lies down on it to sleep); tiṟavāi – please do open; maṇik kadavam tāḷ – the bolt of the door that is made of ornamental stones; māmīr – O aunt!; avaḷai eḻuppīrō – please wake her up; un magaḷ tān – is your daughter; ūmaiyō – dumb so cannot speak?; anṟi – or; sevidō – deaf so cannot hear?; anantalō – (or) just sleeping (due to tiredness)?; ēmap pattāḷō – (or) is she being restricted/prevented (to come out); perum tuyil mandirap pattāḷō – (or) under control of a spell that makes her sleep for a long time?; māmāyan – He who possesses unfathomable abilities; mādavan – nātan (husband) of lakśmī; vaikuntan enṟu enṟu – srī vaikunta nātan, and similar and more; nāmam palavum navinṟu – we chanted several of such divine names; (still your daughter is not waking up!?)

**Перевод:**

**tūmaṇi mādattu**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*They are waking up a gōpikā who has the full jñānam that it is kriṣṇan’s duty to come and get her to be with Him, as she understands that it is her nature to be subservient to Him and it is His nature to save us (from samsāram).*

Using the jewels which did not have any flaw to begin with, which did not have to go through the process of removal of flaws; like the difference between a samsāri who removed that samsāram and got mukti, and those nitya sūris (like [viśvaksēnar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/senai-mudhaliar/), garudāḻvān) who did not have the smell of samsāram to begin with; The palace/room embedded with such flawless jewels;  (kriṣṇan would use the flawless jewels for pirātti’s palaces, and for decorating other places He would keep the ones that had had flaws and then were cleaned; it implies here that the gōpikā sleeping is also a favorite of kriṣṇan).

They are praising the house of this devotee; [kūratāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) said, “if one thinks great of other srīvaiśnavas’ houses, that would help us go in the right path (to our destiny) .

**cuṟṟum viḷakku eriya**

She has lit lamps in all the places where kriṣṇan would walk with her holding her hands;

eriya – while the lamps (and our hearts) are not lighted (because of separation from kriṣṇan), here she is, sleeping surrounded by auspicious lamps.

**tūbam kamaḻa**

With wonderful fragrance (which is soft and does not irritate the eyes) also surrounding her. While we are not able to enjoy any good fragrance (due to separation), how is it that you are able to relax with all these around you?

**tuyil aṇai mēl kaṇ vaḷarum**

While our beds feel like heated surface for us, you are feeling so comfortable sleeping in your bed as if kriṣṇan is with you; like iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇan in forest) we are not sleeping, and you are sleeping so nicely;

When it is kriṣṇan who has to come and rescue us, why are these gōpikās getting anxiety – was the state of mind of the gōpikā inside; when kriṣṇan is the *puruśārttam* (upēyam / goal / one to reach) why is she being lazy lying in the bed – was the stance of the gōpikās outside.

kaṇ vaḷarum – they are using gentle/sophisticated words to say that she is sleeping; that is the respectful way to address those who are involved in bhagavat viśayam;  when *nambiyēṟu-tiru-udaiyār-dāsar* left for paramapadam, [nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/) said we should use appropriate phrases about this depending on how they lived – in case of those who were deeply involved in bhagavat-kainkaryam, we should say ‘reached tiruvadi’; for those who were focused on bhāgavata-kainkaryam, we should say ‘ascended to tiru-nādu’; in case of those who were focused on ācārya-kainkaryam, we should say ‘reached ācāryan tiruvadi’.

**māmān magaḷē**

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) wishes to have the gōpikās of tiruvāyppādi as Her relatives (**translator’s note**: *srīvaiśnavargaḷaik kaṇḍāl bandukkaḷai kaṇḍāṟ pōlēyum* – srīvacana bhuśaṇam. *ஸ்ரீவைஷ்ணவர்களைக் கண்டால் பந்துக்களைக் கண்டாற் போலேயும்* – ஸ்ரீவசநபூஷணம்); [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) a small brahmin girl in [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) is calling the covherd girl who lived in the north in tiruvāyppādi and that too in a different yugam several tens of thousands of years ago; being included in the group of bhāgavatās who played and lived with kriṣṇan is how She wants to be.

==\> āḻvār: By māmān magaḷē, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is referring to [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar). Similar to “*tūmaṇi mādattuc cuṟṟum viḷakku eriya*“, [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar) in his nānmugan tiruvandādi says “*en madikku viṇṇellām uṇḍō vilai*“, “*jñānamāgi jñāyiṟāgi*“. [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar) had sung “*mādavanai*“, and “*vaikuntac celavanār*“, and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) also similarly “*māmāyan, mādavan, vaikuntan*” in this pāsuram.

The gōpikā from inside says “You open the door yourselves and enter my house”.

**maṇik kadavam tāḷ tiravāi**

“The gem crystals are so bright that we cannot see the lock of the door. So you get up and open the door”

(Only ācāryan can open and bring out our jñānam; we go to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) through the ācāryan only)

The gōpikā’s mother who is also sitting inside asks her daughter “why are you being quiet when they are calling you for this long?”

**māmīr! avaḷai eḻuppīrō!**

Hearing this, the gōpikās ask from outside, “māmīr! (dear aunt!) would you please wake her up?”

srīvaiṣṇavan looks to serve not only [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), not only His devotees, but also devotees of devotees. [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is calling the gōpikā’s mother as māmi to gain relationship not only with the gōpikā but also with her mother.

On the other hand, her mother might have felt bad for being addressed as māmi – she would have like to be addressed as an adiyār (devotee) – this is similar to the following from aitīhyam:  [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) in ‘*kaḷvan kol*‘ pāsuram where he is in nāyikā bhāvam (considering himself as girl-lover of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), leaves her mother and goes off with [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); so her mother cries out that her daughter (āḻvār) left her considering her relationship as a mother only and not as His devotee. So she preferred not the relationship name but being referred to as a devotee. So [kūrattāḻvan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/)‘s wife āṇḍāḷ used to refer to [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) as ‘*vettimaiyar*‘ (one who talks rough); and she would refer to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) as ‘kōyil aṇṇar’ (which is related to 100 tadā akkāra adisil that [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) presented to tirumāliruncōlai aḻagar based on the wish of [srīvilliputūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/), and when she came out of arcai to thank [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) for that, she called [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) as kōyil aṇṇar (brother from srīrangam)). So when [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/)‘s wife āṇḍāḷ referred to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) as kovil aṇṇar, emperumānār felt sad and said, “Am I not a devotee? You are calling me with a relationship name of ‘aṇṇar’, because of which [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)‘s daugter also might forget about me in the name of relationship.

The gōpikā is still not getting up, so..

**un magaḷ tān ūmaiyō?**

Is your daughter dumb that she cannot respond to our requests? Is kriṣṇan keeping her busy?

**anṟic cevidō?**

a dumb could at least hear us and come open the door, so, is it that she did not even hear us as she could be a deaf?

**anantalō?**

or did she play with kriṣṇan for a long time and got tired and only now she lied down on her bed?

==\> (Above three relate to asking whether she is not getting ready for vāk, mānasa, and kāyika kainkaryams).

**ēmap perum tuyil mandirap pattāḷō?**

did some one (you) put protection for her to not be able to go out?

ōr did [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) put some spell (mantram) powder (சொக்குப் பொடி) on her to get her to go behind him?

“This is not working. Please say some tiru nāmams to wake her up” says the mother.

“We say a lot of tiru nāmams”

“Good, what are those tiru nāmams?”

“They are..”

**māmāyan**

With His surprising sēśtitams (activities/mischief) He got all the innocent gōpikās of tiruvāyppādi for Himself

**mādavan**

The husband of pirātti, to show His kindness/to-not-look-at-our-mistakes, is here in āyppādi.  He might have done gurukulam (school) to learn these qualities from tāyar.

When pirātti is around, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is even more kind to us; cakravarty tirumagan did not kill anyone when pirātti was near Him.

**vaikuntan**

The creator of all the worlds; with the place (vaikuntam) fit for such a [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); not only gōpikās, all the men also wish to be subservient to Him.

vaikuntan mādavan māmāyan: A [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with such greatness has made him easily available to you dear māmān magaḷē!

vaikuntan māmāyan mādavan: With the names announcing His greatness, the names announcing his kindness/simplicity, and between the two the one who stole the hearts of the covherd gōpikās

**enṟu enṟu nāmam palavum navinṟu (in these ways, reciting so many of His names)**

māmāyan enṟu, mādavan enṟu, vaikuntan enṟu – Covering the range of three sahasra nāmams (1000 names of Him).

Also read as: nāmam palavum navinṟu, māmīr avaḷai eḻuppīrō! (saying so many names of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would you please wake her up, dear aunt).

ōr, nāmam palavum navinrōm (we said so many nāmams of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), still she is not getting up, māmīr could you please wake her up!

——–

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 10. Norru suvarggam

*nōṟṟu suvarggam puguginṟa ammanāi! māṟṟamum tārārō vāsal tiṟavādār nāṟṟa tuḻāi mudi nārāyaṇan nammāl pōṟṟap parai tarum puṇṇiyanāl paṇḍu oru nāḷ kūṟṟattin vāivīḻnda kumbakaraṇanum tōṟṟum unakkē pērum tuyil tān tandānō āṟṟa anantal udaiyāy arumkalamē tēṟṟamāi vandu tiravēlōr empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

nōṟṟu – (you) did nōnbu; suvarggam puguginṟa – (and now) enjoying the heaven without any break; vāsal tiṟavādār – you are not opening the door; māṟṟamum tārārō – would you not answer us at least?; nāṟṟattuḻāi mudi – One who is wearing fragrant tiruttuḻāy; nārāyaṇan – one having the unparalleled name of nārāyaṇan; nammāl pōṟṟap paṟai tarum – one who is sung pallāṇḍu by us; one who gives us our goal that is doing kainkaryam; puṇṇiyanāl – by (such) emperumān who is darmam; kumbakaraṇanum – that kumbakaraṇan; paṇḍu oru nāḷ – once upon a time; kūṟṟattin vāi vīḻnda – fell on yama’s way; unakkē tōṟṟu – (did he) lose to you; tandānō – (and) went away giving you; perum tuyil tān – (his) big sleepiness?; āṟṟa anantal udaiyāy – you who is having a nice sleep!; arum kalamē – you who is like a rare jewel; tēṟṟamāi vandu tiṟa – get yourself together, and open (the door)

**Перевод:**

**nōṟṟu suvarggam puguginra ammanāi!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The love that all gōpikās have towards kriṣṇan and the effort they take to go behind him – that is the effort Kannan takes to go behind this gōpikā – they are waking up such a gōpikā in this pāsuram.*

Since this gōpikā did not wake up even after dawn, they are being sarcastic saying ‘This is nice, you wanted to do nōnbu and be happy’; also means that this gōpikā has already achieved the benefits of nōnbu so she is in the position of not having to do nōnbu.

This gōpikā has understood that He is the means (upāyam), so she is thinking she does not have to do anything from her side, so she is just sleeping; if one understands His nature of protecting us, then one doesn’t have to do anything to gain His protection; so too if one understands one’s *sēśatvam* (servitude/secondary nature to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/));

Is this not the nature of those gōpikas who are trying to wake her up? (How come they are not just sleeping too?) – since they are having the anxiety of getting their goal, it is making some of them not stay put in their place, so they want to hurry and get to kriṣṇan. For us who are having *siddōpāya* niśtai (that He would come to us and accept us) too there are karma, jñāna, bhakti – for us the karmam would be about doing kainkaryam;  our jñānam would be about understanding of our svarūpam (nature/relationship with [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), bhakti would be about interest/eagerness about the destiny that is [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

nōṟṟu – you who said that we will all go together early in the morning, will do nōnbu together, will get kriṣṇa anubhavam together is now sleeping even after the day has progressed; you are enjoying your early morning as if you have already done the nōnbu and gained [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

suvarggam – pleasure/enjoyment;  suvarggam puguginṟa – continuously enjoying the pleasure (kriṣṇan). (It does not refer to the lowly 3rd world sorggam (suva: ))

ammanāi – you are still sleeping, like you have to save us and we have to be saved by you.

The gōpikā inside did not move, thinking how come you are calling me ammanāi, when I am a devotee of you all; she is happy to be just hearing their voices;

The gōpikās outside are thinking good about the one inside, that she is having kriṣṇa anubhavam and so she is keeping quiet.

The gōpikā inside is so happy to receive atiti that she is not able to speak. (atiti – guest – srivaiśnavās who visit our house without looking for a titi – good day/time)

The ones outside tell her to speak because they are afraid that bhagavān might misunderstand the sleeping gōpikā to have done bhāgavata apacāram by keeping quiet to bhāgavatas.

==\> āḻvār: ammanāi refers to [kulasēkarāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/). In his perumāḷ tirumoḻi, he said “*vaṇḍu kiṇḍu naṟum tuḻāi mālai uṟṟavaraip perum tiru mārvanai*” and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) here says “*nāṟṟat tuḻāi mudi nārāyaṇan*“; he is going ranga yātrai (after doing samāsrayaṇam), and so [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is calling him ‘*nōṟṟu, suvarggam puguginra ammanāi*“.

**māṟṟamum tārārō vāsal tiṟavādār** (you have not opened the door, would you not reply even?)

They are saying this as they are not able to tolerate her silence; if the door is closed, should the mouth also be closed?  Can you not reply to the relatives even if you are having a lot of wealth? Inside your house you have given yourself to kaṇṇan, but can you not even give your talking to us? He is not letting you get up but can you not at least say a few words to us? While looking at Him, can you not lend your ears to us? You may not want to give your kriṣṇan, can you not give us yourself (for going to the nōnbu)?

The gōpikā replies, “You are blaming me that I am keeping kaṇṇan here. Do you think He is here?”

**nāṟṟa tuḻāi mudi**

**“**You may try to hide Him and He may keep quiet too, but it is not possible like how you cannot hide the fragrance of sandalwood.”

“If He hugs me just once, His fragrance will be with me even after bathing nine times; I came to bed after meeting you all, and you all came to my door last evening itself to wake me up. With so many of you standing there, and with elders’ protection of the house, would He have been able to enter this house?”

**nārāyaṇan**

“Does Hē need the doors or entrance to enter your house, like we do? Is He not present everywhere? If He is with you in your house, it is difficult for Him to only leave your place (not difficult to enter)”. He is [nārāyaṇan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

He won’t leave even those who don’t show their affection to Him, will He leave you who is in love with Him?

**nāṟṟat tuḻāi mudi nārayaṇan** – He is wearing the tuḻāi garland to save all the people who want Him.

“If I don’t open my mouth they would say that I am not talking. If I said anything they would say I am giving answer to everything” – thinking so, she kept quiet lying in her bed as if sleeping.

**nammāl**

He is such that even we raw covherd girls could go and surrender to Him, He is so noble and simple.

**pōṟṟa**

These covherd girls’ surrender is not upāyam (means), it is done only because of their interest in surrendering to Him; as we are born like the way we are, He is also born so,  so that He is praised

**paṟai tarum puṇṇiyanāl**

He gives us mōkśam

He is the means and the destiny (both upāyam and upēyam; puruśārtam); we enjoy praising Him.

Praise as we are His friends too.

By that [perumaṇ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/),

**pandu oru nāḷ** (once upon a time,)

Once upon a time there was someone who kept sleeping; we had only heard about that day, but today we are seeing that here.

**kūṟṟattin vāi vīḻnda**

Who because of doing wrong in front of [perumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) fell and died like an insect falling into the lamp; like drowning in a lake created for drinking water;

**kumbakaraṇanum**

Has even that kumbakaruṇan, who is the leader of sleeping, lost in a sleeping match with you and went away after giving you his sleep also (that is, along with the mistakes)? His – sleep; Yours – Big sleep (perum tuyil); He – separated one woman (due to his association with rāvaṇan); You – separating all the gōpikās of this place from you and sleeping in your bed; he slept for 6 months and was awake once in 6 months – you are sleeping till from the creation time (they are saying like this because even one second away from kaṇṇan feels like a very long time for them).

“They are comparing adiyēn with that person who did asahya apacāram (doing wrong even without reason)” – thinking so, the gōpikā inside wants to show them that she should not be compared to him and that she is getting up quickly, so she says “kriṣṇa, kriṣṇa”.

‘kumbarkaraṇan’ indicates tamō guṇam (other two being satva gunam (serenity/tranquility) and rajō guṇam (action/anger));  tamo guṇam is laziness. (You are with a lot of satva guṇam – even without having the tamō guṇam you are sleeping so much; seeing this, kumbakaraṇan accepted defeat and gave his sleeping power to you)

**āṟṟa anantal udaiyāy**

“Would we be able to see you getting up, so that we can live happily?”

Do we need to see Him wake up? We would be happy to do [tiruppaḷḷiyeḻuci](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=374) to you.

**arumgalamē**

As she got up quickly, they felt that she is the central jewel for them.

“You are the rare jewel that we can get”. \[ like [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)\] . (they are happy that if she gets ready, kaṇṇan also will come and celebrate all their presence).

Listening to this she tried to walk hurriedly, so

**tēṟṟamāi vandu tiṟa** (carefully come and open the door)

Walk carefully without tripping over the steps or other bumps; keep in mind, we are all assembled here including those who can scold you, so don’t come as is from the bed, but get yourself composed and then come join us.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 11. Karruk karavai

*kaṟṟuk kaṟavai gaṇangaḷ pala kaṟandu ceṟṟār tiṟal aḻiyac cenṟu ceruc ceyyum kuṟṟam onṟillāda kōvalar tam poṟkodiyē puṟṟaravalgul punamayilē pōdarāi cuṟṟattut tōḻimār ellārum vandunin muṟṟam pugundu mugil vaṇṇan pēr pāda ciṟṟādē pēsādē selva peṇḍātti nī eṟṟukku uṟangum poruḷēlor empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

gaṇangaḷ pala kaṟandu – draws milk from several groups; kanṟu (kaṟṟu) kaṟavai – of young cows, and; cenṟu – goes to the enemies’ places; ceruc ceyyum – and fights with them; ceṟṟār tiral aḻiya – such that the strength of enemies get destroyed; kuṟṟam onṟillāda – (but) not having faults like hitting an enemy who is showing his back while running away, etc; kōvalar tam – O (sister) of (such) gōpālars; poṟkodiyē – who looks like a slim (and beautiful) vine made of gold; puṟṟu aravu algul – and having the back curved like the shape of head of a snake living in its hole; puna mayilē – and like the peacock living in its place; pōdarāi – please get up and come here; cuṟṟattut tōḻimār ellārum – all of your friends who are relatives; vandu – have come (together); nin muṟṟam pugundu – into your tirumāligai’s verandah; mugil vaṇṇan pēr pāda – to sing the names of kaṇṇan who is of the color of dark blue clouds; selvappeṇḍātti nī – you who is all the treasure (for us); ciṟṟādē pēsādē – not moving, and not talking; uṟangum poruḷ eṟṟukku – what could be there to sleep?

**Перевод:**

**kaṟṟu kaṟavai**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*If kriṣṇan is there for all the girls, this girl is special among all. She is thinking “Do I have to do nōnbu to get kaṇṇan? Let him do nōnbu and come to me”, and she is sleeping early in the morning in her bed. All the girls are in front of her house trying to get her up and join their nōnbu. They think she is equal to kriṣṇan for them.*

Cows that are looking young like calves – because they grow young with the fond touch of kriṣṇan;

**gaṇangaḷ pala**

gaṇam -\> crowd/bunch; pala-\> many

It is not possible to count the cows in a bunch, and cannot count the number of such groups as well; there are so many cows, even higher than the number of stars in the sky, the number of grains in the beach; the number of pāpams in an individual; similar to how there is no count for jīvatamās.

**kaṟandu**

Like how one [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) is determining and controlling the actions of all the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s, one covherd boy is able to follow his varnāsramam and get all the milk from all these countless cows; the cows, because of the wealth of kaṇṇan’s touch, are so full of milk that the covherd boy milks the cows fully to reduce the tension in their teats;

gaṇangaḷ pala kaṟandu – ācāryan giving jñānam to lots of groups of siśyas.

**ceṟṟār**

enemies of such covherd boys – those who are trying to create problems to kriṣṇan (that is kamsan, et al) \[but kriṣṇan would say that He does not have any enemies; only those who are enemies to his bhaktās are His enemies\]

svāpadēśam: those who are upāyantaraparar (using some other means to reach their goal, e.g., jñāna yōgam etc.,) and upēyāntaraparar (asking for some other benefits/goals)

**tiṟal aḻiya**

kamsan and his group, who think that even dēvas are not equal to them, cannot intimidate these āyppādi men and so their ego is killed.

svāpadēsam: tiṟal – trying to put in their own efforts (and the accompanying ahankāram) to achieve their goal of reaching Him or other benefits; aḻiya – for that attitude to be destroyed

**cenṟu seruc ceyyum**

āyppādi men cannot wait for the enemies to come to them and then fight, so they would go to the enemies’ places, even if they have to cross the river, and fight with them to save kaṇṇan; equal in valor to cakravarty tirumagan who went to the enemy’s place to fight him.

maturā was very close to tiruvāyppādi. How come vasudēvar thought it was a safe place for kaṇṇan? It is because kamsan is scared of the tiruvāyppādi gōpars. He Never entered that place. That is why he sent his asura servants instead of going there by himself – that too he told them to go in other forms (மாறு வேஷம்). And kriṣṇan said do we need these great gōpars to kill kamsan? Just the lowly me the covherd boy (கடையனான இடையன்) is enough.  At the end too, kamsan sent akrūrar to bring kaṇṇan.  kriśnan *cenṟu* (went and) killed kamsan. He killed him so that he would not commit more pāpams.

svāpadēśam: the ones who keep Him as their means, would do vijaya yātrai themselves (to correct other people who use other means)

**kuṟṟam onṟillāda**

tiruvāyppādi gōpars who would not be idle if they see anyone coming to fight; and would fight without breaking the rules, (when the enemy loses his weapon, they wait for him to gather one, and would not attack when he does not have a weapon to fight back) – *inṟu pōi nāḷai vārāi* – said cakravarty tirumagan.

Because they have surrendered to kaṇṇan, even if someone complains to kaṇṇan about something that they did, He would say, “Whatever they did was right”.

**kōvalar tam poṟkodiyē**

Hey the (sleeping) girl,

kōvalar tam – the most favorite of this whole tiruvāyppādi,

poṟkodi – who has fully given her livelihood and well-being in her husband’s hands; [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s who have fully given their livelihood and well-being in the hands of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); like the vines that fully depend on their support; *por kodi* – soundaryam – samudāya sōbhai – overall beauty; and lāvaṇyam – avayava sōbhai – beauty of specific parts.

poṟkodi – hēma, latā

poṟkodi – possessing jñāna vairākyam (small waist)

==\> āḻvār – In this pāsuram, ‘kōvalar tum por kodiyē’ refers to [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/). In periyāḻvār tirumoḻi he sang, ‘*vittu cittan manattē kōil kōṇḍa kōvalanai kōḻunguḷir mugil vaṇṇanai*‘, and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) in this pāsuram is singing along those lines, ‘*mugil vaṇṇan pēr pāda*‘, and he was also fond of the the kōvalar (gōpars/gōpikās) as he sang many including ‘*kaṇṇan kēsavan nambi piṟandinil*‘.

**puṟṟu aravu-algul**

You who is slim and elegant like a snake entering its den; her beauty makes women to wish to be men (like draupati’s girl friends); similar to how His beauty makes men wish to wear women’s clothes (riśis upon seeing rāman); This is consistent with how srīvaiṣṇavas should be interested in the vellness of ācāryan’s (and srīvaiṣṇavas’) tirumēni (divine body).  vāḻi tirunāmams describe the tirumēni too; [tirukōśtiyūr nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) thinks of [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)‘s back (that is like turtle’s back) as his savior; [pinbaḻagiya perumāḷ jīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/04/20/pinbhazhagiya-perumal-jiyar/) ate medicines so that he could be in this world to see the tirumuka maṇḍalam (divine face) of his ācāryan [nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/) having water drops in it, as he regularly did every morning after his ācāryan took bath in the river.

svāpadēsam ==\> algul – para bhakti

**puna mayilē**

like how the peacock in its home showing its beautiful tail feathers would attract the other, you have a beautiful hair that is attractive to kaṇṇan and all the gōpikās too.

about parama bhakti

**pōdarāi**

you don’t have to help us be with kaṇṇan, at least, just for our benefit walk a few steps; we want to walk behind you and see your beautiful walk; please go with us for our full satisfaction; like how it was beautiful to see rāman going to the forest (*sōbhayan daṇḍakāraṇyam*).

“Are all of you ready? Are you all here?”

**suṟṟattut tōḻimār ellārum**

All the girls are her friends; all the people are her relatives; all her people are like her, doing kainkaryam for the sake of doing it (not expecting anything in return / not doing for any other reason).

**ellārum vandu**

They are all here – they go to sleep everyday thinking that they live for your well-being and your relationship.

**nin muṟṟam pugundu**

nin muṟṟam – The verandah(?) of your house is the favorite place for kaṇṇan to come and play; whenever He knows that all of us are going here to play, He wants to join us immediately so that He can be here; He would come stand here and look at us with His happy and naughty smile;

When this place is kaṇṇan’s favorite, it goes without saying that it is our favorite too. (*muṟṟam pugundu muṟuval seitu nīnṟīr* (periya tirumoḻi 10-8-5); *muṟṟattūdu pugundu ninmugam kātti* *pun muṟuval seydu* (nāciār tirumoḻi 2-9)).

vālmīki writes ‘vibīśaṇan ‘came to rāman’ instead of using the phrase ‘went from lankā’. It was the native place to be for vibīśaṇan; your muṟṟam is the native place to be for us, say the gōpikās.

“What are you going to do entering this place in my house?”

**mugil vaṇṇan pēr pāda**

We will sing his nāmams thinking about His beauty and kindness which you love of Him.

**ciṟṟādē pēsādē**  (you are not moving about or talking)

The girl inside was keeping quiet reminiscing the beauty of ‘mugil vaṇṇan’ (kaṇṇan who is dark like the rain clouds).

ciṟṟādē – Not moving about to indicate that you have woken up; her mischief and words are the food for the eyes and ears of these girls who are so fond of her. They are feeling sad because of not getting these.

**selvap peṇḍātti nī**

You are a girl with pāratantriyam (subservient to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), being with you is the wealth for us.

You are fully in kriṣṇa anubhavam, don’t we have to get that anubhavam?

“I am also missing kaṇṇan; am I enjoying here with kriṣṇan?”

If not, then:

**eṟṟukku uṟangum poruḷ?**

Is there any use in lying down in your bed and not joining all of us?

Why are you delaying, without looking at our wishes, without looking at our anxiety, and without realizing the benefit of enjoying kaṇṇan?

In this pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is waking up a gōpikā whose brother is duty-bound in getting the milk from all the cows, and this gōpikā was thinking that perumān should come and see her as that is His nature. The gōpikās outside are desperate to go see Him, and since they want to go with her, they wake her up.  In this pāsuram it says that if one does the activities following their varṇāsrama darmam with the thought of doing it as a kainkaryam (instead of as a means), then there is no kuṟṟam (fault).

**Additional details relating this pāsuram to mudal āḻvārs**:

kaṟṟuk kaṟavai gaṇangaḷ pala – [mudal āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) gave lots of pāsurams made of small veṇbās (as named in tamiḻ grammar).

cenṟu ceruc ceyyum – they roamed around as yōgis;

kōvalar – who reached (tiru)kōvalur – [mudal āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/).  kuṟṟam onṟillāda – without any defects – [mudal āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) were born not from a womb; Of the three, poṟkodi is [bhūdatāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) – ‘*kōl tēdi ōdum kodi … māl tēdi ōdum manam*‘. For him the cuṟṟattu tōḻimār – related friends – [poigai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/), and [pēyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) (other āḻvars are friends);  algul – small waist – indicates vairākyam;  jñānam -\> [poigai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/); vairākyam -\> [bhūdattāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/); bhakti -\> [pēyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/);

mugil vaṇṇan – this phrase was first used by [bhūdatāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/);

ciṟṟādē pēsādē – [bhūdattāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) – did not cry out or complain in his pāsurams (unlike [poigai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) and [pēi āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/))

selvap peṇḍātti (dear wife) – [bhūdatāzvar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/10/22/mudhalazhwargal/) was born in the same flower as tāyār did;

**This pāsuram is said to fit** [**kūrattāḻvān**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) **as well. If anyone knows how, please provide in comments.**

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 12. Kanaittilam karrerumai

*kanaittu iḷam kaṟṟu erumai kanṟukku irangi ninaittu mulai vaḻiyē ninṟu pāl sōra nanaittu illam sēṟāakkum naṟcelvan tangāi panittalai vīḻa nin vāsal kadai paṟṟi sinattināl ten ilangaik kōmānaic ceṟṟa manattukku iniyānai pādavum nī vāi tiṟavāi inittān eḻundirāi īdenna pēr uṟakkam anaittu illattārum aṟindēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

iḷam kaṟṟu erumai – buffaloes having young ones; kanaittu – are making desperate calls (because no one is there to draw out the milk); kanṟukku irangi – and being kind to (its) little ones; ninaittu – they think of the little ones; mulai vaḻiyē ninṟu pāl sōra – (and because of such deep thinking , they automatically) pour out milk continuously from their nipples; illam nanaittu – making the whole house wet (with milk); sēṟu ākkum – and (due to mixing up) become slushy; naṟcelvan – (gōpan of such a buffalo; that gōpan is having – ) the best wealth that is kriṣṇa-kainkaryam; tangāi – (and you are) his sister!; nin vāsal kadai paṟṟi – (we are) holding the entrance beam of your (house); talai pani vīḻa – and the mist/snow is falling on our head; pādavum – even though (we) sang about; manattukku iniyānai – srī rāma pirān who is sweet to our hearts; sinattināl ceṟṟa – who killed (rāvaṇan) due to anger (that he separated pirātti); then ilangaik kōmānai – (that is) rāvaṇan the wealthy king of lankai; nī vāi tiṟavāi – you are not opening your mouth and talking; inittān – at least now; eḻundirāi – please get up; īdu enna pēr uṟakkam – what is this great sleep?; anaittu illattārum – all the people (of āyppādi); aṟindu – have known (about your big sleep)

**Перевод:**

**kanaittu**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram it talked about a kōvalan (gōpan) who was following his varṇāsramam duties by milking the numerous cows; in this pāsuram, this kōvalan is ignoring those duties, but it is justified when he is going behind perumān to help Him; to do kainkaryam to kriṣṇan.*

(Since the gōpan has gone to kriṣṇan), the buffalo, without getting milked, is finding it hard on its nipples and is crying out.

([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with so much of love towards us is not able to wait to help us.)

**iḷam kaṟṟu erumai**

the baby of this young buffalo is not able to drink milk by itself

(samsāris are not able to fully recognize [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s love.)

**kanṟukku irangi**

while its nipples are hurting, the cow is affectionately worrying about the state of its child.

([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is worried about our jñānam, bhakti, saraṇāgati so that we would reach Him in srī vaikuṇṭam.)

cakravarty tirumagan could not tolerate rāvaṇan hurting tiruvadi (hanuman). kriṣṇan could not tolerate the suffering of draupati. vaikuṇṭha nātan could not tolerate the state of all [jīvatmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s looking like acit during praḷayam, so He gave jñānam and equipment (body).

The gōpikās are saying, “Like how the buffalo would suffer if the covherd boy (kōvalan) does not get the milk out, we suffer because of your delay”.

**ninaittu mulai vaḻiyē**

just by looking at its calf, the mother is thinking that it is trying to drink the milk, so it is pouring out milk, even without the covherd boy using his hands;

**ninṟu pāl sōra**

Like the falls in tiruvēnkatam, this buffalo is continuously pouring out milk keeping its child in mind.

Why is this gōpan not milking the buffalo? He is going along with kriṣṇan and lending his shoulder for kriṣṇan to lean on. iḷaiya perumāḷ too went along with cakravarty tirumagan and did not worry about his kśatriya duties. These duties are enemies for their destiny.

**nanaittu illam sēṟu ākkum**

Because of excessive milk pouring out, it flooded the floor in the house, and it mixed with other dirt and has become mud. So it is difficult to get inside the house.

**naṟcelvan**
