not a temporary wealth, but strong and permanent wealth which is the darśan of kriṣṇan’s lotus feet;  kainkaryam is the permanent wealth for all [jīvatmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s; because of this covherd boy’s wealth (srīvaiṣṇavam), the whole world is getting the effect of it and are becoming srīvaiṣṇavas.

**tangāi**

As a sister of such a great srīvaiṣṇavan, since females would be more loving towards [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), the gōpikās outside are saying that she is even better than her brother in following srīvaiṣṇavam.

\[svāpadēsam: ācāryan becomes kind and lets the siśya and their children know about bhagavat guṇa anubhavam; and being thankful to ācāryan that he helped us, siśyan’s mind is fully on bhagavat guṇams through ācāryan’s blessings.\]

**panittalai vīḻa nin vāsal kadai paṟṟi**

With rain/fog pouring on our heads, and milk flowing from the mud near our feet, we are standing in front of your house holding the sides of your doors, like people standing on a float would dearly hold on to their support – this is our precarious situation that you are not understanding well.

panit talai vīḻa – [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) got மயர்வற மதிநலம் and from their [divya prabandam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2013/02/dhivya-prabhandha-pramanya-samarthanam-1.html) we get bhagavat guṇa anubhavam.

nin – ‘na-kaaram’ (of nama:) – we hold on (paṟṟi) to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)

Even after this, she was just relaxing inside in her bed; the gōpikās continue..

**sinattināl –** In order to wake up this gōpikā, they set aside kaṇṇan who sits in their hearts and who  troubles them endlessly, and now they talk about cakravarty tirumagan whom any gōpikā’s parents would be confident about.

rāvaṇan’s so many arrows on Him did not make Him upset, but when rāvaṇan sent one arrow hurting tiruvadi (hanuman) that is when cakravarty tirumagan got angry ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gets angry when someone hurts his devotees- bhāgavata apacāram) and started pouring his arrows on rāvaṇan. It was only that anger which killed rāvaṇan.

**then ilangai**

the place which houses the criminal who separated [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and [pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); as if not wanting to see in that direction, cakravarty tirumagan went to fight

**kōmānai**

as tiruvadi (hanuman) praised that place as wealthy. hanuman was glorified as great renunciant. Even he was amazed by the wealth of lankā.

**ceṟṟa**

Instead of just cutting off rāvaṇan with one arrow, he decimated the chariot, broke the enemies’ bows, and the army, ego and everything, and then the heads.

destroys our ahankāram

**manattukku iniyānai**

cakravarty tirumagan is sweet to our minds and hearts (unlike kriṣṇan who attracts us so much but does not show up, plays foul games, makes our hearts pain us due to His separation, who does not let out a single drop of tear (as against cakaravarty tirumagan who cries even for his enemies)).

He killed rāvaṇan and others, and we say He is sweet to our heart? He killed the enemies of bhāgavatas, so He is sweet to our hearts; He kills our *svātantryam*, so He becomes sweet to our hearts.

**pādavum nee vāi tiṟavāi**

“You said that we kept driveling kriṣṇan’s nāmam and troubled your heart, so we kept is names aside and are singing the sweet name of cakravarty tirumagan which would soothe our hearts – you are still not opening your mouth”

“As you are singing perumāḷ’s names I am relaxing and enjoying it. Why do I have to open my mouth?”

**inittān eḻundirāi**

You are still sleeping, even after we explained you our angst. Please get up.

**īdenna pēruṟakkam?**

You are sleeping so much, in yet another way, even different from [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s sleep when all the worlds slept, different from samsāris’ sleep that is out of tamō guṇam (laziness, etc.). Your sleep is so big that you make the sleep of the gōpikā of previous pāsuram (who got additional sleep from kumbakarṇan) look like a nap.

You are so close to Him – He gets up quickly upon seeing His devotees’ worries, but you are sleeping like this and not concerned about our worries?

samsāris’ sleep would end during the morning when there is more satva guṇam (tranquility/peacefulness); īsvaran’s sleep would end when seeing others’ troubles, because of His protectiveness towards the world;  your sleep would end when seeing eagerness of those having *carama parva niśtai* (living as per the last stage of being, that is bhāgavata kainkaryam); and since it is very difficult to see such people, your sleep is not ending?

**anaittu illattārum aṟindu**  (people of all houses in the neighborhood know (about your sleeping late))

“If you wanted all the people to know about our standing in front of your house to wake you up, then yes all the people now know about this, so please get up”

**==\>** Another meaning: [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) is saying to the gōpikā inside, “If you are enjoying bhagavat viśayam in secrecy (rahasyam), and if you think that nobody outside should know, well, the truth is that bhagavat viśayam is now known to everyone, and it has benefited everyone.”

**==\>**The gōpikā inside is like [tirukkōśtiyūr nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/). The gōpikā outside (āṇḍāḷ) who is saying that everyone knows about the rahasyam is like [emperumānār](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-emperumanar.html)! What the one inside was enjoying in secret, the one outside let everyone know. *\[As we know,* [*tirukkōśtiyūr nambi*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/)*, an ācāryan of* [*emperumānār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)*, gave the rahasyams to emperumānār but not before putting him through a lot of hardship and tests (that was the method used by* [*ācāryas*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) *of those days – ōrāṇ vaḻi); he instructed* [*emperumānār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) *to not tell the rahasyam to anyone without putting them through similar tests. But* [*emperumānār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) *called up the bhāgavatas and gave them all the meanings of the rahasyam he learned from* [*tirukkōstiyūr nambi*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) ***(so, anaittu illattārum arindār! (everyone came to know!).***

*(When* [*nambi*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi) *reminded him that he would go to narakam (hell) for not following ācāryan’s words,* [*emperumānār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) *replied that if all these people would go to srī vaikuṇṭam because of knowing the rahasyam through your blessing, then, I am just one person, I am ready to go to narakam.)*

*emperumānār then told his siśyas that if there are siśyas with samam and damam (control of inner and outer faculties), have got just the interest to learn, then that qualification is enough to teach them everything (krupa mātram).*

***{{***  
***Translator’s note:***  [*maṇavāḷa māmunigaḷ*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/) *writes about this in upadēsa ratinamālai:* *பாருலகில் ஆசையுடையோர்க்கெல்லாம் ஆரியர்காள் கூறுமென்று பேசி வரம்பறுத்தார் பின்.* *எம்பெருமானார் தரிசனம் என்றே இதற்கு நம்பெருமாள் பேரிட்டு நாட்டி வைத்தார்,* ***அம்புவியோர்*** *இந்தத் தரிசனைத்தை எம்பெருமானார் வளர்த்த அந்தச் செயல்* ***அறிகைக்கா.*** \[pārulagil āsai udaiyōrkkellām āriyargāḷ kūṟum enṟu pēsi vaṟambaṟuttār pin; emperumānār darisanam enṟē idarkku namperumāḷ pērittu nātti vaittār*,* ***ampuviyōr*** indad darisanattai emperumānār vaḷartta andac ceyal ***arigaikkā*****\]**

***So, āṇḍāḷ used the word: anaittillatārum; māmunigaḷ uses: ampuviyōr;***  
***āṇḍaḷ used: ‘aṟindu’, and*** [***maṇavāḷa māmunigaḷ***](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-mamunigal.html) ***uses: aṟigaikkā*** *\]*  
**}}**

**==\>** Another meaning: The gōpikā *inside* is like [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/). The gōpikā inside possesses the greatness that her buffalo pours milk by itself to its calf. [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/), by just the krupā (kindness/eagerness) gave all the meanings to all his siśyas – just poured out all good things to all who were interested.

==\> Another meaning: All the people are knowing bhagavat viśayam after [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) nāciār’s avatāram.

As a summary, In this pāsuram they wake up a gōpikā whose brother is not focused on his duties like getting the milk, but is always with kriṣṇan and lending his shoulder for kriṣṇan to lean on; the gōpikās want to wake up a sister of such a gōpan, and they sing about *manattukku iniyān* (sweet to think about) that is srī rāman, and they ask her to get up as the neighbors are also coming to know that she is still sleeping.  In aitīhyam, ‘*anaittillattārum aṟindu*‘ is related to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) telling the meanings of rahasyams to all interested srīvaiṣṇavas instead of restricting it to one at a time after testing them several times.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 13. Pullin vai kindanai

*puḷḷin vāi kīṇḍānai pollā arakkanai kiḷḷik kaḷaindānai kīrtimai pādippōi piḷḷaigaḷ ellārum pāvaikkaḷam pukkār veḷḷi eḻundu vyāḻam uṟangiṟṟu puḷḷum silambina kāṇ pōdarik kaṇṇināi! kuḷḷak kuḷiṟa kudaindu nīrādādē paḷḷik kidattiyō pāvāi nī nannāḷāl kaḷḷam tavirndu kalandēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

puḷḷin vāi kīndānai – (empirān who) tore and threw away the mouth of bakāsuran who came in the form of a bird; pollā arakkanaik kiḷḷik kaḷaindānai – who very easily/casually destroyed rāvaṇan who was bad deeds personified; kīrtimai pādip pōi – (we) go while singing of (such empirān’s) brave deeds; piḷḷaigaḷ ellārum – all the gōpikās; pukkār – entered; pāvaikkaḷam – (to) the place marked by kriṣṇan and us for nōnbu; veḷḷi eḻundu – sukran has risen, and; vyāḻam uṟangiṟṟu – guru has set; puḷḷum silambina kāṇ – (and) birds are spreading out (to look for prey); nī – you; pōdu arik kaṇṇināi – with the eyes like flower and deer; pāvāi – (and) naturally having women’s greatness; nal nāḷ – (we are going to be together with kriṣṇan) in this good day; kaḷḷam tavirndu – (so) avoid the ill will (of being alone with kriṣṇan); kalandu – (and) join (us); kuḷḷa kuḷira kudaindu nīrādādē – (you,) without taking bath well in the cold water (enjoying kriṣṇan); paḷḷik kidattiyō – sleeping in the bed (instead)?; Al – surprised (that we got such a good day)!

**Перевод:**

**puḷḷin vāi kīṇḍānai**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*This gōpikā is thinking that if her eyes are beautiful enough (if we have the svarūpa jñānam (that it is He who looks after us)), then kaṇṇan would automatically come to her. So she is just lying in her bed. She heard the gōpikās singing praise of cakravarty tirumagan in the previous tirumāḷigai, and so decides not to keep quiet and asks the gōpikās when they came to her door, about the rāma nāma sankīrtanam. They reply, yes we can sing about not only rāman, but also about our favorite kriṣṇan.*

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who killed the bird that was bakāsuran

For people who have surrendered to Him, He helps them get rid of their kāmam (lust) and other dōśams (ills) which come as hindrance to kainkaryam.

**pollā arakkanai**

Like the arakkan (rākśasa) who separated pirātti from [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); he is a bad arakkan, unlike the good one like vibhīśaṇan. Because he is so bad, they do not want to say his name in the pāsuram; [tirumangai mannan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) also said ‘*mun poḷa irāvaṇan*’ to say that his main defect is his pollāngu (bad deeds).

Our ahankāram;

**kiḷḷik kaḷaindānai**

Like how we snatch out the leaves affected by insects and leave the good ones in the tree, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) saved vibīśanāḻvān and snatched the head of pōllā arakkans like rāvaṇan.

pollā arakkanai (does not destroy the Good aspect of ahankāram, which is – when ‘aham’ (I), and ‘mama’ (mine) make us think that [īsvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) and His belongings are liked by us; srīvaiṣṇavas living with the realization that being subservient to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is their nature. [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would help us keep this good thing.

pollā – Bad, ahankāram – feeling of an independence from [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), feeling that we can decide about something on our own without considering what the scriptures say – He helps us by snatching away only such bad aspects without affecting the good aspects.

**kīrtimai** – greatness due to valor;

Enemies, and others who supported them, lost themselves to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – rāvaṇan for perumāḷ’s valor, his sister melted for His beauty, and his brother became the target of His kindness/greatness.

So it goes without saying that we His adiyārs would fall flat on His lotus feet.

**pādip pōi**

Thing to do while on our way to go to Him is to sing His names; they won’t go if they don’t sing; singing itself is their goal; muktan would be taking puṇḍarīkākśan’s divine names as the journey-food (food that is packed along for consumption during the journey), while going to paramapadam.

**piḷḷaigaḷ ellārum**

even those little gōpikās whom we have to wake up have already woken up and are on their way for the nōnbu

**pāvaik kaḷam pukkār**

to the place where we and kriṣṇan have marked to meet for doing nōnbu.

(bhāgavatas woke up early and went to the place of kālakśēpam).

“Is it dawn already for them to be going now?”

**veḷḷi eḻundu viyāḻam uṟangiṟṟu**

sukran (veḷḷi) has risen in the sky, and the bruhaspati (vyāḻam) is set.

(ajñānam fell and jñānam about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) rose)

“Any star you see, you would think it is veḷḷi rising and vyāḻam setting”, (because you are always thinking of a reason to start going for the nōnbu)

They reply, “not only that sign, …”

**puḷḷum silmabina, kāṇ**

All the birds too – they have not only woken up, but are also making noise as they have started going to find their food.

(remaining bhāgavatas have also gotten ready and gone to kālakśēpam)

**pōdari kaṇṇināi**

O, gōpikā, whose eyes are like a flower (podu: flower)

and like those of a deer (ari -\> deer);

ōr, like how a bee inside the center of a flower would look like. ( ari -\> bee)

ōr, your eyes are winning the competition against flowers on beauty.

We want kriṣṇan to go deep in to the water that is the beauty of your eyes, and seeing that, we want to go deep in to the water that is kaṇṇan’s beauty and your being with him (உமிழ் நீர் உண்ணல்).  (bhāgavata sambandam);

The gōpikā inside is a deer with beautiful long eyes; kriṣṇan is aravindalōcanan (lotus eyes) who owns the whole world; and we are the target of both your eyes.

(O the bhāgavatā who has the jñānam and bhakti to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/))

==\> [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) – In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is referring to [tiruppāṇāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/) by saying ‘*pōdarik kaṇṇināi*‘. He sang so much about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s eyes in his only prabandam of amalanādipirān: ‘*adiyārkku ennai At padutta vimalan*‘, ‘*en kaṇṇin uḷḷana*‘, ‘*koṇḍal vaṇṇanai kōvalanāi … en amudinaikkaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ*‘… so [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is calling him ‘pōdarikkaṇṇināi’. He sang ‘*catura māmadiḷ sūḻ ilangaikku iṟaivan talai pattu udira ōtti ōr vengaṇai uyttavan*‘, and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is saying here, ‘*pōllā arakkanai kiḷḷi-k kaḷaindānai*‘.

**kuḷḷakkuḷira**

before the sun rises and water becomes warm

**kudaindu nīrādādē**

without bathing to reduce our tāpam for not being able to see kaṇṇan, and making sure we will never miss Him, and that we would go and enjoy His anubhavam, you are still sleeping;

(without entering the place of kālakśēpam and enjoyng bhagavat anubhavam along with other bhāgavatas)

**paḷḷik kidattiyō**

you are lying down in the bed – you are just enjoying the fragrance of the bed that had been touched by kriṣṇan’s tirumēni (divine form), instead of going to kriṣṇan – this is like collecting and eating a few grains when there is so much of them available grown in the paddies.

(doing bhagavat anubhavam staying alone?)

**pāvāi nī**

you are not of the nature to be alone, (you always want to join the other bhāgavatas and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) for kainkaryam).

**nan nāḷāl**

The future from today are good days, unlike the rāvaṇan days where there was separation; we don’t know what the elders will say after this month – we got a good month to be with kriṣṇan, but you are smelling the bed lying down;

**aitīhyam -\>** for vaikunta ēkādasi, [parāsara bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html)’s tiruttāyār (mother) āṇḍāḷ had done upavāsam (fasting), and next day she asked [bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html) for tīrtam; since vaikunta ēkādasi comes during the great days of pagal pattu and rāppattu utsavams, [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) asked his mother, ‘why did you choose *this* ēkādasi for doing fasting?’ (this means, when one needs strength to enjoy the utsavams, this ēkādasi is coming in between the utsavams; or it means, when during utsavam we should be thinking of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) all the time, where was the time to think of food and do upavāsam to avoid the food? \[explanation is taken from śri u.vē. vēnkatakriṣṇan swami’s “vāḻvum vākkum”, item 88.\]

**kaḷḷam tavirndu kalandu**

kaḷḷam – enjoying alone, thinking about kriṣṇan’s mischief

tavirndu – do not do Kaḷḷam,

kalandu – come and join us

Not showing us your face is like stealing (kaḷḷam) the jīvātmā from paramātmā.

Cannot tolerate the feeling of svātantryam (feeling that we are independent of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and can decide right or wrong about things without considering what He has given in vēdas).

(read also as: kalandu kuḷḷakkuḷira nīrādādē paḷḷikkidattiyō?)

In this pāsuram āṇḍāḷ is waking up a gōpikā who has got beautiful eyes (jñānam), to sing about the great deeds of kriṣṇan, and calling her to go and bathe in cold water to reduce their angst of missing kriṣṇan (join with kriṣṇan), asking her to join quickly. bhāgavatas go to kālakśēpams early and quickly to gain jñānam, and get anubhavam.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 14. Ungal pulaikkadai

*ungaḷ puḻaikkadait tōttattu vāviyuḷ sengaḻu nīr vāi negiḻndu āmbal vāi kūmbina kāṇ sengal podik kūṟai vēṇ pal tavattavar tangaḷ tirukkōyil sangiduvān pōginṟār engaḷai munnam eḻuppuvān vāi pēsum nangāi! eḻundirāi nāṇādāi! nāvudaiyāy! sangodu cakkaram ēndum tadakkaiyan pangayak kaṇṇānaip pādēlōrempāvāi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

engaḷai munnam eḻuppuvān – would wake us (who are your friends) up first; vāi pēsum – mentioned so with your mouth (in passing); nangāi – who is full of good qualities; nāṇādāi – not feeling guilty (that you did not wake us up as promised); nā udaiyāi – having a tongue (that says sweet words); ungaḷ puḻaik kadai tōttattu vāviyuḷ – in the pond that is in the back of your house; sengaḻunīr vāi negiḻndu – lotus flowers have blossomed; āmbal vāi kūmbina kāṇ – āmbal flowers have closed down; see!; sengal podik kūṟai – (also) one with clothes brick in color; veṇ pal – and having white teeth; tavattavar – having the get up of sanyāsis who do tapas; pōginṟār – are going; sangu iduvān – to sound the konc; tangaḷ tirukkōyil – in their temples; pangayak kaṇṇānai – sarvēsvaran having lotus eyes; sangodu cakkaram – and conch and the wheel (sudarsanar); ēndum – (which he) easily holds in both the hands; tadak kaiyan – (he) having big hands; eḻundirāi – please get up; pāda – to sing (about him, as a conduit of our love)

**Перевод:**

**ungaḷ puḻaikkadai ….**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The one who was to organize everything about nōnbu, and who had talked about doing all these wake-ups, is the one being woken up in this pāsuram. This gōpikā would connect all the bhāgavatas with emperumān; full in jñāna, bhakti, vairākyam.*

“The red lotus flowers have blossomed, and the āmbal flowers have closed themselves (as it has become morning), and you are still sleeping?”

“Did you go to the fields?”

“No, we saw this in the backyard”

“You would have forced those flowers”

**ungaḷ puḻaikkadai tōttattu vāviyuḷ sengaḻu nīr vāi nēgiḻndu āmbal vāi kūmbina**

“No, we are talking about the flowers in **your** **own** backyard, the backyard to which we are not able to get into (because the entrance is still closed, and you are still sleeping)”

They are saying particularly in her backyard, to say that it is she who is still sleeping. Not because of sunlight, but because it has become morning.

“When it is just about to open up their buds, you are saying they are blossoming fully. Is there any other sign for dawn?”  (In this pāsuram, the day has progressed even further compared to that in previous pāsurams).

In the nama: that is in the middle of tirumantram, pāratantriyam became pronounced, and svātantriyam diminished/went away.

**sengal podik kūṟai veṇ pal tavattavar tangaḷ tirukkōil sangiduvān poginrār**

The sanyāsis wearing clothes colored with sengal (brick color) powder, and with clean/white teeth of brahmacāris, are going to their temples;  sanyāsis have finished their [sandyāvandanam](https://pillai.koyil.org/?page_id=695) and are getting ready to do [tiruvārādanam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html), the day has progressed that much.

[*embār*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/07/embar/) *nirvāham*: even the people with tāmasa guṇam (laziness, etc.,), who use sangu (conch) for pouring water on their god have woken up; you with satva guṇam have not yet woken up?

[anantāṇpillai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/ananthazhwan/) who volunteered to go to tiruvēnkatam to do puśpa (flower) kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gives the meaning as: ‘jīyar swami is already climbing up the tirumalā mountain steps to open the temple (sangu also can mean key (for opening the door to the temple)).

“those who are always thinking about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) might do [tiruvārādanam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html) even before dawn; what the ones having tāmasam do, can be a pramāṇam? So can it be a proof that it is already dawn”

**engaḷai munnam eḻuppuvān**

“Your sleeping here is also not a proof for your words yesterday that you will get up first and wake up all of us. *எல்லாரையும் நானேயெழுப்புகிறேனென்று சொன்னது உன்பக்கல் கண்டிலோமீ !*”

**vāi pēsum**

They continue, “You are talking, but not implementing. You are a close friend of kriṣṇan – telling lies must be nothing new to you.”

Even before we ask you, you would make us jñānis by telling us bhagavat related special meanings.

**nangāi**

We see how you are following your words that you will wake up all of us, that you won’t go without us, and so on. Would you do at least some of what you said?

**eḻundirāi**

Please get us to save us from this (delay)

**nāṇādāi**

Are you not feeling anything about not following your plans?

Pumpkin’s skin has a certain roughness called suṇai, without which it does not grow; ‘suṇai’ also means being ashamed (for not keeping the word here). So [periyavācān piḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/periyavachan-pillai-english/) says ‘does your place not grow pumpkins?’.

(ācāryan does not shy away from explaining us the meanings)

**nāvudaiyāy**

“Why are you coming here even when you are upset?”

“To listen to your sweet voice and speech; how much ever hardship you give us, your speech mesmerizes us”.

(ācāryan with his jñānam corrects us into right path)

“What do you want to do now?”

**sangodu cakkaram ēndum tadakkaiyan pangayak kaṇṇānai**

Because of carrying cangu and cakram, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s hands grow and appear beautiful; his lotus eyes are so beautiful they have grown up to the cangam and cakram in his hands; young gōpikās fall for his beautiful eyes; people who see the cangam and cakram which are like sun and moon, along with his eyes which are opening up like lotus flowers would become mesmerized. First kriṣṇan becomes dāsan of gōpikās, and then with his eyes attracts them and makes them His dāsar. Does **kriṣṇan** have those in his hands? Yes, but it would be visible only to some; He uses them to scare the young gōpikās while playing with them. ([emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) asked whether [embār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/07/embar/) was present in the back of the gōśti, when embār showed the action of cangu cakram to araiyar when araiyar was showing a different action for kriṣṇan scaring the young gōpikās, and then corrected it based on [embār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/07/embar/)‘s sign). kriṣṇan showed them even at the time of birth to his parents in jail – they pleaded him to hide them, fearing for his safety.

**pāda**

sing kaṇṇan so that we who are like dry land would get the rain that is kaṇṇan’s nāmams.

svāpadēsam: Singing tiruvāimoḻi is the benefit by itself (please get up to do that)

**ēlōrempāvāi** – think well and follow us.

[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) ==\> This pāsuram matches with [tiruppāṇāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/). puḻakkadai – ‘backyard’ to srīrangam is uṟaiyūr where he was born;  tirukkōyil sangiduvān – for [tiruvārādanam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-thiruvaaraadhanam.html), veṇ pal tavattavar – lōka sāranga muni; munnam vāi pēsi – this [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/) talked about bhāgavata sēśatvam first – adiyārkkennai ātpadutta; since muni wished for [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/) to be in his shoulders and he agreed, even that is a kainkaryam of [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/) to the adiyār; (lōka sāranga muni is seen in the tiruvadi of [tiruppāṇāḻva](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/21/thiruppanazhwar/)r in the latter’s sannidi at srīrangam).

In this pāsuram, āṇḍāḷ wakes up an adiyār whom the gōpikās want to follow to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), but who is having kriṣṇa anubhavam and so not getting up from bed; they ask her to go with them so that their anubhavam would be fulfilling. They want to sing about the [puṇḍarīkākśan](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), who is holding majestic cangu and cakkaratāḻvars.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 15. Elle ilam kiliye

*ellē iḷam kiḷiyē! innam uṟangudiyō? cil enṟu aḻaiyēnmin nangaimīr pōdarginṟēn vallai un katturaigaḷ paṇḍē un vāi aṟidum valleergaḷ nīngaḷē nānē tān āyiduga ollai nī pōdāi unakkēnna vēṟudaiyai ellārum pōndarō pōndār pōndu eṇṇikkoḷ val ānai konṟānai māṟṟārai māṟṟaḻikka vallānai māyanaip pāda ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

iḷam kiḷiyē – O gōpikā, who looks like a young parrot (in speech and looks); ellē – wow! (how sweet is your speech!); innam – even after everyone has come and stood here; uṟangudiyō – you are still sleeping? (and so they try to wake her up); nangaimīr – (gōpikā from inside says) – dear girls!; sil enṟu aḻaiyēnmin – please talk without fighting (with me); pōdarginrēn – I will start (now itself); vallai – (from outside:) you are smart (in talks); paṇḍē aṟidum – we know for quite long; un katturaigaḷ – about your strong words; un vāi – and about your taking in length (but no action); nīngaḷē valleergaḷ – (from inside:) – it is you who can talk strongly/rudely (as you are arguing with me); nānē tān āyiduga – (then truly accepts the fault as hers) – ok, I shall be the one who is talking strongly/rudely; nee – (from outside:) you; ollai pōdāi – get up quickly; unakku enna vēṟu udaiyai – what separate goals/plans are you having?; ellārum pōndārō – (from inside:) – has everyone come (who are supposed to come)?; pōndār – (from outside:) – they have come; pōndu eṇṇikkoḷ – you come out and count them; pāda – (from outside:) – to sing about; konṟānai – the one who destroyed; vallānai – the strong elephant (kuvalayāpīdam); māṟṟārai māṟṟu aḻikka vallānai – and who can destroy the strength of enemies; māyanai – that is, kaṇṇan, who does wonderful actions

**Перевод:**

**ellē iḷam kiḷiyē**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Note: To get the deepest meanings of especially this pāsuram kindly attend kālakśēpams, and from your ācāryan).*

When the gōpikās asked the gōpikā of previous pāsuram to sing about kaṇṇan who has got beautiful eyes and who carries cangu and cakram in His beautiful hands, the gōpikā in this pāsuram heard that while lying down in her bed, and she started humming that song.

That sweet sound reached the ears of these gōpikās, and they came to this house and now they are saying “ellē! iḷam kiḷiyē!”

kiḷiyē – parrot’s sweet voice is like that of this gōpikā! (it cannot match this gōpikā’s young beauty);

also: we are kiḷi, you are iḷam kiḷi;

ellē! – Wow! (they are wowed by the sweetness of her voice)

ellē! – calling her; Like how the people from south like vānamāmalai divya dēsam would address a girl, ‘yēdi’.

The gōpikā inside thought that they are scolding her as ‘while our lips and body are pale due to separation from kriṣṇan, you are like a parrot with green/fresh body and red lips, thinking of kriṣṇan?’. So she kept mum.

**innam uṟangudiyō**

Their calling her is disturbing her singing, so she is not responding to them. So, they are asking, “Are you still sleeping!”

Due to missing kriṣṇan we are all tired and – sleepless in āyppādi – and standing here waiting for your blessing, but you are not giving even that?

When we have got the best opportunity to get kriṣṇa anubhavam (which we might lose in a few days as our nōnbu would end), can you be sleeping?

When we are going to get what we wanted, would anyone sleep like this?

**sillenṟu aḻaiyēnmin**

These words are from the gōpikā inside.

Their calling her is disturbing her singing the nāmams, so she is saying, ‘Please do not address me like this’.

Can their calling her be a disturbance? When [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s siśyas were having tiruvāimoḻi kālakśēpam, the selvar came with his vādya gōśti which disturbed the kalakśēpam – similar to that, this gōpikā is feeling disturbed.

**nangaimīr pōdarginrēn**

When they asked, ‘what is it with you, ignoring us and our words?’, she felt ashamed and wanted to join them quickly. She replied,

“If you can keep your mouth closed, I will start now.”

**vallai un katturaigaḷ paṇḍē un vāi aridum**

“You are curt/rude (vallai) – you are calling us ‘nangaimīr’ to imply some distance between you and us (so, they are harsh words). We know for long that you have the ability to talk like this (katturai) and put the fault on us. You want to have kriṣṇa anubhavam by yourself inside the house”, say the gōpikās waiting outside.

**valleergaḷ nīngaḷē**

The gōpikā inside the house at first replies, “valleergaḷ nīngaḷē (you gōpikās are the ones who are rude/curt. You first said, ‘are you still sleeping?’, ‘you talk smart’, etc., so you are ones who are being harsh with their words.”

“you said that you will join once we closed our mouths – is it not your mistake to say that?”

“no, it is not. instead of feeling happy that the gōpikā who is joyful to hear our voice is now so involved in singing about kriṣṇan, you gōpikās found fault with me – so the mistake is yours”.

(Till now even though it sounds like they are arguing, it is that the gōpikās outside think highly of the one inside, that she is having great kriṣṇa anubhavam, singing about Him, etc. And, the gōpikā inside is also thinking highly of the ones outside as to their enthusiasm to go and be with kriṣṇan.)

The gōpika inside says – valleergaḷ nīngaḷē –  I too have come to your doors and got words from you earlier; they reply – that was past; see the pratyakśam (what is seen/now, we are here), instead of anumānam (what can be guessed/ what happened long ago).  (that is how those belonging *naiyāyika* matam argue – they focus on anumānam and not pratyakśam – when they see an elephant they would not say anything; but after it has left, they would look at its foot steps, etc., and say there was an elephant here).

Even though the gōpikā **inside** said this, because the nature of a srīvaiṣṇavar is to accept mistakes (even if someone assigns that on the srīvaiṣṇavar when there is none), \[paraspara nīca-bhāvai: – every srīvaiṣṇava to have the attitude that we are of lower stature than other srīvaiśnavas\], the gōpikā inside says …

**nānē tān āyiduga**

“Yes, (sorry), I am the one who is vallai’.

bharatāḻvān too said that it is because of his own pāpam that perumāḷ had to go to forest, and did not assign blame on those who created the problem.

*இல்லாத குற்றத்தையும் சிலர் “உண்டு” என்றால், இல்லை செய்யாதே இசைகையிறே வைஷ்ணவ லக்ஷணம்.* (*illāda kuṟṟattaiyum silar “uṇḍu” enṟāl, illai seiyādē isaigaiyiṟē vaiṣṇava-lakśaṇam.*)  
That is, even if someone assigns a non-committed mistake/fault on us, truly accepting it (even in one’s own mind) without denying is the identification of a srīvaiṣṇava; this is the meaning given in vēdas.

[nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/) came and waited at tunnu pugaḻ kandādai tōḻappar’s tirumāḷigai, to apologize, when in fact it was tōḻappar who was ready to go to nampiḷḷai’s tirumāḷigai to apologize. And [nampiḷḷa](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/)i got the famous name of ‘ulagāriyan’ \[*என்ன உலகாரியனோ*!  O! you are the ācāryan for the whole world!\] {and we got the famous name in the great ācāryar – [piḷḷai ulagāriyan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/18/pillai-lokacharyar/) ([piḷḷai lōkācaryar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/18/pillai-lokacharyar/)) as the name was given to him by his father [vadakkut tiruvīdip piḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/17/vadakku-thiruveedhi-pillai-english/) because of his love for his ācāryan [nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/)).

[parāsara bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) was happy when a (newly appointed) kovil kuṟattu maṇiyar (manager of some aspects of the temple)  scolded [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) without knowing about him, when [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) entered the sannidi without any restrictions as usual (as he is the son of [perumāḷ tāyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) themselves). [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) was very happy at this scolding, and he was thankful to that maṇiyam for saying the faults in him which he himself was supposed to think about everyday.

This is followed by what is said in srīvacana bhūśaṇam – *kuṟṟam seidavargaḷ pakkal poṟaiyum, krupaiyum, sirippum, ugappum, upakāra smrutiyum nadakka vēṇum – குற்றம் செய்தவர்கள் பக்கல் பொறையும், க்ருபையும், சிரிப்பும், உகப்பும், உபகாரஸ்ம்ருதியும் நடக்க வேணும்* – towards those who did (the mistake of) finding fault in us, we should show patience (poṟai), be kind (krupai) to think that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) might count his faulting us as his apacāram, laughter (sirippu) because they scolded our body which is our enemy; happiness (ugappu) because due to the scolding our pāpa karmā would reduce, and so be thankful (upakāra smruti) to them.

*If the svarūpam is bhāgavata sēśatvam, then accepting the fault they assign as our own is also part of that svarūpam.*

Other side of the coin is that we should not expect/pressurize vidvāns/srīvaiṣṇavās to talk about our positive aspects.

The gōpikā thus showed this great attitude of a srīvaiṣṇava in anuśtānam (doing it in action);

She  continues, “what do you want do now?”

**ollai nī pōdāi**

Since we cannot be without you even for one second, quickly join our crowd.

Leave the kriṣṇan in your bed as is (as she is doing kriṣṇa anubhavam), and you come and join us.

For the gōpikā too this crowd is what is needed for going to kriṣṇan.

**unakkenna vēṟudaiyai**

Do you have anything to enjoy alone, like cooking and eating alone,  that is not available to enjoy for the gōpikās from 5 lakhs houses of tiruvāyppādi? \[***Note:***6000-padi adds: adāvadu madyama padamum mūnrām padamum *– if anyone could give contextual explanation, please add it in the comments section*\] *.*

Goal is to be included in sādu gōśti (aruḷiceyal gōśti).

There is no difference between dēvatāntaram (vorśipping someone other than perumāḷ), and any perumāḷ viśayam that excludes other srivaiṣṇavas; and no difference between going after viśayam (materialistic), and any perumāḷ viśayam that avoids other srīvaiṣṇavas.

**==\>** We think of [kūrattāḻvan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/)‘s words in the following incident – the kirumikaṇḍa cōḻa king had ordered that [emperumānār,](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) and anyone associated with him, should not be allowed inside kōyil (srīrangam). Not aware of this, when [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s disciple [kūrattaḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) was about to enter the temple, he was stopped by a guard and was told “You cannot enter the temple because you are one of [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s people”. But then another guard said that [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) is parama bhaktan of perumāḷ, he is not an enemy to anyone, he is a good person, so it is okay to allow him inside the temple (“*நீ ஒருவர்க்கும் விரோதியல்லை; நீ புக்குத் திருவடி தொழு*“). When [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) understood king’s order, he returned without going into the temple saying “*எம்பெருமானார் சம்பந்தமே போரும்* – [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s association is enough for me. (ātma guṇam helps in doing ācārya kainkaryam; if that guṇam is used for separating me from ācāryan, then there is no use of that.)

**==\>** There was one subrahmaṇya bhattar who was very fond of [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) and his gōśti, and he worked for the cōḻa king. When there was a flood that was going to enter the temple premises, [kūra nārāyaṇa jīyar](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2013/12/30/kura-narayana-jiyar/) redirected the water – this affected a neighboring village, cintāmani. So the villagers were arguing about it with the srivaiṣṇavas. When subrahmanya bhattar was asked by the king to go as a mediator to solve this issue, he refused: “If I went as a mediator then I would have to be mentally separated from the two groups, which I cannot do because I don’t want to be separated from srīvaiṣṇavas even for half a second”.

So, no separate plans/goals that separates from other srīvaiṣṇavas.

No separate path from what our pūrvācāryās have followed; not follow own mind/ideas/opinions. (*munnōr moḻinda muṟai tappāmal kēttu pinnōrndu; முன்னோர் மொழிந்த முறை தப்பாமல் கேட்டு பின்னோர்ந்து*).

The gōpikās continue, “So immediately join us to pray the lotus feet”.

**ellārum pōndārō**

When bharatāḻvān, accompanied by whole of ayōdyā, came to get perumāḷ (srī rāman) back to ayōdyā, iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇan) mistaking that bharatan was coming to kill them, had started preparing the bow and arrows; perumāḷ did not like this, and told him “bharatan is not interested in ruling the kingdom; if you are interested, then I will get it for you (from bharatan, and keep him here with me); iḷaiyaperumāḷ bowed his head down and calmed down – in the same way when gōpikās asked whether she is having her own plans, she bowed down her head, and immediately replied, ‘ellārum pōndāro?’

“Do I have any other happiness than joining you? I was just waiting for all the smaller gōpikās also to wake up. Have all the gōpikās come here?”.

**pōndār**

“yes all of them, who are tired due to separation from kriṣṇan, are here in your door step”

“you are saying this just to get me out of the bed. If they are here, why don’t you send them all inside?”

**pōndu eṇṇikkoḷ**

**“**You show your face to us and take attendance of them (we cannot go even if one bhāgavata has not yet joined); the benefit of that would be that you would touch and count all of us, (koḷ) hug each and every one of us”.

“What would we all do if we get together?”

**val ānai konṟānai**

The one who killed kuvalayāpīdam and gave the life, that is, Himself, to us;  kamsan had set up an elephant near the entrance and was waiting to hear that the little boy was killed, and was ready to behave sad as the boy’s uncle; but the boy killed the elephant and made our life and His own life to be meaningful.

like how He killed kuvalayāpīdam there, He killed our characteristics like shyness and made us take up nōnbu (so that we could go see Him).

Like ‘venjina vēḻam (elephant) maruppositha vēndar kol, endiḻaiyār (gōpikās) manattai tanjudaiyāḷar kol’ (periya tirumoḻi 9-2-6).

(“*ōr ānai kāttu, ōr ānai konṟu*” (saved one elephant, killed one elephant) in tirucandaviruttam talks about gajēndrāḻvān and kuvalayāpīdam).

**māṟṟārai māṟṟaḻikka vallānai**

who killed without any effort kamsan and his rākśasās who came in front of Him, and saved all the gōpikās who were afraid of kaṇṇan’s safety,

Similar to that, He changed the minds of gōpars who had prevented Him from seeing His gōpikās.

Unlike caravarty tirumagan who said ‘inṟu pōi nāḷai vā (go back today and come tomorrow (for the war)’, kriṣṇan did to kamsan and his group what they wanted to do to Him, and removed the chains from His parents.

([rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) and other [ācaryās](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) who killed the objections (māṟṟu aḻikka) of other schools and enabled those people to join him – ([aruḷāḷaperumāḷ emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/11/28/arulala-perumal-emperumanar/), and others)).

**māyanai**

what kamasan et al undergo in His hands, He undergoes in His gōpikās’ hands, losing Himself to them –  māyan

**pāda**

Sing such a wonderful kriṣṇan’s names; sing after how we are lost to the wonder of His losing to us. Like how those who lost (arakkar) sang ‘*ēttuginṟōm nā taḻumba, rāman tiru nāmam* \[periya tirumoḻi\]’. This is the place where losing became victory. Please sing with your sweet voice.

Sing about Him who is hard to reach by our own efforts, who can remove our ahankāram, kāmam, krōdam, etc., and who is dear to His devotees.

Singing about Him makes other srīvaiṣṇavas happy, and that is our kainkaryam to them.

– – In pāsuram 5, *vāyināl pādi* – singing kriṣṇan’s avataram.  
– – In 7: *kēsavanaip pāda*;  – – in 27: *pādip paṟai koṇḍu*; and in – – 13: *kīrtimai pādi* – these phrases sing about his valor, getting rid of the enemies of bhāgavatas.  
– –  In 11: *mugil vaṇṇan pēr pāda* – sings about how all the people lose to His beauty.  
– – In 14: *cangodu cakkaram ēndum tadakkaiyan pangayak kaṇṇānaip pāda* – sings about how we are lost to his divya cangu cakrāyudam and the beautiful parts of tirumēni  
– –  and, in this pāsuram, they want to sing how kaṇṇan without any weapons in his hands won kamsan et al, and how without any weapons He lost himself to gōpikās.

=\> For the bhāgavatas who have come together, ‘*uṇṇum sōṟu, parugu nīr, tinnum veṟṟilai – ellāmum kaṇṇan*‘. (food, water, enjoyment is all kaṇṇan).

[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) ==\> In this last wake-up pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is calling  [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) as *ellē iḷam kiḷiyē*. ‘*iḷam*‘-\> young -\> [tirumangai aḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is the youngest of [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/);   [tirumangai mannan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) sang in his periya tirumoḻi ‘*adigaḷ adiyē ninaiyum adiyavargaḷ tam adiyān / அடிகளடியே நினையும் அடியவர்கள் தம்மடியான்’* where he wanted to be like this gōpikā that is adiyārs’ adiyār. He sang about himself pretending to be a gōpikā, ‘*men kiḷi pōl mig mizahṟṟum*‘, ‘*paṇ miḷirak kiḷi pōl miḻaṟṟi nadandu*‘) (‘*மென்கிளிபோல் மிகமிழற்றும்’, ‘பண் மிளிரக்கிளிபோல் மிழற்றி நடந்து*‘), and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is also saying ‘*ellē! iḷam kiḷiyē*‘. His words ‘*kari muninda kaittalamum / கரி முனிந்த கைத்தலமும்*‘ is available here as ‘*val ānai konṟānai / வல்லானை கொன்றானை* ‘.

(Following are [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) viśayam from srī U.Ve. M.A. vēnkatakriṣṇan swami’s tiruppāvai upanyāsam)

– parrot would repeat words – [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) in his prabandams gave the six angams (parts) related to what [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) gave us, about 4 vēdās;

Now an interesting conversation between āṇḍāḷ and [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/):

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) to [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) – innam urangudiyō – you who does srīvaṣṇava tadiyārādanam, are still sleeping?

[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) to [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) – sillenṟu aḻaiyēnmin

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) to [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) – paṇḍē aṟidum –  we know about you even before your avatāram (through this pāsuram!);

un katturaigaḷ – [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is known for strong words like ‘*vāḻndē pōm nīrē / வாழ்ந்தே போம் நீரே*‘, etc.

[āḻvar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) to [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) – valleergaḷ nīngaḷē – it is āṇḍāḷ who used strong words in nāciyār tirumoḻi, that if she does not get kriṣṇan then she will pull her breasts out and throw away at His chest.

[tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) – nānē tān āyiduga

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) – unakkenna vērudaiyai – whereas [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) only *said* that he would do madal,  [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) actually went ahead and performed madal twice (siriya/periya tirumadal).

val ānai konṟānai – [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/)‘s elephant is ‘*kadamā kaḷiṟu*‘; kaliyan is capable of controlling the elephant;

māṟṟarai māṟṟaḻikka vallānai – [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) can fight and win his enemies;

māyan – surprising aspects of [tirumangai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) – given in ‘*prapannāmrutam*‘ – when a ship came to shore with beetle nut (pākku), [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) asked for ‘1’ pākku – he took half of it, and gave the other half to the captain; He asked the captain to give in writing that half of it belongs to [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/). Then after reaching the other shore, he claimed that half of the load of the ship was his, and argued and got it, and used that amount for kainkaryam by doing gold plating for tiruvarangan!

parāsara bhattar mentions in his srīranga stavam about [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) getting gold from a budist temple to do kainkaryam to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – so tirumangai [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) is also māyan like [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). e[mperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is also like [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) in his valor – *viṟperu viḻavum kanjanum mallum… விற்பெரு விழவும் கஞ்சனும் மல்லும்…*

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/), in this key pāsuram, tells us about srīvaiṣṇava lakśaṇam, and has set this pāsuram and these words in the middle part of tiruppāvai to shows its significance.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 16. Nayaganai ninra

*nāyaganāi ninṟa nandagōpanudaiya kōyil kāppānē kodit tōnṟum tōraṇa vā(sa)yil kāppānē maṇikkadavam tāḷ tiṟavāi āyar siṟumiyarōmukku aṟai paṟai māyan maṇivaṇṇan nennalē vāi nērndān tūyōmāi vandōm tuyil eḻappāduvān vāyāl munnam munnam maṟṟādē ammā nī nē(sa)ya nilaikkadavam nīkku ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāppānē – You the protector of; kōyil – tirumāḷigai; nāyaganāi ninṟa nandagōpanudaiya – of nandagōpar who is (our) svāmi; tiṟavāi – open; tāḷ – the bolt; kadavam – (of the) door; maṇi – having gem stones embedded; āyar siṟumiyarōmukku – for us small gōpikās; māyan – kaṇṇapirān with admirable deeds; maṇivaṇṇan – whose color is like that of blue diamond; aṟai paṟai nennalē vāi nērndān – promised yesterday itself that He would give paṟai instrument that makes sound; tūyōmāi vandōm – we came with the pure mind; tuyil eḻap pāduvān – to sing so that He would wake up from His sleep; ammā – svāmi!; vāyāl munnam munnam māṟṟādē – do not refuse with your first words; nī – only you; nīkku – (should) open the; nēya nilaik kadavam – door which has got the disposition of having love for kaṇṇan

**Перевод:**

**nāyaganāi ninṟa nanda gōpanudaiya kōyil kāppānē  (O the one who is protecting the house of nandagōpan who is our nāyagan)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*This is also a central / main pāsuram of tiruppāvai (pāsuram also starts with ‘nāyagan‘..(central))*

They refer to srī nandagōpa as nāyagan – Because he is a helper, ācāryan – helping the gōpikās to get kriṣṇan.

kōyil kāppānē – O the one who is protecting the tirumāḷigai – They are saying that more than the main protector, the ones who are protecting (his) entrance are their protectors.

[bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) told his siśyar [nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/) – ‘think of those as your protectors who think [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as their protector’. (also) ‘live with the thought that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is your protector; since I taught you this, live with the thought that I (ācāryan) am your protector’.

nandagōpanudaiya kōyil –  The one we have surrendered to is paratantran (serves His adiyārs; serves nandagōpan); He took his avatāram to come to this place and enjoy the company of His adiyārs who agree that He would be their paratantran ; in srīvakuṇṭam too He is a paratantran, but the ones there don’t agree to that, so here He is looking for His adiyārs to tell Him what to do. Actually in srīvaikuṇṭam too, hH grows under the stick (scepter) of [srī sēnāpati āḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/senai-mudhaliar/), in the lap of tiruvanantāḻvān, and under the feathers of tiruvadi (garudāḻvān).

kōyil kāppānē – as they are very little girls, they are calling him by the work he is doing; that person also enjoys being addressed like that so they are using these words; whatever name would indicate devotees’ progress of subservience, that is the name that [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) enjoys.

It is also considering him equivalent to a bhāgavata. *பாகவதனை முன்னிட்டுக் கொண்டு பெருமாளைப் பற்ற வேண்டும்*.

kōyil – the place where He resides; He resides also in tirumantram; kōyil kāppānē – Oh the protector of tirumantram from anadikāris (those who do not have inclination/qualification to learn tirumantram).

==\> [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/). This pāsuram is addressing [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/).

nāyagan -\> [udaiyavar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) holds the central place of [ācārya guru paramparai](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/).

nanda gōpan -\> [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) protected us cows.

udaiya -\> ‘udaiyavar’ (holds control of both the vibhūtis of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) as bestowed by Him to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)).

‘kōyil kāppānē’ -\> protected kōyil (srīrangam) and many other temples; [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) organized all the activities of many temples, how and who should do what and when, and so on, which are followed even today. Because of him, our *sampradāyam* and the *temples* have been *protected*.

**kōdit tōnṟum tōraṇa vāsal kāppānē**

The guard of the temple gives his permission signalling with his eyes, and the gōpikās proceed to entrance door and wake up the guard of the entrance.

Thinking “someone might stop our goal!”, they are falling on everyone’s feet. They would pray even to other dēvatais to save kaṇṇan – vouldn’t they go pray to the guard who is *fond* of kaṇṇan?

kodit tōnṟum – since his tirumāḷigai looks similar to others’, nandagōpa has hung decorative flags in front of his tirumāḷigai. He did that also to make sure that if gōpikās came looking for kaṇṇan in the middle of the night, they would be able to identify his house without any problems;

Those who arrange for water booth would erect tall poles with flags so that people coming from afar could see this sign from a distance and feel better and rush to them for water. nandagōpa has erected these decorations for us to come here and enjoy kaṇṇan. (so please open the door).

vāsal kāppānē – kodi and tōraṇam are insentient things ([acētanam](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/)), they cannot ask us to proceed. So you please let us inside.

Once, kaṇṇan had told the guards to allow arjunan to take away subhadrā; to you also kaṇṇan would have told to allow us inside if we showed up.

Oh you who protects the meanings of ‘a’ (‘அ’) and nama: which indicate His ability to protect us.

=\> [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/): kodi tōraṇa vāsal -\> Entrance to srī vaikuṇṭam -\> [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) is protecting that, and enabling anyone to reach vaikuntam who has done saraṇāgaty through an ācāryan associated with him. { *viṇṇin talai ninṟu vīdaḷippān; விண்ணின் தலை நின்று வீடளிப்பான்* }

**maṇik kadavam**

She sang ‘[*māmān magaḷē maṇik kadavam*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=465)‘ to gōpikas’ houses – this is kriṣṇan’s tirumāḷigai so she is even more enthusiastic to sing praises of the door.

This door would attract the adiyārs to go inside; once they are inside maṇi vaṇṇan’s beauty would make it impossible for them to go out, making us say *‘மற்றொன்றினைக் காணாவே’ maṟṟonṟinaik kāṇāvē* \[amalanādipirān\] – ((after seeing your beauty), my eyes won’t see anything else).

**maṇik kadavam tāḷ tiṟavāi**

**“**Please open this beautiful and attractive door with gems embedded”.

“This is an unsafe town (for kaṇṇan), and you are coming here in the middle of the night and asking to open the door. Who are you?”

“In this place where kaṇṇan is there, who removes our fears, what is there to be afraid of?” (prahlādan said to his father that there is no fear in him because he thinks of anantan who removes our fears).

“Is this trētā yugam, with the father (dasaratan) very strong who killed samparan an enemy of dēvas, and with the sons growing like lions, with ministers like vasiśtar, and a country like tiru ayōddi which other kings are afraid of?  No, this is near the end of dvāpara yugam rubbing shoulders with kali yugam, father is of soft nature, the kids here are very small and naughty, this place is not even a town, kamsan the enemy, things that move turn out to be asuras bent on killing Him. Can I be without any fear?”

“should you be afraid of us girls too?”

“sūrpaṇakā was a girl too”

“She was a rākśasi. We are small covherd gōpikās who are born to the gōpas who fear for this boy’s safety (*இவனுக்கு என்வருகிறதோ!*)”

“After pūtanai I have to be afraid of even covherd women”

maṇik kadavam tāḷ tiravāi: Please save us by removing any inclination towards ātmānubhavam which results in svātantriyam (feeling independent of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)).

[rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) – we request [rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) to open our minds to receive jñānam.

**āyar siṟumiyarōmukku**

We are small little covherd gōpikās, too innocent to know even counting, we did not come alone like phūtanai, we are the gōpikas from all of 5 lakh houses of tiruvāyppādi.

“Can I trust because something is small? (That asuran) came as a little calf too (*கன்றாயன்றோ வந்து நலியப் பார்த்தது*).  Tell me what you came here for, and I will judge about you”

**aṟai paṟai**

“We came to get paṟai (instrument) for nōnbu” – trying to say that it is an important matter.

“For that I will ask when they wake up. You may wait.”

**māyan –** kaṇṇan who seems to be under the control of the gopikās

**maṇivaṇṇan**

maṇi – gem – one can easily handle/control it by tying into their saree – Even if He does not get under their control, they would not let go of Him because of His beauty

[rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) – He is the central gem of the chain of [ācāryas](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/).

**nēnnalē vāi nērndān –** You do not have to tell Him about this, He promised us yesterday itself that we can go to Him at this time.

**nennalē** – not like today when we are begging you, it was the wonderful yesterday when he was begging us

**vāi nērndān**

“How can I trust His words told in public”

“He gave His word to draupati in public and it did not fail. What he promised us also would not fail.”

nennalē vāi nērndān – [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) -\> [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) informed [madurakavi āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/17/madhurakavi-azhwar/) quite earlier about [bhaviśyadaācārya](http://bhavishyadhacharyan.koyil.org/) ([rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)) when he asked [madurakavi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/17/madhurakavi-azhwar/) to boil water from tāmiraparani river. He also said long ago, ‘*kaliyum kedum kaṇḍu kōṇmin*‘ about how [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) would help defeat kali by spreading the truth about samskrita and tamiḻ vēdams (divya prabandams).

The guard continues, “Though He told you yesterday, śouldn’t there be some use of His putting us for guarding here? āren’t we here to refuse entry to some people; Is He responsible for our duty? I need to know what you are thinking of doing. You are all here with the expectation of getting something from Him (paṟai related to nōnbu)”

**tūyōmāi vandōm**

“‘paṟai’ is just a front. We are ananya prayōjanaigaḷ (not expecting anything except [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)). We want to do kainkaryam (mangaḷāsāsanam) and that is the only thing we want.

vandōm – “we are doing what He should have done (come to us).”

“What is the sign that you are ananya prayōjanaigaḷ? (not prying Him for a benefit other than Him)”

**tuyil eḻap pāduvān**

“We came here to sing [tiruppaḷḷiyeḻucci](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=374) for Him (wake Him up).” sītā pirātti enjoyed how He was sleeping (when kākāsuran troubled Her, before She woke Him up). Here these gōpikās want to enjoy looking at Him slowly get up.”

These are the gōpikās with the background of those who sang ‘*arangattammā paḷḷi eḻundaruḷāyē* \[[tiruppaḷḷiyeḻucci](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=374)\]’.

Though he understood their hearts, the guard wanted to enjoy talking to them more, so he has been trying to act strict. When he says “Okay, I will open; please wait”, they say:

**vāyāl munnamunnam māṟṟādē**

Even if you think in your mind negatively, please do not say it in words.– it might become true and affect our goals. perumāḷ accepted vibīśaṇan only after sugrīvan agreed that vibhīśaṇan should be treated equal to them.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/): unlike prior ācāryas who wanted to put people through a lot of test and then give the meanings and rahasyams, [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)! You wanted to give those to anyone who sincerely wanted to learn.

**ammā**

Making him feel special; Is the one inside kind to us, or are you the one who is kind?

The guard finally says, “pull the lock, and push the door open”

**nee nēsa nilaik kadavam nīkku**

That door is even more affectionate (to kriṣṇan). It won’t open if we pushed; only you please open it.

All cētanācētanams ([sentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and [insentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/)), all in tiruvāippādi are for kaṇṇan (unlike in kamsan’s palace where everything is for killing Him).

As a protector of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) you are making sure that only the right people are allowed inside; because of your extreme affection towards Him, you are not allowing those who are fond of Him too? So you please get convinced and you open the door for us.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)/ācāryar: We the siśyās know that our efforts won’t help, it is only Yōūṟ wish and your blessing / anugraham that will help us to be a srīvaiṣṇava, and to learn the meanings of vēdams and divya prabhandams (*srī vacana bhūśaṇam: sadācārya prasādattālē varugiṟa bhagavat prasādattālē enṟu tuṇindu – ஸதாசார்ய ப்ரஸாதத்தாலே வருகிற பகவத் ப்ரஸாதத்தாலே என்று துணிந்து*).

In this pāsuram, they get permission of the guards of srī nandagōpa’s tirumāḷigai, to go inside and do kainkaryam of singing [tiruppaḷḷiyeḻuci](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=374) through adiyārs.

English translation ṟaghuram srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 17. Ambarame tannire

*ambaramē taṇṇīrē sōṟē aṟam sēyyum emperumān nandagōpālā eḻundirāi kombanārkkelām koḻundē kula viḷakkē emperumātti yasōdāi aṟivuṟāi ambaramūdarttu ōngi ulagaḷanda umbar kōmānē uṟangādu eḻundirāi sempoṟ kaḻaladic celvā baladēvā umbiyum nīyum uṟangēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

emperumān nandagōpālā – O nandagōpa who is our svāmi!; aṟam – who donates; ambaramē – clothes; taṇṇirē – water; sōṟē – and food (all the time, all of it); eḻundirāi – please wake up; asodāi – dear yasōdai pirātti!; koḻundē – fine one among us; kombu anārkkellām – women who are like vanji tree that is full of flowers; kula viḷakkē – who is a bright lamp for the covherds; aṟivuṟāi – please wake up!; umbar kōmānē – O dēva dēva!; ambaram ūdu aṟuttu – who pierced the ākāsam (space); ōngi – raised; ulagu aḷanda – and kindly spanned/measured all the worlds; uṟangādu – without sleeping; eḻundirāi – please get up; selvā – O srīmān!; baladēvā – O baladēvā!; sem pon kaḻal adi – (who is) having reddish gold tiruvadi (of wealth); umbiyum nīyum – your brother kaṇṇan and you; uṟangēl – please do not sleep

**Перевод:**

**ambaramē taṇṇīrē sōṟē**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As the guard of the tirumāḷigai of nandagōpar removed the lock, opened the doors and let them in, they are going inside and waking up nandagōpar and others. First they wake up nandagōpar who was lying down in the front as he was wondering whether the gōpikās are trying to steal kaṇṇan. If women would steal aniruddāḻvān (grandson of kriṣṇan), does it not go without saying that they would steal kriṣṇan from whom aniruddan came?*

Clothes, water, food whatever is needed for people, nandagōpar gives them all;

The ‘E’ and the end of these words is to question whether this is all what nandagōpar has learned to do; that it looks like as if he keeps doing each and every one of it only.

ambaram: saree; also means sky/space (this meaning is used in 5<sup>th</sup> line of this pāsuram – *ambaram ūdu aṟuttu*).

**aṟam seyyum**

he is giving all these things out of kindness, and as if that is his life, and without expecting anything in return.

Like how you give the people what they need for life, please give us what We need for our life – kaṇṇan!

**emperumān …**

You give sarees/clothes (bhōjñam as it gives comfort), water (dārakam, life-saving), and sōru (bhōśakam, nurturing); go got us these things for us which is in one form that is kriṣṇan;

Can all the three be one kriṣṇan? Yes, [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) agrees! ‘*uṇṇum sōṟu (bhōśakam), parugu nīr (dārakam), tinnum veṟṟilai (bhōjñam), ellām kaṇṇan*’.

\[This applies to ācāryan as well – he can be many at the same time for siśyas:  *srī vacana bhūśaṇam of* [*piḷḷai lōkācāryar*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/18/pillai-lokacharyar/)***:*** *mantramum, dēvataiyum, Phalamum, Phalānubandigaḷum, palasādanamum, aihika bhōgamum ellām ācāryan enṟu ninaikkak kadavan / “mātā, pitā, yuvataya:”  engiṟa slōkattilē, ivvarttattai* [*paramācāryarum*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) *aruḷic ceidār. (ஸ்ரீவசந பூஷணம்****:*** *மந்த்ரமும், தேவதையும், பலமும், பலாநுபந்திகளும், பலஸாதநமும், ஐஹிகபோகமுமெல்லாம் ஆசார்யனென்று நினைக்கக் கடவன். “மாதா பிதா யுவதய:” என்கிற ஸ்லோகத்திலே, இவ்வர்த்தத்தை* [*பரமாசார்யரும்*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) *அருளிச்செய்தார்*).\]

Since you are intending to give kaṇṇan to us who are always living for him, you are our [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

As it seemed that nandagōpar woke up and gave his permission, they proceed to wake up yaśōdā.

Unlike how they are going to wake up nappinnai first and then kriṣṇan, why did they first wake up nandagōpar instead of yaśōdā? Since she wanted to be near kaṇṇan, she is sleeping inside.

This refers to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) as well. \[-Ar: adding Ar is to show more respect. (maman-ar).\]

*svāpadēsam* for the above lines: [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)‘s nature of working on emperumān’s happiness, removal of bhōgam of our own when doing that, and because of that the kainkaryam – our ācāryan gives these as if this is all he is going all the time for his siśyās.

**kombanārkkellām koḻundē**

She (yaśōdā) is the head of all the people who have got daughters. She is the only mother who had the joy of enjoying [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) compared to all other mothers of various avatārams of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

kombu – ‘vanji’ tree’s.

anār – like the kombu

koḻundu grows first out of the tree, so it is the head; if any problem happens to the root, it is the koḻundu that would first look dull.

koḻundē –  if any gōpikā is having any problems, isn’t it your face that becomes dull?

**kula viḷakkē –** you are the light of the families of all these gōpikās

**emperumātti –** you got our kriṣṇan for us, and so you are our svāmiṇi!

**yaśōdāi –** Unlike nandagōpar, being the same gender you can understand our angst; when nandagōpar who is strict has himself woken up, you who is kind (to kaṇṇan) can still be sleeping?

like yaśōdai, prapannas also can tie Him – with tirumantram (*srī vacana bhūśaṇam: tirumantram kai pugundavāṟē īsvaran kai pugurum… prāpaka lābham tirumantrattālē. ஸ்ரீவசந பூஷணம்: திருமந்த்ரம் கைபுகுந்தவாறே ஈஶ்வரன் கைபுகரும்… ப்ராபக லாபம் திருமந்த்ரத்தாலே*.).

**aṟivurāi –** if you wake up then all our troubles will go away

After getting the acceptance from yaśōdai pirātti, they proceed to inside and wake up kriṣṇan.

*svāpadēsam:* Like how the vanji plant would bend and accommodate to the flows of the river, of all the mantrams that make themselves appropriate based on the interest of samsāris, tirumantram is considered the head, like a lamp for the prapannas, natural swami for them, which can give life in good ancestry and other benefits. (*mumukśuppadi: cētanar ninṟa ninṟa aḷavukku īdāyirukkum. முமுக்ஷுப்படி:  சேதநர் நின்ற நின்ற அளவுக்கு ஈடாயிருக்கும்.*)

**ambaram ūdu aṟuttu ōngi**

For dēvas, who come to you asking for their own benefits, you grew big and strong (as trivikraman) into all the space regardless of whether they are good ones or not;

You grew with your kindness, with your guṇam of protection as the water for growing.

**ulagaḷanda**

Like a mother who keeps her arm on her child while sleeping next to it, you grew while placing your kind tiruvadi on everyone regardless of whether they were aware of it or interested in it;

**umbar kōmānē**

And by doing them the favor you got dēvas/nityasūris under your spell. They and these gōpikās are lost to the simplicity/approacability of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who used His most supreme feet for small tasks like covering the forests and small places  (the world).

**urangādu eḻundirāi**

Do we have to be men or be those who come to you asking for own benefits in order for you to pay attention? Can it not be gōpikās who came to you for doing only kainkaryam? You touched your lotus feet on all the people, made it so easily available to even those who did not realize your greatness – we know its greatness, can you not show your feet to us?

Can you at least show us your face?

You who stole our sleep, is happily sleeping here? We had heard that you deeply missed a woman and suffered with no food or sleep (*anidra: satatam* ***rāma****:*). We thought that would happen here too, and what we see here is upside down. *அழகிதாயிருந்தது!*

The gōpikās woke up and got their acceptance in this order:

adiyār – nandagōpar;  
[pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – yasōdai;  
[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – kriṣṇan.  
~~~~~ praṇavam.

Since He did not wake up even after saying all these, they realize their mistake that they should have woken up nambi mūtta pirān (balarāmar) first (that is, go to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) through adiyār), so they wake up balarāmar:

**sem poṟ kaḻal adic celvā baladēvā**

kriṣṇan came to this world after you – you were born here first and blessed this place with your golden feet; The wealth that iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇan) got after being born later, you got by being born earlier; Like lakśmaṇan you also got the wealth that is kainkaryam, śouldn’t you also be awake like lakśmaṇan? ḍidn’t he stay awake to protect pirātti and perumāḷ when they slept! āren’t you too great in getting kriṣṇan together with this gōpikā ([āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/))? If so, is it acceptable for you to be sleeping now?

When [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) are having ūdal (tiff) with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) they would refer to ādi sēśan as ‘*tī mugattu nāgaṇai*‘ (bed of serpent who emits fire from his face)**;** when are happy about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) they would be kind and say ‘*nāgaṇai misai nampirān* ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is on a comfortable bed of ādi sēśan)’. Here they are being nice and saying ‘*sem poṟ kaḻal adi celvā*‘.

\[perumāḷ requested lakśmaṇan (ādi sēśan), “You are born as younger brother and are doing so many kainkaryams to me in this avatāram. During another avatāram please be born as elder brother to me”. In kriṣṇa avatāram, ādi sēśan was born as elder brother balarāmar, and did kainkaryams to kaṇṇan!\]

**umbiyum nīyum urangēl**

(addressing balarāmar). “He is beloved to you and you are beloved to Him, and both are sleeping with legs on each other; He being younger brother is nice to you, you being adiyār (*cenrāl kudaiyām…*) is nice to Him and we in the middle are losing His time with us as you both are sleeping”.

“Those who lie on the bed would sleep, but is there sleep to the bed itself? You (balarāmar) who is His bed, please wake up, and also wake Him for us; we are His bed, and give Him to us.”

In this pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) wakes up nandagōpar, yasodai pirātti, then both kriṣṇan and balarāman. They go through the proper order to kriṣṇan.

English translation ṟaghuram srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 18. Undu madakalirran

*undu madakaḷiṟṟan ōdāda tōḷ valiyan nanda gōpālan marumagaḷē nappinnāi gandam kamaḻum kuḻalee kadai tiṟavāi vandu engum kōḻi aḻaittana kāṇ madavip pandal mēl palkāl kuyil inangaḷ kūvina kāṇ pandār virali un maittunan pēr pādac centāmaraik kaiyāl sīrār vaḷai olippa vandu tiṟavāi magiḻndu elōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

madam undu kaḷiṟṟan – strong like an elephant that is driven by fury producing water in eyes; ōdāda tōḷ valiyan – with strong shoulders so he does not run away from the battle field; nandagōpālan marumagaḷē – O daughter-in-law of such nandagōpar!; nappinnāi – who possesses the name of nappinnai!; gandam kamaḻum kuḻalee – O who has got her hair/tresses emitting fragrance all around!; kadai tiṟavāi – please open the door; vandengum kōḻi aḻaittana kāṇ – spreading in all four directions, the cocks are yelping – please see; kuyil inangaḷ – (also) groups of cuckoos; mādavi pandal mēl – (sleeping) on the pandal (structure for giving shade) having plants of kurukkatti; kūvina pal kāl kāṇ – calling many times, please see; pandu Ar virali – O you having fingers holding the (flower) ball (which gets kaṇṇan to lose to you); un maittunan pēr pāda – (for us) to sing the names of your nātan (husband) kriṣṇan; vandu – come walking; sīr Ar vaḷai olippa – with your sweet bangles making jingling sounds; sem tāmaraik kaiyāl – and with your hands that are reddish like a lotus; magiḻndu tiṟavāi – open the door happily

**Перевод:**

**undu madakaḷiṟṟan**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*kākāsuran, vibhīśaṇāḻvān lived because they had the blessings of tāyār. sūrpaṇakai did not because she avoided tāyār and went straight to rāman; rāvaṇan did not live because He went only to tāyār and avoided perumān.*

Strong like the elephant that is pushed by must;

ācāryan removes our aggressiveness.

**ōdāda tōḷ valiyan**

Strong, and would not back off when looking at the enemies;

Saying these things about nandagōpan is to reduce their angst about dangers to kriṣṇan.

tōḷ valiyan – when we are afraid thinking about our pāpams/karmas we think about perumān’s strength and reduce our fear.

ōdāda – such strength is not common in this place.

ācāryan havng the strength of jñānam

**nandagōpālan marumagaḷē**

Referring to nappinnai as the daughter-in-law of the great nandagōpalan, instead of as daughter of srī kumbar. This is what she likes too – like how sītā pirātti is fond of referring herself as dasarata cakravarty’s daughter-in-law.

nandagōpālan – ācāryan who saves us;  marumagaḷē – If [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) can be amicable like a son to ācāryan, then there is nothing wrong in saying pirātti as ācāryan’s daughter-in-law.

**nappinnāi**

Since she (nappinnai) kept quiet thinking that after kriṣṇan was born in this tiruvāippādi, every girl became a daughter-in-law of nandagōpan, so this is not specifically about me, the gōpikās say, ‘nappinnāi’.

**gandam kamaḻum kuḻali**

Fragrance becomes meaningful on the day of joining nappinnai’s divine plait;

It is said that [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is the source of all fragrances *‘sarva ganda:’,* and her plait gives fragrance even to Him.
