She attracts [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with this, encourages Him to protect those who surrender to him, and makes everyone appreciate and celebrate Him.

**kadai tiṟavāi**

kriṣṇan’s fragrance and your fragrance have mixed and its flooding your place, please open your doors and let them out for us to quench our separation; please open the dam for us;

Please remove our ignorance (ajñānam).

“why are you waking us in the mid-night?”

“it is dawned”

“how do you say that?”

**vandu engum kōḻi aḻaittana kāṇ**

“The roosters are crowing”

“Just one of them doing that will make it a dawn?”

“engum kōḻi aḻaittana” (everywhere) – like tapping on our laps to wake us up.

**mādavip pandal mēl**

If the cuckoo birds which slept in a cozy bed have woken up and are cooing, then it is not a sign?

“Just one of them does not make it a dawn”

**palkāl kuyil inangaḷ kūvina kāṇ**

“The one bird is calling so many times after not seeing you in the morning”.

“Just one calling many times would not make it a dawn”

“different varieties of them, calling you for a long time”

**pandār virali**

“It is our loss that we are not acētanams ([insentient](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/))”

“why so?”

“then we would have been in your hands (like that ball)! “

nappinnai won kriṣṇan in playing the flower ball, and she is holding the ball in one hand and hugging kriṣṇan with Her other hand; He is for enjoyment (bhōgam), and the ball is for playing (leelai);   *nāram* (jeevātmās, etc.,) in one hand, and *nārāyaṇan* in the other hand;  vibhūti in one hand, vibhūtimān in another. From this, with these two relationships, Her puruśakāram can be seen.

Flower ball is like devotees – pirātti is holding us in one hand and perumān in the other and is always helping the two parties reach each other.

can you not include us in your game? and we will nicely play with you and kriṣṇan.

“What have you come here for?”

**un maittunan pēr pāda**

maittunan:  husband

When you are having interesting arguments with your husband, we want to join your side and say things that would support your side, and talk about His (mis)deeds; don’t the children always side their mother when there are any arguments between parents? Like how when sītā pirātti told iḷaiya perumāḷ that she won the swimming race against perumāḷ, then iḷaiya perumāḷ also supported only pirātti.

when you are happy together, we will also go along with it.

un maittunan – like it is phrased in the first part of dvaya mantram.

pēr pāda – nāraṇa nambi has got so many names; also He can be identified as this girl’s adiyān and that girl’s adiyān;

pēr pāda – when you both play with the ball and He loses, we want to sing His names for that.

The girls tell nappinnai pirātti that kriṣṇan is common to all the girls in tiruvāyppādi, without us He is not complete, etc..

Hearing this, pirātti says, “If so, kriṣṇa! open the door”.

But, He says, “If they are on your side, would I open the door for them?”.

pirātti tells the girls, “I have setup the doors to automatically open for you. Come inside”

==\> They reply: ” We cannot get You by ourselves, we can get only by yourselves;  we look for your efforts”:

**sentāmarai kaiyāl**

pirātti, we look for your hand – perumān keeps His Hand in the pose that says ‘anjēl’ (don’t be afraid) when we are worried about our pāpams. Your hand says ‘anjēl’ – that we shall not be afraid of perumān’s svātantriyam. perumān is also fond of your hands;

Your hands which have become red due to playing with the Flower ball.

kai (hand) – implies jñānam. ([āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) sang: jñānak kai tā (tiruvāimoḻi 2-9-2)

Flower’s attributes are beauty, softness, and fragrance. In svāpadēsam, these are: beauty – reducing interest in other matters; softness – reducing enjoyment for self on other matters; fragrance – love for Him.  kai – jñānam with these understanding.

nappinnai tries to open the door but without her bangles making any noise as she had raised them tight in her arms:

**sīrār vaḷai olippa**

open, with your bangles making sweet jingling sounds; like how the rain clouds would thunder and rain;  we would survive by hearing that sound of your bangles;  since you are always with Him – ‘*agalakillēn iṟaiyum* \[tirvāimoḻi\]*‘,* those bangles stay in your arm – *sangu tangu mun kai nangai* \[*tiruccanda viruttam-57*\]; it is unlike our arms that don’t hold the bangles due to separation, like *‘iru kaiyil sangivai nillā’ (tirunenuntāṇḍakam-24).* It would stay in their hands only if they both together (with kriṣṇan helping) put the bangles in their arms (after the nōnbu).

with that sound kriṣṇan also would wake up and we will live happily.

svāpadēsam – with ever lasting bhagavat sambandam, having the brightness due to getting the meanings of rahasyams.

As nappinnai thought she should not be separated from kriṣṇan, she tried to open the door while lying down:

**vandu tiṟavāi**

Please give us the fame that **“**she walked a few steps for gōpikās”. Please walk a few steps this way and that way and open the door – kriṣṇan would enjoy the beauty of your walk and so would we see that and live happily;

svāpadēsam – without considering our efforts, you please come and remove our ajñānam related to obstacles related to svarūpam (our/His nature), upāyam (means to reach Him), and puruśārttam (goal :- kainkaryam).

**magiḻndu**

without thinking of the darmā like “when these gōpikās are standing outside could we have other anubhavam”, come and open the door with happiness in your mind, that it is a blessing for you; show your happy face after being with Him; we wanted to do nōnbu to ‘*magiḻndu nīrāda*‘ (take bath happy / be with kriṣṇan happily), and as a main benefit we want to see you happy.

More than being with Him, you are happy to see His adiyārs – we want to see that happiness so please open the door.

The reason for doing kainkaryam is to see tāyār perumāḷ’s happiness; (owner becomes happy when he gets his properties that is we.)

svāpadēsam – puruśakāram be yours.

vaḷai ōlippa – reduce the angst of *ears*  
pēr pāda – reduce the angst of our *tongues*  
sentamarai kaiyāl – reduce the angst by *touch*  
gandam kamaḻum kuḻali – reduce the angst by smell  
magiḻndu – with all the *indriyams* becoming happy, your *manas* will get the happiness.

ācāryan ==\> [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) is very fond of this pāsuram.

206<sup>th</sup> item in **vārtāmālai** by [pinbaḻagiya perumāḷ jīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/04/20/pinbhazhagiya-perumal-jiyar/) who is a siśya of [nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/):

[nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/16/nampillai-english/) says, “As [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) was reciting this line from the pāsuram’s ‘*sīrār vaḷai olippa vandu tiṟavāi*‘ (open the doors, with your bangles making sweet music), he happened to come in front of [tirukkōśtiyūr nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/)‘s tirumāḷigai, and at the same time [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/)‘s daughter opened the door to go out, (with her bangles making noise); [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) felt that this is nappinnai pirātti herself and he prostrated in front of her, falling on the floor; she got afraid and ran inside the house; when her [father](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) asked what was the matter, she told her [father](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) that [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) fell on her feet; when [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) came insde, [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) knowing the reason, asked [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)*,”*[*iḷayāḻvīr*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)*! Are you doing recital of undu madakaḷiṟṟan?” ‘  (இளையாழ்வீர்! இப்போது அநுஸந்தானம் உந்துமதகளிற்றனோ?” என்று அருளிச்செய்தாரென்று பிள்ளையருளிச்செய்வர்.)*

English translation ṟaghuram srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 19. Kuttu vilakkeriya

*kuttu viḷakkeriya kōttukkāl kattil mēl mettenṟa panca sayanattin mēlēṟi kōttalar pūnkuḻal nappinnai kongai mēl vaittuk kidanda malar mārbhā vāi tiṟavāi maittadam kaṇṇināi nī un maṇāḷanai ettanai pōdum tuyil eḻa ottāi kāṇ ettanaiyēlum piṟivāṟṟagillāyāl tattuvam anṟu tagavu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kuttu viḷakku – (with bright) lamp; eriya – lit up; panca sayanattin mēl ēṟi – climbing on to the mattress made of cotton; kōdu kāl kattil mēl – on the bed with legs made of elephant tusks; mettenṟa – and very soft; vaittuk kidanda malar mārbhā – O (kriṣṇā) you have placed your broad chest; kongai mēl – on the divine chest; nappinnai – of nappinnai; kottu alar pū kuḻal – who is having hair that holds bunches of flowers; vāi tiṟavāi – open the mouth and talk; mai tadam kaṇṇināi – Oh (nappinnai) who is having broad eyes decorated with mai (kājal); nī – you are; ottāi kāṇ – not agreeing; tuyil eḻa – to wake up; un maṇāḷanai – your husband kaṇṇan; ettanai pōdum – even for a second; pirivu āṟṟagillāyāl – you are not able to tolerate separation from Him; ettanaiyēlum – even for a short time; tattuvam anṟu – (this) is not according to your svarūpam (your true nature); tagavu anṟu – and it is not matching your true behavior

**Перевод:**

**kuttu viḷakkeriya (with the lighted lamps)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As she (nappinnai) tried to get up to open doors for gōpikās, kriṣṇan thought “She śouldn’t open the doors for my adiyārs”, and so He hugged Her and did not let Her get up. gōpikās ask kriṣṇan to please get up. But He doesn’t, so they ask nappinnai again to come and open the door for them. Both of them are important to adiyārs (devotees).*

While we are desperately waiting here, you (nappinnai) are having kriṣṇānubhavam. Unlike we who had to wait for elders to give permission (for nōnbu, kriṣṇānubhavam), (you) without any worry about the dawning day, without having to look for dark for going with kriṣṇan (*naḷḷirutkaṇ ennai uyttidumin* – nāciār tirumoḻi – 12-3); you are enjoying with kriṣṇan making this morning as a night; and with lighted lamps, you are lying in bed looking at kriṣṇan’s face – what a wonderful opportunity for you!

(viḷakku – jñānam)

**kōttukkāl kattil mēl**

This bed is made of tusks of *kuvalayāpīdam*; as a brave wife of kriṣṇan you cannot sleep in any other bed; unlike us who have to search for Him all over brindāvanam, you are with Him inside the house in the bed without any shyness. This is a great blessing for you!

kattil – instead of saying to imply the bed’s greatness using respectful words like ‘tiru paḷḷik kattil’ they say just ‘kattil’ – due to their belonging to covherd clan (ஜாதிப் பேச்சு).

svāpadēśam ==\> The wild elephant is the ‘me/mine’ in us. The four legs of the bed relate to four types of this *ahankāram* 1) – *sēśatvē kartrutvam* (when thinking that we are the only good devotee who worships emperumān; and we do it for our own benefit only); 2) *jñātrutvē kartrutvam* (when thinking only we have got a better knowledge about emperumān); 3) *kartrutvē kartrutvam* (when thinking that we are solely capable and responsible for doing tasks); and 4) *bhōktrutvē kartrutvam* (when enjoying for our own sake).

**mettenṟa**

While we are desperately waiting here, you are enjoying about the inner size and outer size of the mattress, and it is fitting you, and you are sleeping happily!

**panca sayanattin mēlēṟi**

mattress made of soft cotton; decorated with five colors; bed having the five attributes, being beautiful, cozy, fragrant, wide, and warm.

Like in srī vaikuṇṭam how we would step on your bed to climb and come to you, should you not wish for us to climb on this bed and come to you?

While we are waiting, śouldn’t you feel the bed to be uncomfortable for you kriṣṇa? Parents won’t be comfortable in the bed that the kids did not jump and play on;  you would not be comfortable in your bed if we don’t come sit on your lap – like in srī vaikuṇṭam; there also, you will ask “*kōsi?*” (Who are you?) and we will say “*aham brahmāsmi*” (I am brahmam – i.e., your (bhagavān’s) body).

svāpadēśam==\>The four types of ahankāram are removed when we (climb) learn and follow ācāryan’s words related to [arta pancakam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2013/01/thiruppavai-artha-panchakam.html) thus softening us like the mattress.

**kottalar pūnkuḻal nappinnai**

nappinnai with beautiful flowers in her thick hair; they blossom due to the contact with her hair – she is *vāsam sei pūnkuḻalāḷ* (tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-2) – her hair gives fragrance to the flowers; whereas, we said ‘[*malar ittu nām mudiyōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)‘ (won’t wear flowers) while doing nōnbu.

**nappinnai kongai mēl vaittuk kidanda**

He has kept her divine breast on Him; or, He has kept his face on Her divine breast – and sleeping happily instead of trying to open the door. (tiruppāvai [taniyan](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=231) also mentioned how [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) woke him up at this time).

breast -\> bhakti / love for Him.

**malar mārbha**

They are addressing kriṣṇan.

The safety She gets from His broad and soft chest. His chest is so broad that Hers feels so small (like in *‘muṟṟāra vana mulaiyāḷ pāvai* \[tiruneduntāṇḍakam-19\]); He feels happy like a flower blossoming, and His chest becomes even bigger because of Her breasts felt on His chest. That is, He gets multiplying happiness to see our love for Him.

If we say that His chest becomes bigger He becomes happier etc., would it not contradict with what vēdas say, that He (brahmam) does not change, it is complete, etc.? The meaning of those words in śṟuti is that He does not have such characteristics like us. If He does not melt for adiyār’s bhakti, that would become a drawback for Him – He would be no different than a stone, isn’t it?

This is the key phrase of the pāsuram’s actual meaning.

{malarnda mārbu of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/): [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s wide chest indicates His kind heart for His adiyārs. First, pirātti argues for us with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and makes Him accept us in spite of our pāpams. This was in the previous pāsuram – pirātti has got a kind heart for us. Once [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) accepts adiyārs, even if pirātti argues with Him saying that we did a lot of pāpams and so He should leave us, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would beg/argue with pirātti in order to keep us with Him. He would say that if He accepted someone who did not do any pāpam then what would be great about that.. only if He accepted people like us with pāpams would His greatness be visible. He would go another step further and say that whatever we did is not pāpam at all! It is like the kids would be quiet and obedient when they go to a house of someone else – but they would do all sorts of mischief only under the shadow of their parents in their house – likewise, these are all our children, whatever they do is within the support of our shadow… so don’t make it a big issue, let them be with me…. says perumaḷ. (*mumukśuppadi****:*** *sērppārē pirikkum pōdum vida māttādē rakśikkiṟa ennai.. / முமுக்ஷுப்படி****:*** *சேர்ப்பாரே பிரிக்கும் போதும் விட மாட்டாதே ரக்ஷிக்கிற என்னை..*). That is why [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) says here, that pirātti’s chest seems small when she is hugging perumāḷ – His kindness is so big.

(One more thing.. pirātti is nudging [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to leave us – this is only to test Him to make sure that He accepted us out of His own affection and not just because pirātti recommended us first. When [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) pleads to Her that He would not leave us, then She becomes extremely happy and confident – similar to how one would shake a pole after setting it to stand on the ground – they shake not to pull it out but to make sure it is going to stay strong). }

What is said for nappinnai being with kriṣṇan is applicable for all [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/).

**vāi tiṟavāi**

(kriṣṇa!) Please open your mouth and say at least one word in your strong voice; without wishing for anything else we are waiting for you to get up, can you not say even a word? If she is not allowing you to get up, can you not say ‘*māsuca:*‘ from your bed?

**maittadam kaṇṇināi**

When He tried to say something to them, nappinnai pirātti is now taking turn to say, ‘You did not allow me, now why are you trying to open the door?”, and with this signal from Her eyes She is shutting His mouth; doing ‘*vāi vāi*‘ (literal: mouth, mouth) -\> (like, ‘cup! cup! in hindi, or, like ‘zip your mouth’).

Now He is in the middle of a beautiful and wide sea that is nappinnai – only when He sees the end/shore would He be able to see us; we who said ‘[*mai ittu eḻudōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)‘ (won’t wear mai in our eyes (as part of nōnbu)), can wear it only if you let us;

we thought it is our bhājñam and were proud to see your (nappinnai’s) beautiful eyes – but now those eyes are coming in the way of our kriṣṇā anubhavam;

**nī**

The one who is supposed to let us join Him, can you separate Him from us? (**u**kāram in praṇavam).

**un maṇāḷanai** – (not) letting your husband

We thought it is our bhājñam to see kriṣṇan doing the chores with bhavyam (obedience) in the place that you looked at with your raised eye brows; we thought He is *lōka bharttā* (common husband/protector to all); or, they are saying, we now think that kriṣṇan is yours (*maṇāḷan*).

**ettanai pōdum tuyil eḻa ottāi kāṇ**

You are not letting Him get up even for one second**;** you would get afraid and become pale thinking that He is going away from you even when He is just moving away in bed a little in order to adjust Himself while being with you in the bed.

**ettanaiyēlum pirivāṟṟakillāyāl**

But that is not your fault; it is your weakness of not wanting to be separated from Him even for a second. ‘*agalakillēn iṟaiyum*‘ and you are enjoying Him every moment; but it is not helping us now.

**tattuvam anṟu tagavu**

*tattuvam****,*** *tagavu anṟu***:**  tattuvam – satyam (truth); tagavu anṟu – not darmam. – Not saying out of our ignorance – really it is not darmam that you are not letting Him open the door for us.

*tattuvam anṟu****:***   tatuvam = svarūpam (nature); tatuvam anṟu – it is not your nature to separate him from us.

*tagavu anṟu****:*** it is not characteristic of you, and does not match your kindness, does not go with your puruśakāram bhāvam as well, to separate Him from us.

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 20. Muppattu muvar

*muppattu mūvar amararkku mun cenṟukappam tavirkkum kaliyē tuyil eḻāiseppam udaiyāy tiṟal udaiyāy! ceṟṟārkku veppam kodukkum vimalā! tuyil eḻāiseppenna men mulaic cevvāic ciṟu marungulnappinnai nangāi! tiruvē! tuyil eḻāiukkamum tattōḷiyum tandu un maṇāḷanaiippōdē emmai nīrāttu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaliyē – O the strong kaṇṇa!; mun cenṟu – who goes voluntarily/early (before any problem comes); muppattu mūvar amararkku – for all the dēvas (muppattu mukkōti dēvas – 33 crore); tavirkkum – and remove; kappam – (their) fear; tuyil eḻāi – please wake up; vimalā – Oh the pure one!; seppam udaiyāy – (who is) honest (in protecting the devotees); tiṟal udaiyāy – (and) strong (to destroy the enemies of devotees); kodukkum – (and who) can give; veppam – hardship; seṟṟārkku – to the enemies; tuyil eḻāi – get up from your bed; nappinnai nangāi – O nappinnai pirātti!; tiruvē – who can equal periya pirātti!; men mulai – (who has got) soft breasts; seppu anna – like a golden pot; sevvāi – (and) reddish mouth/lips; siru marungul – (and) tiny waist; tuyil eḻāi – get up from the bed; tandu – give (us); ukkamum – fan (Ala vattam, required as part of nōnbu); tattu oḷiyum – and mirror; un maṇāḷanai – and (give) Him too who is your nātan; nīrāttu – (and) bathe (i.e., kriṣṇā anubhavam); emmai – us

**Перевод:**

**muppattu mūvar**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Based on what the gōpikās requested in the previous pāsuram, nappinnai pirātti tries to get kriṣṇan to go and open the door, but He doesn’t move. So they tell pirātti – He does not belong to Himself, you are the one for Him and us, you please get us the kriṣṇa anubhavam.*

muppattu mukkōti dēvar (33 dēvā types like aśvini kumāras; 33 crore dēvas); will you help only when it is these many numbers? Would you not bless and be with us if the number is 5 lakhs? (number of families of gōpikās in tiruvāyppādi)

Would you not help those who are desperate to be with you?

Would you help only those who wake you up and get you hurt by arrows (cakravarty tirumagan), would you not wake up for us who are fond of just seeing your beauty?

\[ mup-**paṟṟu**-mūvar – paṟṟu -\> desires/wants. People with one of three types of desires – aiśvaryārti – desires wealth; kaivalyārti – desires enjoyment of his own ātmā only; and prapannan – desires [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) only. \]

**amararkku**  – for dēvas

What is the point of helping only those who don’t have death? Should you not be helping us who would die out of desperation when you don’t see us?

Would you only help dēvas and others who come to you whenever they have some problems? Would you not help us innocent gōpikās who want nothing but doing kainkaryam?

\[ [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) never dies, so ‘amarar’ refers to [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) too\]

**mun cēnṟu –** mun -\> before/voluntarily

you would go by *yourself* and help them even *before* they feel the pain; we are feeling the pain in our hearts because of missing you and can you not open the door for us?

Will you only walk for those who wanted a piece of land? ([acit](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/)).  Would you not walk and open the door for us who want a view of your beautiful divine face?

You stood in the front during kurukśētra – even in the front of great arjunan, as his charioteer.

{  
[ācāryas](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) go by themselves to siśyas for the well-being (jñānam) of siśyas and their families. [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) goes by Himself to a siśyan if the siśyan is not attending upadēsams properly.

The story about [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) walking 18 times from kōvil (srīrangam) to tirukkōśtiyūr is well known; but [tirukkośtiyūr nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/), his acaryan, walked many times from tirukkōśtiyūr to srīrangam to meet srī [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) :

– sometimes to teach him rahasyams,

– once when [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s food was poisoned by some people who did not like his reforms in the temple, he went on a fasting – as he continued that for more than 3 days, tirukkōśtiyūr [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) came rushing to srīrangam – when [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) received him near the shores of the river in the hot sand and fell on his ācāryan’s feet – [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) did not ask him to get up for quite long… [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) was waiting for nambi to tell him to get up, so he continued prostrating down in the hot sand… seeing this, rāmānujur’s siśyan [kidāmbi āchān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/kidambi-achan/) jumped under [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) to save him from heat of the sand and raised objection *to* [*nambi*](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/).. Upon hearing his words, [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) became very happy! He said that he waited so he could identify someone who is very concerned about [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s well being; and [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) asked [kidāmbi āccān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/kidambi-achan/) to be in charge of preparing safe food for [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) from then on….

– [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) arranged for [tirumālai āṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/24/thirumalai-andan/) to teach the meanings of tiruvāimoḻi to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/); but since [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) was giving different meanings to some of the pāsurams of tiruvāimoḻi while attending [tirumālai āṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/24/thirumalai-andan/)’s kālakśēpam, [tirumālai āṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/24/thirumalai-andan/) got upset and stopped the kālakśēpam for him.. Again, [nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) came from tirukkōśtiyūr to srīrangam, and convinced [tirumālai āṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/24/thirumalai-andan/) that he himself has heard those meanings from their ācāryan [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/), and also convinced him that like how kriṣṇan went to sāndīpani for learning, it is just a formality that [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) is our siśyar, said [tirukkōśtiyūr nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/).

– There are many more such vaibhavam about how ācāryas ‘cenṟu’ (went themselves and) took care of their siśyas ([maṇakkāl nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/25/manakkal-nambi/) -\> [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)**;** [kūra kuḷottama dāsa nāyan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/11/02/kura-kulothama-dhasar/) -\> [tiruvāimoḻippiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/19/thiruvaimozhi-pillai/), etc)  
}

**kappam tavirkkum**

kappam – kampam – shivering out of fear

You helped dēvas (who lost their place to asurās), rid their fear; would you not help us little gōpikās to avoid our shivering? Would you help only dēvas who wanted to get rid of their sadness? Would you not help us who wanted to see you get rid of your sleep?

\[ ācāryan helps siśyas having the above three types of desires by highlighting the wrongs of those types: For aiśvaryārtīs he advises not to follow [acit](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) (matter); for kaivalyārtis he advises that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is much much greater than the [jīvatmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/); For prapannas? What do prapannas need to avoid? He advises that prapannas should do kainkaryam but avoid thinking that it is for her/his own happiness; prapannas should do kainkaryam for [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s happiness only (may become happy at seeing [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s happiness \[*padiyāyk kidandu un pavaḷa vāi kāṇbēnē* */ படியாய்க் கிடந்து உன் பவள வாய் காண்பேனே*.\])

**kaliyē**

Your smartness and style (மிடுக்கு); your strength; for the poor and innocent gōpikās like us, that is what is lovable;

that is our protecting wall;

**tuyil eḻāi**

For dēvas it is their comfort and their places (heaven, etc) that are important; for us it is your waking up and opening the door that is important.

**seppam udaiyāy**

you who has the protection – you are our protection; you are protected by [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) (cankam, cakram; [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)); You being honest;

**tiṟal udaiyāy**

you had promised not to peruse weapon in the war against kauravas; but when bhīśmar hit arjunan, you forgot about the promise and got bhīśmar into the bed of arrows; you made day as night to give advantage to pāṇḍavas; You may show all your smartness to them, but is it fair to show that on us poor little gōpikās who just want to be with you?

**seṟṟārkku veppam kodukkum**

seṟṟār – enemies; there is none equal to or above Him; His enemies: none; but those who are his devotees’ enemies are His.

veppam kodukkum – will create fear in His enemies’ hearts.

**kodukkum vimalā**

kodukkum – kind in giving everything to His adiyārs.

vimalan – pure heart; honest – gives the best to His adiyārs – He gives Himself to them; gave trouble to duryōdanan; gave Himself to arjunan (and gave śrī bhagavat gītai).

**tuyil eḻāi**

They are saying again ‘tuyil eḻāi’ in this pāsuram as they are very desperate to see kaṇṇan.

Please wake up. No need of throwing arrows for us, just wake up.

**seppenna menmulai**

Since He did not make any movement, they again ask nappinnai pirātti (puruśakāra bhūtai \[who can make [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) realize His duty of being kind to us, and get Him and us together\]).

She (nappinnai pirātti) is the reason that He is not showing interest in us gōpikās – being with Her is His life; (seppu) where He saves all His good things. Her mountainous chest is where this lion lives in. Like how it is not easy to get jewels/money kept inside a container, it is hard to get kaṇṇan out of pirātti’s chest (*mā kadal nīr uḷḷān,* ***malarāḷ tanattu uḷḷān*** (mūnrām tiruvantādi)).

menmulai – refers to bhakti and affection. Like a mother’s affection towards her children.

Now they talk about the places where He gets trapped / where He is usually found :

**sevvāi**

With the beautiful lips by which she gets kaṇṇan for Herself; kaṇṇan fought with the bulls to marry nappinnai so that He can enjoy her smile and lips; *sem pavaḷat tiraḷ vāyan* lost to these lips of hers;

vāi refers to jñānam.

**siru marungul**

nappinnai with waist so slim that one has to guess it exists based on other parts above and below the waist; ‘*osinda nuṇṇidai mēl kaiyai vaittu*‘ (tiruvāimoḻi).

marungul refers to  vairākyam (detachment/renunciation) – determination to ‘not have much’ (like the waist) of desire on other things than kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**nappinnai nangāi**

One who is complete in all the wealth, all the wealth we mentioned and failed to mention.

**tiruvē**

nappinnāi, it is because of you that His greatness is shining.

He is caught in your beauty and is finding it hard to get out of it to save us.

We are calling you with the name of [periya pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (tiru / śrī)– we want you to help us like how She helps us to reach Him; Like how She did not have to sleep in a womb for 10 months, we want you also not to be sleeping (now).

“I am not sleeping; I am thinking about how to get Him up and give to you. Tell me why I need to get up and what I need to do for you”

“so then here are what we need:”

**ukkamum tattōḷiyum**

ukkam – Ala vattam (hand-held fan)

tattu oḷi – mirror

also paṟai (instrument),

and other equipment needed for our nōnbu.

\[ācāryan gives us upadēsam to reduce the angst of ātman, and the upadēsams act as clear as our seeing the face in a mirror\]

Also implies removal of ahankāra mamakāram (me/mine) in kainkaryam, and svarūpa jñānam.

**un maṇāḷanai**

(also (by-the-by) give your sweet husband) (include Him in the list of things to give us)

Your husband who obediently follows and does all the chores for you just by looking at the place you look towards with your raised eyebrows – we need your nice husband also as part of nōnbu.

\[ācāryan’s nātan is [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), we ask ācāryan too to give [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)\]

They are asking for kriṣṇan as if He is equivalent to a fan, a mirror, or an instrument. When visvāmitrar asked cakravarty to send perumāḷ with him to fight with the rākśasas, vālmiki says that with the advice of his ācāryan vaśiśtar, this cakravarty ‘gave’ perumāḷ to the riśi – as if He is an [acit](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) (thing/non-sentient) – both of these imply that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) makes Himself so easily available to His adiyārs.

**ippōdē**

we cannot wait for some other time; now itself;

**emmai –** us

They are pointing to their bodies that are destitute because of not seeing kaṇṇan; though they differentiated the (representative number of ) 10 gōpikās with ‘[*pēyp peṇṇē*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=424)*‘, ‘*[*nāyagap pēṇ piḷḷāi*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=424)*‘,* etc., they are united and one here (emmai) when it came to having nīrāttam with kaṇṇan.

**nīrāttu**

Please let us enjoy with kriṣṇan; what they are happy about is kriṣṇan – the one that nappinnai gives to them. When kriṣṇan’s beauty and affection is there, they are happy that it is nappinnai who gave Her kriṣṇan to them.

\[Immediately help us to get ācārya abhimānam (liking by / blessing from, ācāryan)\]

In this pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) wakes up kriṣṇan and nappinnai; she requests nappinnai to give the items used for nōnbu and also give kriṣṇan and let them enjoy with Him. She goes through nappinnai as per the norm of getting the help of ācāryan/devotees to reach out to emperumān.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 21. Erra kalangal

*eṟṟa kalangaḷ edir pongi mīdaḷippa māṟṟādē pāl soriyum vaḷḷal perum pasukkaḷ āṟṟap padaittān maganē aṟivurāi! ūṟṟam udaiyāy! periyāy! ulaginil tōṟṟamāi ninṟa sudarē! tuyil eḻāi maṟṟār unakku vali tolaindu un vāsaṟkaṇ āṟṟādu vandu un adi paṇiyumāpōle pōṟṟi yām vandōm pugaḻndu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaḷḷal – generous; perum pasukkaḷ – big cows; pāl soriyum – can produce out milk; ēṟṟa kalangaḷ – (such that) all the containers placed to get the milk (that is produced out automatically); edir pongi – get filled out; mīdu aḷippa – and pour outside; māṟṟādē – without any break; āṟṟap padaittān maganē – son of such a person having such cows; aṟivuṟāi – please wake up (tiruppaḷḷi uṇara vēnum / தி்ருப்பள்ளியுணரவேணும்); ūṟṟam udaiyāy – vēdas which are the top most reference/pramānam talks about your having the determination / enthusiasm; periyāy – (you) having the greatness (which the vēdās are not able to completely determine); tōṟṟam āy ninṟa – did avatāram (for everyone to see); ulaginil – in this world; sudarē – and are very bright in appearance; tuyil eḻāi – please wake up from sleep; māṟṟār – your enemies; unakku vali tolaindu – lose (their) strength because of you; āṟṟādu vandu – came without any other place/person to go to; un vāsal kaṇ – to the entrance of your tirumāḷigai (house); un adi paṇiyum A pōlē – and be praying unto your divine feet, (in the same way); yām – we; vandōm – have come and reached (your tirumāḷigai entrance); pugaḻndu – praising (you); pōṟṟi – and doing mangaḷāsāsanam (to you)

**Перевод:**

**ēṟṟa kalangaḷ edir pongi mīdaḷippa**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram they list what those who are lost to his beautiful guṇams would say, and ask him to wake up.*

Any container kept under it will get filled up so quickly; there may be a limit to the number of containers, but there is no limit to the milk from them; it would even fill the sea; these cows have been touched by kriṣṇan and they are like him – kriṣṇan would bless everyone; it is their mistake if someone doesn’t accept it well; He wants everyone.

ēṟṟa kalangaḷ – siśyās who are amicable to receive upadēsam (advise/rahasyams); he who is qualified to receive; qualification – the thought that He is the means.

edir pongi mīdaḷippa – siśyas have gained full knowledge due to ācāryan, such that the knowledge pours out to the extent that people would say they are better than ācāryan himself.

This makes ācāryan say to siśyan like – “*vaḷarttadanāl payan peṟṟēn varugavenṟu madakkiḷiyaik kai kūppi vaṇangināḷē – வளர்த்ததனால் பயன் பெற்றேன் வருகவென்று மடக்கிளியைக் கைகூப்பி வணங்கினாளே*“.

(kānci svāmi’s): Even after arjunan was convinced during gītai, [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) kept him listening and continued the flow of upadēsam. ārjunan did not say ‘don’t continue’, so kriṣṇan used that as acceptance and continued.

**maṟṟādē pāl soriyum**

Even if someone does not put the container, it would not hesitate – would continue giving milk.

Even when a siśyan/bhaktan is not interested, ācaryān/[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would continue giving their blessing/knowledge.

[ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) follow and advise what they heard from their ācāryas, they do not change (*māṟṟādē*).  *munnōr moḻinda muṟai tappāmal kēttu / முன்னோர் மொழிந்த முறை தப்பாமல் கேட்டு பின்னோர்ந்து*

**vaḷḷal perum pasukkaḷ**

Cows are not helping just a few, but helping as if doing for nandagōpa’s own benefit. Like how kriṣṇan made it easy for women and girls to hug him;

these cows are obedient like kriṣṇan;

perum pasukkaḷ – with kriṣṇan’s touch they are also big – like kriṣṇan; like satrunjayan the elephant of cakravarty tirumagan. [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) says ‘*sirumāmanisar*‘ in tiruvāimoḻi 8-10-3.

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) thinks his adiyārs are vaḷḷal (udārar / generous) for allowing Him to get the name that he gives everything that his adiyārs pray for. Someone who is always looking to provide tadiyārādanam would praise if someone is available to eat.

vaḷḷāl – āḻvār =\> *enakkē tanait tanda kaṟpagam* – He provided jñānam to me, he attracted and made me His, and now He is thankful to me as if adiyēn did all these for Him.

**āṟṟap padaittān**

nandagōpa, who has got fulfilling wealth (that is kriṣṇan);

wealth of countless number of cows. We could count His kalyāṇa guṇams, but not nadagōpa’s cows.

[āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) had lots of wonderful siśyas like [periya nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/periya-nambi/), [tirumalai nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/01/periya-thirumalai-nambi/), [tirukkōśtiyūr nambi,](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/27/thirukkoshtiyur-nambi/) [tirumālaiyāṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/02/24/thirumalai-andan/), and more. So did [piḷḷai lōkācāriar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/18/pillai-lokacharyar/).

**padaittān maganē**

The wealth nandagōpa got working hard, kriṣṇan got because of his birth to him.

maganē – calling kriṣṇan who is the son (to get up).

kriṣṇan is happy to be known as a son and not as nārāyaṇan. [pūrvācāryārs](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) also call perumāḷ as cakravarty tirumagan because that is how He liked to be called. He enjoys saulabhyam (simplicity) more than paratvam (supremacy). Since as nārāyaṇan He does not have a father (தான் தோன்றித்தனம்), he is more happy to be associated with the father he got during his avatāram.

\[maganē – refers to an ācāryān. āṟṟap padaittān is ācāryān’s ācāryān (periya svāmi)\].

ācāryān becomes so much happy if his siśyan shows greater jñānam and anuśtānam; [emperumānār](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-emperumanar.html) -\> [āḻvān](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-kurathazhwan.html) (example, while returning from kāśmīr), [mudaliyāṇḍān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/29/mudhaliyandan/), and [piḷḷān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/04/14/thirukkurugaippiran-pillan-english/);   [embār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/07/embar/) -\> [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/);  [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/)-\>[nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/);  [nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/)-\>[nampiḷḷai](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/) …. [maṇavāḷa māmunigaḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/) who was the only ācāryān who was in a position to learn āḷḷ of their [pūrvācārya’s grantams](http://granthams.koyil.org//), because of which [namperumāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) waited till then, and wanted to be [māmunigaḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/)‘s siśyan.

edir pongi-\> siśyar knowing and following more than his ācāryān.\]

**aṟivurāi**

Since He is in yōga nidrā it is easy to awaken him (not actually sleeping, but enjoying about the innocent gōpikās that he already befriended, and deeply thinking about any remaining gōpikās).

He thought, ‘these cows were there even before I came here, they are saying that they are like this because of my birth; does this say anything great about me’, and so he was lying down and not moving.

Also they thought that he might think that they are talking about some other gōpar who owns a lot of cows; so they decide to talk about his greatness in other ways..

**ūṟṟam udaiyāy**

you who is identified in the vēdas; whom yasōdai is fond of;

when you wanted to accept vibhīśaṇan, you convinced mahārājar (sukrīvan) and others, and accomplished that.  vibhīśaṇan is like anṟu īnṟa kanṟu (new calf born on that day) so he was more determined to accept him, against mahārājar.

\[ācāryāns’ enthusiasm to teach their siśyas\]

**periyāy**

how much ever vēdas tried to explain you fully, they finally gave up – you are bigger than anything.

Even after doing everything for your saraṇāgatas, you look as if you did not do much; as if it is your pleasure/opportunity; blesses us with the greatness that matches his greatness.

**ulaginil tōṟṟamāi ninṟa**

but without just stopping with the knowledge that no one can fully explain about you as said in the vēdas, you decided to come to this world to help the samsāris, for sādu paritrāṇam, as you said ‘*sambhavāmi*‘. You came to this world for everyone to see.

You not only are, but you also make it famous about being, partial to those who surrendered to you;

Like how you were partial to pāṇḍavas because they depended on you and you made that clear to duryōdanan and his people and to this world.

**sudarē**

this guṇam (characteristic) of yours shows brightly as/after you came to this world, unlike that of samsāris’ births which make [ātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) more and more covered with karmās.

**tuyil eḻāi**

please wake up, don’t let your wonderful guṇam sung as above become dull because of your sleep.

**māṟṟār unakku vali tolaindu un vāsal kaṇ vandu adi paṇiyumā pōlē**

maṟṟār – your enemies, that is those who are enemies of your devotees

vali – strength

You make your enemies lose their strength and come fall on your feet; they would not want to go back after seeing you. They may die and thus be saved (from further agony / to go to next birth/mokśam, etc).

We are lost to your beauty and guṇams, and we also come and fall on your feet; but we cannot be saved if you don’t show us your face;

svāpadēsam – we are lost to your sēśitvam (lordship), got rid of dēhātbhimānam (giving importance to [dēham](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) as if it is [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)), svasvātantriyam (behaving as if independent of you), anya sēśatvam (serving others), svādīna-kartrutva-bhōktrutvam (independent efforts and self enjoyment), upāyantaram (using other means to reach you), and upēyāntaram (aiming for other benefits/goals); even if you ask us to take up any of these again, we think of the sadness/emptiness of it all and say “we don’t want them”, and come to the entrance of your tirumāḷigai, and say you are all kinds of means and goals for us, and so praise your lotus feet, and we came to do mangaḷāsāsanam to you and surrender to you.

Also, people of other philosophies lose their strength after debate with sampradāya ācāryas and come to their tirumāḷigai.

**pōṟṟi**

Singing tirupallaṇḍu for you (may you live forever).  We came like [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/).

**yām vandōm**

Like how he ([periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)) did not worry about his living but sang for you, we also did not worry that we should wait for you to come and bless us, but we came here to see you; we could not wait in our place because we could not bear the separation.

**pugaḻndu**

(we came) while praising your guṇams that made us lose to you.

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 22. Anganma jnalattu

*angaṇmā jñālattu arasar abhimāna bhangamāi vandu nin paḷḷikkattil kīḻē sangam iruppār pōl vandu talaippeidōm kingiṇi vāic ceida tāmaraippūp pōlē sengaṇ siṟuciṟidē emmēl viḻiyāvō tingaḷum ādittiyanum eḻundār pōl angaṇ iraṇḍum kōndu engaḷ mēl nōkkudiyēl engaḷ mēl sābham iḻindu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

am kaṇ mā jñālattu arasar – kings (who rule(d) the) beautiful, spacious, and big world; abhimāna bhangamāi vandu – come after losing their ahankāram; nin paḷḷik kattil kīḻē – below your throne; pōl – likewise; vandu – (we) came (to your place); talaippeidōm – and approached (you); tāmaṟai pū pōlē – like a lotus; kingiṇi vāic ceida – that is blossomed a little bit, like the opening of ‘kingiṇi’ (metal ball with openings like a half-open flower, with a metal ball inside it to make noise when shaken); sem kaṇ – (would you open your) reddish eyes; siṟucciṟidē – little by little; em mēl viḻyāvō – and bless us (your devotees)?; tingaḷum ādittiyanum eḻundār pōl – like how the moon and the sun rise; engaḷ mēl nōkkudiyēl – if you would bless us; am kaṇ iraṇḍum koṇḍu – with the two beautiful eyes; engaḷ mēl sābham – our sadness; iḻindu – would get destroyed

**Перевод:**

**angaṇ – beautiful place; nice that from brahma to a small ant (pipīlikai/பிபீலிகை) could enjoy here with all comforts;**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The previous pāsuram was about losing ahankāram (attitude of me-mine) – (māṟṟār vali tolaindu un vāsal kaṇ). This pāsuram says that after coming here to you there is no other place / person to go to. This is about ananyārha sēśatvam (subservience to perumān and perumān only / no other place or person to go to).*

**mā jñālattu arasar –** kings of the beautiful big places on earth/world – kings with the pride of ‘all these areas of this earth are mine’; like a king named pouṇḍarakan who ruled kāsi who thought that the whole universe was his and was walking with cangu and cakram in his hands to compete with kriṣṇan.

**abhimāna bhangamāi vandu –** losing their svātantriyam; those kings come (to you) after losing their kingdom, and become humble;

some kings were bhāgavatas (some of whom are also mentioned in some of the divya prabandams), and they came and surrendered to you even while being kings; [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) surrendered to [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) even when having everything (jñānam, lineage, wealth). [kulasēkarāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/18/kulasekara-azhwar/) and sīrk [kaliyan](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/23/thirumangai-azhwar/) too were kings!

**nin paḷḷik kattil kīḻē –** even if they (the former types) are given back their kingdoms, they recall becoming humble and refuse to go away (from you), and stay under you (‘under’ your safety). They want you and you only.

**sangam iruppār pōl –** like those great kings we have also come to you after losing our pāratantriyam – like how significant it is for them to lose their pāratantriyam (because it is very hard for such great kings), we have come losing ours; they came to you because there was no other place to go to – we came to you for doing kainkaryam.

Like iḷaiyaperumāḷ who was lost to cakravarty tirumagan, we have come here after losing to your guṇams and becoming your sēśans.

**vandu talaip peidōm**

All the delays we endured till now in waking up every one, now we are relieved of all the troubles after coming here. All these years (lives) we had lost the opportunity of this sambandam (association) with you, and today we got the complete you after coming here, and without doing anything on our part (nirhētukam). We have shed our dēhātmābhimānam (giving importance to bodily comforts), also our svātantriyam (feeling of we can do everything by ourselves without His help) and have come here as saraṇāgatas to do kainkaryam only to you; and without any thought that it is We who Did the kainkaryams.

When [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) was asked, “vēdas say that brahmā and other dēvas are born 7 times, they do very hard penance/tasks before they are able to get mōkśam; but we, who are so less able compared to them, have just done saraṇāgati and with all surety we are waiting for this life to end for going to mōkśam (like we have packed our bags and are just waiting for His call, to go to mōkśam); how come there is such a big difference in the required efforts?”

[bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) replied, “There is nothing wrong in this; those dēvas are trying everything out of their own efforts, and they want to try like that because/thinking-that they have got that ability. We, on the other hand, have accepted from the beginning that we cannot do anything on our own to become eligible for mōkśam, we have fallen on [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s feet and have told Him that we don’t have anything to show for becoming eligible – so, because it is now [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is taking the efforts to provide us mōkśam, we are assured of it; but the dēvas / riśis – they are trying with their own ability so it would take them a long time before they get mōkśam.

{ முமுக்ஷுப்படி – ஒரு பலத்துக்கு அரிய வழியையும் எளிய வழியையும் உபதேசிக்கையாலே, இவை இரண்டுமொழிய பகவத் ப்ரஸாதமே உபாயமாகக் கடவது / mumukśuppadi – oru palattukku ariya vaḻiaiyum eḷiya vaḻaiyum upadēsikkaiyālē, ivai iraṇḍumoḻiya bhagavat prasādamē upāyamāgak kadavadu. }

(Also we are not supposed to go for other means (karma/jñāna/bhakti) for getting mōkśam; only śaraṇāgati is prescribed for us śrīvaiṣṇavas because it is against the svarūpam (our true nature) to try on our own even if we would be able to. Doing śaraṇāgaty is also not the ‘means’, it is only an acceptance that He is the means.)

**kingiṇi vāic ceida tāmaraip pūp pōlē**

(please open your eyes) like the lotus that blossoms when seeing the sun; these flowers blossom seeing our desperation; like how it slowly blossoms;

our desperation is like the sun, and you eyes are like the lotus – please open seeing our desperation.

**sengaṇ –** your beautiful reddish eyes

red due to your affection towards us. Even that flower is not as red/beautiful as His eyes, so they use the word as a metaphor.

**siṟuc ciṟidē**

unlike flooding by suddenly opening a dam, the force of your kindness which we cannot withstand if it is available all at once, open your beautiful eyes slowly.. slowly..

We want to enjoy from the beginning the whole process of your opening your beautiful eyes;

we are like dry land which needs to be watered slowly and steadily.

svāpadēsam: please elevate us step by step; begin with:  
– remove our dēhātmābhimānam (thinking of dēham as ātma – attachment/priority to physical comforts),  
– as we *accept* it (*accept this effort of yours*), remove our svasvātantriya abhimānam (thinking that we are independent of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), i.e., thinking that we can do some of the things He has forbidden, and that we need not do some of the things He has commanded us to do),  
– as we *accept* it, do anya sēśatva nivrutti (subservience to others),  
– as we *accept* it, remove sva sēśatvam (kaivalyam – enjoying our own ātmā only),  
– as we *accept* it, remove svarakśaṇē sva anvayam (thinking that we can manage to do things to protect ourselves),  
– as we *accept* it, remove us from upāyāntaram (using other means like karma/jñāna/bhakti yōgams, or other ways, to reach Him),  
– as we *accept* it, make us come to you as the only means (to reach you),  
– as we *accept* it, remove the thought in us that when we carry out activities in our life it is because of our efforts only,  
– as we *accept* it, make us be a paratantran (subservient to Him),  
– as we *accept* it, make us immersed in all of His auspicious qualities (ஸமஸ்த கல்யாண குணங்கள்),  
– then create anubhava-janita pritikārita kainkaryam (doing kainkaryam out of affection that came out of experiencing Him),  
– and then remove the thought of doing that kainkaryam for own enjoyment.  
— In the next phrase of the pāsuram when they say ‘after you open your eyes just enough’ – it means that ‘after we reach such stage here’. To a dēhātmābhimāni it would not work if He tries to grant parama bhakti straight away, would it?

**em mēl viḻiyāvō –** would you open your eyes and see us?

Like how desperate one would be when rains came for a dry land, like how the sādaga bird would hope to get rain water (it drinks only rain water direct when the rain drops fall from the sky), they are hoping for His blessings. {*turisaṟṟuc cādakam pōl nātan tandu aruḷē pārttiruttal kōdil adiyār guṇam* \[*jñāna sāram-2*\]  */ துரிசற்றுச் சாதகம் போல் நாதன் தனதருளே பார்த்திருத்தல் கோதிலடியார் குணம் –* \[ஞான ஸாரம்-2\]}

Would you please give us parama bhakti after we reached the stage as above?

**tingaḷum ādittiyanum eḻundār pōl**

ādittiyan – sun – hot – your enemies cannot go near you

tingaḷ – moon – nice – your adiyārs find it nice and wonderful to approach you; they find the moon (un)comfortable when they miss you.

Even though [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was fighting hiraṇyan, He was kind to his son prahlādan. [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) considered hiraṇyan as enemy only because hiraṇyan was troubling his bhaktan. [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) said: Even while the mother lion is fighting with an elephant it would be kind to its cub which is drinking milk from her.

Also means, to remove our inner darkness (sun), and to make us feel better after having gone through separation from you (moon).

**angaṇ iraṇḍum kōndu**

the sun and moon are not comparable to your anger and blessing;

so they stop comparing and mention his eyes itself – am kaṇ – beautiful eyes; with your wonderful eyes (please see us)

As He opened just enough for them to to be able to bear it (where they had said *siruc ciṟidē*), they ask Him to look at them with both his eyes.

iraṇḍum kōṇḍu – see with both the eyes – emphasizing with ‘both’ to imply that He should see them fully with all of His kindness and love. (*“நான் என் ரெண்டு கண்ணால பார்த்தேன்*” / I saw with both my eyes).

**engaḷ mēl**

See us – we who became desperate without your looking at us

**nōkkudiyēl**

īF Yōū see us – it is not possible with our efforts – You with your efforts please see us and accept us.

**engaḷ mēl sābham iḻindu**

sābham – curse, or, what we HāVē to go through – because of your playing with us but leaving us and hiding; and we cannot tolerate your absence. Separation from you is our problem; since this trouble is having to be undergone, they use the word ‘sābham’.

With your sight falling on us, our troubles from samsāram will go away;

With those two beautiful eyes if you see us slowly but fully, our troubles will go away.

They mention thrice – em mēḷ, engaḷ mēḷ, engaḷ mēl in this one pāsuram itself (lines 5, 7, and 8) – the ’em’ (we) in these lines relate to us who have got *‘poi ninṟa jñānamum, pollā oḻukkum, aḻukkudambum’* \[tiruviruttam-1\].

In this pāsuram, after kriṣṇan opened His eyes, they told Him that they came to get His blessings to serve Him forever.

We were having svātantriyam the size that of great kings and emperors.  
After great difficulty we have lost all that and are here falling at your feet – ananyārha sēśatvam.  
If you would please slowly but fully open your kind eyes to look at us and give your blessings,  
then we will get the ever-lasting opportunity to always do kainkaryams to you.  
We request Him to allow us to do kainkaryam to Him and Him only, so that we don’t gain svātantriyam after doing kainkaryam (not having the feeling that We Did It).

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 23. Mari malai mulainjil

*māri malai muḻainjil mannik kidandu uṟangum sīriya singam aṟivuṟṟut tī viḻittu vēri mayir ponga eppādum pērndu udari mūri nimirndu muḻangip puṟappattup pōdaruma pōlē nī pūvaippū vaṇṇā un kōyil ninṟu inganē pōndu aruḷik kōppudaiya sīriya singasanattu irundu yām vanda kāriyam ārāyndu aruḷ ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

māri – During the rainy season; malai muḻainjil – in a mountain cave; sīriya singam – majestic/angry lion; mannk kidandu – which is deeply set (together with the lioness) and; uṟangum – sleeping; aṟivuṟṟu – (would) get up after waking up (after that season); tī viḻittu – open the flaming fiery eyes; pērndu – and move; eppādum – on all sides; udaṟi – and shake (its body); vēri mayir – (such that its) fragrant mane (hair); ponga – (would) rise around; mūri nimirndu – (then it would) stand erect and straighten up its body, and; muḻangi – roar, and; puṟappattu – exit (the den); pōdarum A pōlē – and come out; pūvaip pū vaṇṇā – (likewise,) Oh you who is of the color like kāyāmpū (flower); nī – you; un koyil ninṟu – (exit) from your temple (bed room); inganē pōndaruḷi – and come to this place; irundu – and be seated; sīriya singāsanattu – on the majestic throne; kōppu udaiya – that is having nice shape and set up; ārāyndu aruḷ – (and then) inquire about and fulfill; yām vanda kāriyam – the requirements for which we have come

**Перевод:**

**māri malai muḻainjil**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*At this, the gopikās request kriṣṇan to listen to their requirements.*

Like the kings who stay in a place and not go for wars during rainy season, the lion would stay in the caves and not go out during that time.

Like how perumāḷ (srī rāman) stayed in the cave of mālyavān during rainy season while sugrīvan stayed with his family, such rainy season is for the lovers to meet, and for the ones who have met to enjoy their time together; this time is also such, but we are at your door with the longing; you are wasting the minutes instead of to us and helping us join you.

**mannik kidandu uṟangum**

Since you don’t have to be afraid of anyone, you are deeply set in your bed, like the peak is well set on the mountain.

You are set in your bed together with your wife nappinnai pirātti (like how they said earlier ‘[*nappinnai kongai mēl vaittu*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=656) [***kidanda***](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=656) [*malar mārbhā*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=656)‘), as if there is only one thing together,  or, like you are an ornament on nappinnai pirātti.

uṟangum – unlike samsāris who really sleep, you are just closing your eyes deeply thinking about ways of helping the samsāris. They are reminded of such a sleep here.

[ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) and srīvaiṣṇavas study vēdas, upaniśads, [divya prabandams](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/), [grantams](http://granthams.koyil.org//) all their life, dwelling in their meanings.

**sīriya singam**

Lion having the greatness of valor, such that it is feared by other animals even when it is sleeping. (as enjoyed by pirātti), perumāḷ (srī rāman) is majestic even while sleeping. They are reminded of such a sleep here.

**aṟivuṟṟu**

samsāris wake up when realizing it is time; you wake up to accept adiyārs’ kainkaryam.

**tī viḻittu**

When the lion first opens its flaming eyes it is scary even for the lioness to look at it. They imply that in front of the tējas of [īsvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) the enemies cannot stand in front of Him.

**vēri mayir ponga**

vēri mayir – fragrance (from your hair, smell that is present in lions)

ponga – shaking your hair;

They are reminded about [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is described as *sarva ganda:* (his body contains all types of fragrances).

**eppādum pērndu udari**

Since you don’t have to put any effort for doing anything, leisurely you shake your head (and get rid of sleepiness).

udari- shaking various parts of your body.

**mūri nimirndu**

Straightening out all parts of your body together

**muḻangi**

Hearing your roar all other animals (enemies) would fear for their life.

Please get up and come with all your power in front of us little girls, and ask us ‘Oh have you girls come here?’ (“*பெண்காள்! வந்திகோளா*“)

“Like a lion that would straighten up its body and start its day afresh, kriṣṇā! you come with full energy/enthusiasm and open your door for us”;

“Is lion the right comparison for me?”;

**nī pūvaippū vaṇṇā**

We compared it to you for its majesty, but, it does not match your beauty and color – those qualities of yours are like the flower (called pūvaippū – blackish/bluish colored flower).  īsn’t only when the flower’s color would be an exact match to you, then the lion also would be an exact match to you?

**un kōilninru inganē pōndu aruḷi**

please walk from your bed room (from anta:puram) (to your throne)

we want to see the four great types of your walk – your walk that is like a Bull’s pride, an Elephant’s respect, Tiger’s anger, and Lion’s charisma. We can see these in the beauty of namperumāḷ’s walk / *நமக்கு இவையெல்லாம் நம்பெருமாள் நடையழகிலே காணலாம்*.

ingane pōndu aruḷi – not become the words told from the bed – which can be lies; we want the words from your majestic throne in your big reception.

**kōppudaiya**

the seat that you use for taking care of both the worlds, and for all beings.

ōr, having the eight legs of darmam, jñānam, vairājñam, aisvaryam (of directing everything),  (and their opposites) adarmam, ajñānam, avairājñam, anaisvaryam.

**sīriya singāsanattu**

Any decisions made from your throne will be final and good – like your words in the sea shore (cakravarty tirumagan’s words during vibīśaṇa saraṇāgaty that he would protect (give mōkśam) for all the related people of the one who does saraṇāgaty once), and from the charioteer’s seat (sarva darmān… by kriṣṇan to arjunan).  — Words from your throne would be wonderful / effective even with just kriṣṇan and all the gōpikās too.

sīriya – even if it is for someone very small the size of an atom, this throne would give then equal greatness of kriṣṇan.  even kriṣṇan cannot tell lies when seated on this.

For [ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/), the singāsanam (simhāsanam) is the wooden seat they use when giving blessings to their siśyas; [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) establśed 74 ‘simhāsanādipatis’ ([ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/)). [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) is like a lion to those opposed to [srīvaiṣṇavam](https://koyil.org/) / visiśtādvaitam;

**irundu**

In addition to enjoying the beauty of your walk, we want to see the beauty of your sitting in your royal throne. To relax ourselves from our angst we want to see you sitting and giving your word. In tirukurunkudi perumān is in standing position, but [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) sings ‘*kaṇṇiṇai kuḷirap pudu malar āgattaip paruga – irundidāi*‘ (tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-9), referring to sitting position.

**yām vanda kāriyam ārāyndu aruḷ**

Hear about the reasons for our coming here – reasons that are not for our own material benefits; and bless for our success in attaining what we wanted.

cakravarty tirumagan felt bad for not helping the riśis even earlier; kriṣṇan too feels bad that he did not quickly help these gōpikās; From his bed, he asks them “what do I need to do for you gōpikās?”

“We cannot tell you while you are lying in your bed – because one could lie when they are lying in their bed. We don’t have anybody else to go to.  So you please come and sit relaxed in your wonderful throne and hear us out”.

“When there is nappinnai pirātti for you, you say that you don’t have any other place to go to”, he feels bad that he did not hear them out early.

\[We go to ācāryan for what kāriyam – for getting jñānam; for bhagavat samāśrayaṇam (surrender)\].

\[[emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s siśya [kidāmbi āccān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/kidambi-achan/) went to tirumāliruncōlai to do mangalāsāsanam to aḻagar perumān. aḻagar asked him to recite a slōkam – [kidāmbi āccān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/kidambi-achan/) recited a slōkam from [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)‘s stōtra ratnam – when he recited the word ‘refugee’ in that stōtram, aḻagar objected to it and said that when there is [rāmānujār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) for all of you {us?!}, one should not use that word anymore. “*நம் இராமாநுசனை ஆசார்யராக உடையாரில் ஒருவரும் அகதியில்லை காணும்”, “நம்மிராமாநுசனையுடையாயிருந்துவைத்து நம்முன்னே இப்படி சொல்லப்பெறாய்” / nam irāmanusanai ācāryarāga udaiyāril oruvarum agadi illai kāṇum”, “nam irāmānusanai udaiyarāi irundu vaittu nam munnē ippadi sollap peṟāi”*.

There is another aitīhyam where a siśyan himself did not want to use similar words after getting his ācāryan’s gatākśam (blessing): In [āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/)‘s stōtram, [ālavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) had said very low of himself as part of being a sēśan to perumān; But [anantāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/03/31/ananthazhwan/) did not wish to recite that stōtram to imply the same about himself because he thought that as a siśyan, with his ācārya sambhandam, those things would not be applicable to a siśyan!)\]

In this pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) does not give kriṣṇan the details of their kāriyam (their requirements). “*She has kept that for ciṟṟam siṟu kālē (29) pāsuram” (வந்த காரியத்தை சிற்றஞ்சிறு காலைக்கு வைக்கிறார்*).

Why are they not talking about it immediately but are keeping it for later? bharatāḻvān along with all the people of ayōdyā went to cakravarty tirumagan thinking that if we all went to him and fell on his feet then He would be kind and accept his request to go back to ayōdyā; but since perumāḷ wanted to fulfill his father’s words / busy, this request was not fulfilled immediately – He gave them His pādukas only. So [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) thinks that she would not hurry but use appropriate time and make sure that kriṣṇan’s disposition is suitable for her to place her request.

ārāyndu aruḷ – kriṣṇan should ask “Oh how much you suffered, what all have you done!! You woke up so early in this cold morning, argued with all the gōpikās and woke them all up, woke up nandagōpar, yasōdai, elder brother, nappinnai and me!? your going through all this trouble is not fair on my part, I should have been the first to come and help you”. *எதிர் சூழல் புக்குத் திரியுமவனுக்கு, இவையெல்லாம் தன் குறையாகத் தோற்றுமிறே / edi sūḻal pukkut tiriyumavanukku ivaiyellām than kuṟaiyāgat tōṟṟumiṟē* – For him who always plans to surround us and make us (accept that we are) His, and who has come from paramapadam to here looking for acceptance of Him from us, it would feel like a big mistake to have let gōpikas make all these efforts.

More then his providing what they require, they enjoy kriṣṇan inquiring them like this.

“You and nappinnai pirātti please go and be seated in the throne and listen to the reason of our visit and hear our requirements”.

**ēlōrempāvāi** – commit and follow our ways.

In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) and other gōpikās finally see kriṣṇan who has slowly opened his eyes and seen them. He wants to know what they wanted, but they request him and pirātti to go and be seated in the throne and listen to their requirements. She describes how he should get up and walk – the walk that would be so enjoyable to see (we can see that walk even today in tiruvarangam – namperumāḷ)

This pāsuram advises us too to go to our ācāryan who is deeply knowledgeable about vēdas, upanishads, prabhandams, etc., and to request him to give us jñānam which he will do sitting in his āsanam (seat).

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 24. Anru ivvulagam

*anṟu ivvulagam aḷandāi adi pōṟṟi senṟangut ten ilangai ceṟṟāi tiṟal pōṟṟi ponṟac cakatam udaittāi pugaḻ pōṟṟi kanṟu kuṇilāi eṟindāi kaḻal pōṟṟi kunṟu kudaiyāi eduttāi guṇam pōṟṟi venṟu pagai kedukkum nin kaiyil vēl pōṟṟi enṟenṟun sēvagamē ēttip paṟai koḷvān inṟu yām vandōm iṟangu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – long ago that time (when indiran and others suffered due to mahābali); aḷandāi ivvulagam – Oh you who measured these worlds! (with two steps); adi – (those) divine feet (of yours); pōṟṟi – shall live for many years and more years (pallāṇḍu pallāṇḍu); angu – in the place where rāvaṇan lived; senṟu – (you) went (எழுந்தருளி); ceṟṟāi then ilangai – Oh you who destroyed the lanka of the south! (which is his country); tiṟal – (your) strength; pōṟṟi – shall live for many years; cakatam – cakatāsuran; udaittāi cakatam ponṟa – Oh you, who kicked that cakatam (cakatāsuran) and destroyed!; pugaḻ – (your) valor; pōṟṟi – shall live for a long time; kanṟu – vatsāsuran who stood as a calf; kuṇilā – was used as a throwing staff (pole); eṟindāi – Oh you who threw (at the asura who took the form of a fruit (viḷānkani / விளாங்கனி) (எறிந்தருளியவனே!); kaḻal – your tiruvadi (lotus feet – which stood ground); pōṟṟi – shall live for a long time; eduttāi – Oh you who lifted; kunṟu – gōvardana mountain; kudaiyā – as an umbrella / shelter!; guṇam – your characteristics (like sauseelyam, etc.,); pōṟṟi – should stay for years together; nin kaiyil vēl – the spear in your hands; venṟu – that won (the enemies); pagai kedukkum – and destroyed them (the opposition); pōṟṟi – should live for a long time; enṟu enṟu – So doing such mangaḷāsāsanam several times; ētti – to praise / stōtram; un sēvagamē – about only your strength/valor; yām – we; vandōm – came and reached; ingu/inṟu – this place / today; paṟai koḷvān – to get paṟai (instrument (mōkśam)); irangu – please show compassion (to help us)

**Перевод:**

**anṟu ivvulagam aḷandāi adi pōṟṟi**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*As kriṣṇan sits on the throne with nappinnai pirātti, they touch His lotus feet and pray to Him. Seeing his soft feet, they say that long ago for the dēvas’ own needs these feet walked and measured the worlds, and today we too asked him to get up and walk and have we become same as those dēvas in making him walk? – They sing pallāṇḍu to him – pōṟṟi.*

– (pallāṇḍu to your feet which measured all the worlds that day (long ago))

anṟu – that day (long ago) – when indiran was suffering after king mahābali stole the worlds from him (they are actually yours)

inṟu – today – when we are suffering because of separation from you; when we got out of our strītvābhimānam (from the thought that as woman one should not go to you (you should have come to us))

anṟu – when you so easily made yourself available to everyone (as trivikraman) by touching everyone’s head – you did dūḷi dānam to them all (when giving lots of things to people one would shake their hands to give all the remaining small particles sticking in their hands too);

inṟu – today you have to do dūḷi dānam to us your beauty and grace; that is how kriṣṇan and vāmanan would be similar.

ivvulagam aḷandāi – [pirāttis](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) would carefully touch your tiruvadi (lotus feet) treating them like flowers, but you took pains to measure all of this world – this world containing rugged land! That day you walked to mahābali and got back the worlds for indiran, and today you are walking for us, saving us from our separation from you.

adi pōṟṟi – just by our requesting Him, kriṣṇan is walking to his royal singhāsanam using his sukumāramāna tiruvadi (soft and pampered feet) – they are singing pallāṇḍu for his tiruvadi for walking to mahābali and measuring the worlds;

you took only two steps when you measured the worlds, but today you are taking 8-10 steps for us! (பத்தெட்டடி). When you measured the worlds that day, indiran went back with some benefit (other than you), mahābali got your generosity; we did not get anything (that is, you) that day, so to reduce that angst we sing pallāṇḍu today.

adi pōṟṟi – they are of the type who say ‘*un sevvadi sevvi tirukkāppu*‘ (tiruppallāṇḍu – 1).

**senṟu angu then ilangai ceṟṟāi tiṟal pōṟṟi**

That is, whom you could not win by your beauty you won by your arrows.

senṟu angu – like going to the place where a tiger is sleeping and then waking it up and killing it, you went there to the place where that fellow was residing who separated pirātti from you.

senṟu – also, while on his way to ilangai,  like (easily) walking over sand castles and destroying them, he killed other asuras like karan, kabandan, and the like who were troubling the sanyāsis in the forest.

The gōpikās stare at kaikēyi who caused that walk by foot.

angu – unlike trivikraman standing in one place and measuring all the worlds like how a flower would blossom standing in one place, you walked in the dangerous and rough forest with your soft lotus feet?! “We are even more concerned about this than the trivikrama avatāram”; “*எவ்வாறு நடந்தனை எம் இராமா! ஓ!” / evvāṟu nadandanai em irāmā! O!* – (perumāḷ tirumoḻi 9-2) – How were you able to walk like that, Oh my dear rāmā?!) “.   Fearing for His safety is in their lineage ([āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)).

then ilangai – with beautiful palaces and protections, a place of arakkars assembled like a wasps’ nest, who trouble noble people.

ceṟṟāi – those who trouble your adiyārs are your enemies whom you win one way or other.

(On the day before the last day of war, when rāvaṇan lost his weapons, cakravarty tirumagan told him to go back for that day and come back next day (*inṟu pōi nāḷai vārāi*) – reason for this is that perumāḷ hoped that he would come next day realizing his mistake and would come with anjali (kai kūppi /கை கூப்பி) – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) melts with affection when he sees someone doing anjali to Him \] – but that did not happen from rāvaṇan next day, so perumāḷ had to kill him and release the ātmā from the body.)

tiṟal pōṟṟi – like building a protective wall for a compound wall, these girls are singing tirupallāṇḍu for his strength/valor (மிடுக்கு). They sing pallāṇḍu due to their lineage who sang ‘*mallāṇḍa tiṇ tōḷ maṇivaṇṇā*‘;  they are the ones who sing “*ilangai pāḻāḷāgap padai porudānukkup pallāṇḍu kūṟudum*”

Even maṇḍōdari while having lost her husband, praised perumāḷ after seeing his greatness and considered him as the protector.

**ponṟac cakatam udaittāi pugaḻ pōṟṟi**

There is even more danger to kriṣṇan, compared to just saying about rāvaṇan as enemy. At least in the case of cakravarty tirumagan, He had reached the right age when he fought with rāvaṇan. In this case, kriṣṇan was just a baby which would not even know what is going on. rāvaṇan came in front of perumāḷ to fight, but here everything is hidden in things, animals, toys, fruits, etc. So the gōpikas are much more worried about this.

ponṟa – unlike mārīcan or sūrpaṇaka who created trouble for perumāḷ even after their first encounter, kriṣṇan completely finished cakatāsuran.

udaittāi  – For perumāḷ the marks are in his hands holding his bows; for kriṣṇan it is the marks in his feet kicking the cakatam.

pugaḻ pōṟṟi – the greatness of casually kicking and killing cakatāsuran when his dear mother could not help him (due to her absence) – pallāṇḍu to that greatness. Without inquiring about anything you kicked him and killed him. (Compare this to killing the enemies with the help of a big army).

pōṟṟi – this is about something even more dangerous to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) than the previous case.
