In the previous case of rāma avatāram, the father was a great warrior who could fight sambaran a dēva; country the great tiru ayōdyai; learned people like vasiśtar were present; ministers were like very wise sumantirar; the boys there easily killed many asuras;  *but, here in tiruvāyppādi*, parents, innocent covherds; place, a small village; ones who are there to give any advice, young covherd samsāris; enemies, kamsan and his asuras; in srī brindāvanam even very small budding plants may turn out to be rakśasas trying to kill dear kriṣṇan; elder brother is of the type who in a fraction of a second slips and falls inside the snake’s mouth; there is no dearth of danger for kriṣṇan from asuras – so the only protection for him is mangaḷāsāsanam (pallāṇḍu / pōṟṟi).

**kanṟu kuṇilāi eṟindāi kaḻal pōṟṟi**

At least in the previous one (cakatam), one might think that the baby did that by accident/coincidence. But as you grew some more, you killed the asuran who came in the form of a calf, and you made that act very famous (our worry is that if many people come to know about it, it might reach kamsan’s ears and he might know where you are living).

You used the calf (asuran who came in that form) as a throwing pole and threw it on the other asuran who was in the form of viḷā fruit and killed them both. These gōpikas are getting afraid what would have happened if those two (vatsāsuran and kapittāsuran) had an understanding with each other to fall together on kriṣṇan!

kaḻal – kāppu for lotus feet. They are singing pallāṇḍu to the feet which stuck to the ground for killing the two asuras.

**kunṟu kudaiyāy eduttāi guṇam pōṟṟi**

You lifted the gōvardana giri to save the people from indiran’s rain, but did not kill indiran (and forgave him) as you thought he is doing all these only because he is too hungry – pallāṇḍu to your guṇam.

In the first line of the pāsuram they talked about what [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) did to *help* indiran, and in this line they are talking about how that indiran forgot all that help and created trouble for kriṣṇan’s people.

At least in the previous cases it is the enemies that gave trouble; now the trouble came from someone whom you had helped. This is even more dangerous to you; saying so, they are even more supportive of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi, and they sing pōṟṟi. When it comes to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s pallāṇḍu, there is no difference between how they see asuras and dēvas.

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) thought, ‘indiran is not an enemy, he is pouring so much rain because he is not able to withstand his hunger; let us protect these cows and people with the gōvardan”; pallaṇḍu to your great guṇam of protecting those who depend on you, with a soft heart (ānrusamsyam / ஆந்ருஸம்ஸ்யம்).

guṇam pōṟṟi – when it was raining you were holding gōvardan for so long that your hands would have become so tired – you stood there for such a long time – we sing pallāṇḍu to that great guṇam. You patiently stood there holding gōvardan thinking that indiran would get tired of pouring rain, like how when her baby pinches her she would think that it would stop whenever its hands would tire, and so the mother would give herself for baby to continue pinching.

that rain – our viśaya prāvaṇyam due to ahankāram that comes from ajñānam

your lifting the mountain – creating in us the prāvaṇyam (interest) in kainkaryams that follow what is accepted in vēdas, and he being the protector of such prapannas.

**venṟu pagai kedukkum nin kaiyil vēl pōṟṟi**

After the rain stopped, he put down the mountain and took the spear in his hands to save the cows in case any lions came across. So they are supporting his act of taking up the spear and saving everyone.

Fearing that others will put ‘kaṇ eccil’ (druśti – casting of eye – ‘being jealous/upset looking at the greatness of kriṣṇan’), they are now saying that all these tasks were done only by that sharp spear in your hand – shifting those people’s attention away from kriṣṇan. ‘*ivvēl anṟō iv vyāpāram ellām paṇṇiṟṟu’ / ‘இவ்வேலன்றோ இவ்யாபாரமெல்லாம் பண்ணிற்று’ என்கிறார்கள்.*

– This is what her [father](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) also said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi (1-1-9) imagining himself to be yasōdai : first he sang:  ‘*kidakkil tottil kiḻiya udaittidum*‘, that is ‘this kaṇṇan baby when lying down, kicks his legs So much that his bed (bassinet) might tear’,  but then to avoid others’ ‘kaṇ eccil’ about his strength and vellness (s)he changed their focus saying ‘*midukku illāmaiyāl nān melindēn*‘, that is, “I don’t have enough strength to handle all these and I became lean”.

vēl – like his father who is a ‘[*kūr vēl kodum toḻilan*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)‘ (1), kriṣṇan has got a spear too.

venṟu pagai kedukkum – enemies of adiyārs are won over and demolished.

nin kaiyil vēl pōṟṟi – since the spear is also His, they are singing mangaḷāsāsanam to it too (out of affection towards Him; for his safety).  Father too sang, ‘*vadivār sōdi valattu uṟaiyum sudar āḻiyum pallāṇḍu*‘, that is, pallāṇḍu to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s weapons/decoration.

nin kaiyil vēl – ‘while we are supposed to do ārati (to remove ‘kaṇ eccil’/druśti) for just your beauty even when you are not even holding anything in your hands, the beauty is even more with how you are holding the spear’ so they sing pallāṇḍu for that.

kaiyil vēl – it does not even have to touch the enemies, just your holding it will finish them.

jīyar ([nanjīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/14/nanjiyar-english/)) said: Like building a dam quite after the water has drained out of the river, this father and daughter have taken up singing pallāṇḍu for his avatārams that happened long ago, long after [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) had gone back safely to srī vaikuntam. This is all what they know to do.

(srīvacana bhuśaṇam: / ஸ்ரீவசந பூஷணம்:

*atīta kālangaḷil apadānangaḷukku uttara kālattilē vayir eṟivadu… /  அதீத காலங்களில் அபதானங்களுக்கு உத்தர காலத்திலே வயிரெறிவது..).*

*jñāna vibhāga kāryamāna ajñānattālē varumavai ellām adik kaḻanju peṟum / ஞான விபாக கார்யமான அஞ்ஞானத்தாலே வருமவையெல்லாம் அடிக்கழஞ்சு பெறும்.*

*mangaḷasāsanam svarūpa virutdam anṟo ennil, jñāna dasaiyil rakśya rakśaka bhāvam than kappilē kidakkum, prēma dasaiyil tattu māṟik kidakkum; avan svarūpattai anusandittāl avanaik kadagāgak koṇḍu tannai nōkkum – saukumāryattai anusandittāl tannaik kadagāgak koṇḍu avanai nōkkum / மங்களாசாஸநம் ஸ்வரூப விருத்தமன்றோ வென்னில், ஞான தசையில் ரக்ஷ்ய ரக்ஷக பாவம் தன் கப்பிலே கிடக்கும், ப்ரேம தசையில் தட்டுமாறிக் கிடக்கும்;  அவன் ஸ்வரூபத்தை அநுஸந்தித்தால் அவனைக் கடகாகக் கொண்டு தன்னை நோக்கும் – ஸௌகுமார்யத்தை அநுஸந்தித்தால் தன்னைக் கடகாகக் கொண்டு அவனை நோக்கும்.*

**enṟenṟun sēvagamē ēttip paṟai kōḷvān**

Singing pallāndu to you is the benefit we are seeking.

enṟu enṟu : Repeatedly; After saying ‘pallāṇḍu’, these people want to say ‘*pallāṇḍu*‘ again (tirupallāṇḍu (1)). Even after going to srī vaikuntam, they would ‘*sūḻndirundu* (all staying there surrounding him)’ say the same thing (tirupallāṇḍu (12)). They too repeat pōṟṟī in this pāsuram, ‘*adi pōṟṟi, tiṟal pōṟṟi, pugaḻ pōṟṟi, kaḻal pōṟṟi, guṇam pōṟṟi, vēl pōṟṟi’*.

samsāris like to get something from others, and also like to get something from [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); but these srīvaiṣṇavas won’t do anything to get something in return from other people, nor would they do something to get something back from their [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – instead they wish to do kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and ‘[*āndanaiyum kai kātti*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)‘ (2) show others too the way to get [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

They will give one or hundred thousand to him (*onṟu nūṟayiramāk koduttu pinnum āḷum seivan* – nācciār tirumoḻi 9-7), and when they see [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) happily taking it, they will do ‘[*āndanaiyum kai kātti*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)*‘* and *pay* him wages for accepting their kainkaryam (srīvacana bhūśaṇam *– koduttuk koḷḷādē, kondattukkuk kaik kūli kodukka vēṇum; tān kai ēlādē avanaik kai ērkkap paṇṇugai. / ஸ்ரீவசந பூஷணம் – கொடுத்துக் கொள்ளாதே, கொண்டத்துக்குக் கைக்கூலி கொடுக்க வேணும்; தான் கையேலாதே அவனைக் கையேற்கப் பண்ணுகை.*)

un sēvagamē ētti – to praise/highlight by telling the stories of your valor, to enjoy and experience you.

ēttip paṟai koḷvān – we get our wish (to be with you), and then the elders need to get what they wanted (rain).

singing about your paratvam *and* sovlabhyam is our kainkaryam.

**inṟu**

Today, when we agree (that we are your property);

Today, when you are sleeping while we are awake and waiting for you; we didn’t come to you all these days, but we are here today; did we come yesterday or all the days earlier when the elders had separated us from you? we came only today when they have allowed us to meet you (for nōnbu/kainkaryam). \[We don’t know whether they will allow tomorrow. We are here, Today.\]

That day others (indiran, pāṇḍavas, and such) came to you asking for small things for their own benefit – you walked with your pampered, divine, lotus feet for them; they did not sing pallāṇḍu to you. Today we think about those efforts of yours, and sing pallāṇḍu for that, and for your walking for us Today.

inṟ***i*** yām vandōm – we came without any means/benefit on our side.

**yām**

We, who were suffering for a long time because of the elders (who kept us away from your tīmbu (naughty activities)).

**vandōm**

While the old women sleep, we came here due to our angst; our troubles will be removed by you.

yām vandōm – you feel terrible that you should have come and seen us first, but we came because of our angst.

**irangu**

Whatever efforts we took, nothing good would happen unless you take pity on us.

So is this the reason for doing pōṟṟi six times in this pāsuram – to get him to take pity on us and help us? No – taking pity on us is His nature; to worry what might happen to Him is adiyār’s nature *(‘அவனுக்கு என் வருகிறதோ’).*

*anṟu ivvulagam aḷandāi – inṟu yām vandōm :* That day you came searching for us in all the worlds, today we came here searching for you.

*aḷandāi, then ilangai cēṟṟāi, cakatam udaittāi – yām vandōm* : Your pirāttis ([āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)) said ‘if trivikraman comes’, ‘if kākuttan (irāman) comes’, and ‘if kaṇṇan comes’.  See the difference here – we came to you.

*anṟu ivvulagam aḷandāi, irangu* – You happily measured the worlds that day with so much enthusiasm (*uvanda uḷḷattanāi (amalanādipirān)/ உவந்த உள்ளத்தனாய்..(அமலனாதிபிரான்)*). Today for us even if you don’t happily take us, please show at least your kindness.

*aḷandāi, irangu; tennilangai ceṟṟāi, irangu; cakatam udaittāi, irangu; kunṟu kudaiyāy eduttāi, irangu.*

They live with the thought that their mangaḷāsāsanam cannot be used by Him as the reason for helping them – it is not the means; only He should help, and that too only out of His kindness and compassion.

**ēlōrempāvāi** – dear girl, think through well and follow us.

In this pāsuram they sing pallāṇḍu to kriṣṇan: You took so much efforts and walked everywhere to help dēvas who came to you for their own benefit and who did not stop to sing pallāṇḍu to you after that; to support you we sing pallāṇḍu; looking at your beautiful and majestic walk we sing pallāṇḍu for that; we sing pallāṇḍu to your valor which is actually for the spear. (They forget what they came for after seeing his beauty… reminded of his efforts to save others, they sing pallāṇḍu for his safety).  பெரியாழ்வார் மகளிறே. After all isn’t she [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)’s [daughter](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)?

{Singing pallāṇḍu for [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is one of the best kainkaryams that one could do. Doing kainkaryams including singing and reciting [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)‘ [divya prabandams](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/) in front of him for making [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) happy, without expecting any benefits in return, without the thought that it is we who did it, is what [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) emphasizes in tiruppāvai.

This pāsuram is everyone’s favorite because people feel happy to say pōṟṟi (pallāṇḍu) to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), because their svarūpam is active when they sing pallāṇḍu. }

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 25. Orutti maganai

*orutti maganāip piṟandu ōr iravil orutti maganāi oḷittu vaḷarat tarikkilān āgit tān tīngu ninainda karuttaip piḻaippittuk kanjan vayiṟṟil neruppenna ninṟa nedumālē unnai aruttittu vandōm paṟai tarudiyāgil tiruttakka selvamum sēvagamum yām pādi varuttamum tīrndu magiḻndu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

orutti – for the one woman who is dēvaki pirātti; piṟandu – (you were) born; magan Ai – as a son; ōr iravil – on the same unmatched night itself; magan Ai – (you) become a son; orutti – for the one mother who is yasōdai pirātti; oḷittu vaḷara – (and as you were) growing in hiding; tān – (kamsan) himself; darikkilān āgi – could not tolerate (even your growing in hiding); tīngu ninainda – (his) thoughts of bad deed (of killing); piḻaippittu – (you) wasted/nullified; kanjan – kamsan’s; karuttai – thoughts; neruppu enna ninṟa – (and you) bothered (him) like a ‘fire’; vayiṟṟil – (in his) stomach; nedumālē – Oh you who loves his devotees; aruttittu vandōm – came begging; unnai – from you (for our needs); paṟai tarudi āgil – if you would satisfy our wishes; yām pādi – we would be able to sing; tiruttakka selvamum – (about) your wealth (which pirātti also would love); sēvagamum – and about your valor; varuttamum tīrndu – (and our) sorrows (of separation from you) would end; magiḻndu – (and we) would become joyous

**Перевод:**

**orutti**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*When asked, “What do you want?”, they say, “Even if there are any obstacles you please remove them yourself, and accept us so we can get rid of our sorrows (due to separation from you).*

– The woman who is special than anyone, giving birth to The One who is inside everyone (as antaryāmi). She is matchless in that she controls Him who is the controller of all the worlds.

orutti **maganāi**

**–** The one who is the father of all living beings is a son of this woman!?

cakravarty did tapas to get 4 children; here 4 people (both the parents) did tapas to get a son?!  
maganāip **piṟandu**

**–** followed his parent’s instructions as soon as he was born; cakravarty tirumagan – only after he had grown did he follow his father’s words. When kriṣṇan was born he showed his four shoulders, and the cangu/cakrataḻvārs – his parents in the jail feared for his safety and pleaded him to hide those and to look like any other child. kriṣṇan immediately obeyed by hiding those signs.

piṟandu –(born)  what people go through due to their karmas, he was eager to go through due to his krupai (kindness / wish). As dēvaki wanted a child like Him, he decided by himself to be born. (rahasyam: [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s avatārams are due to his own wish unlike our births which are due to remaining karmas; [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tirumēni is different when doing avatāram – it is not made of pancēndriyams like ours – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) just goes back to srīvaikuṇṭam unlike our body which has to be cremated).

piṟandu – could he have not just come to this world without going through a birth, that too not just for 10 months in his mother’s womb, but 12 months??  ‘periyāḻvār tirumoḻi’ (3-2-8) had to say ‘*panniru tingaḷ vayiṟṟil koṇḍa*‘. He did that due to his kindness and simplicity – for people to relate to him.

riśi thought he should use the word āvirbhāvam (manifestation / rise) to describe his birth (as sun is rising), because he thought describing it as ‘born’ would be lowly for his paratvam; the gōpikās think that describing it ‘āvirbhāvam’ would be lowly for his sovlabhyam; so they say ‘piṟandu’ (born). Since he liked to be born, they use that word as it would make Him happy.

Our karmas would make us to be born and make him also to be born (to help us) and thus make *him* equivalent to us; his kindness would help *us* be equivalent to Him (on many qualities, while we are in paramapadam) – *paramam sāmyamupaiti.*

His birth for us would help us, as our birth-cycle would go through what the chains in his parents’ legs went through (that is, we won’t have to be born again).

Our birth would be the reason (due to prakruti) for separation from Him ([emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)); his birth would help us both to unite with each other.

Thinking about all of these they say ‘piṟandu’.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> His ‘birth’ is when he first got upadēsam from his first teacher that is yādavaprakāsar.

**ōr iravil**

On that night itself kriṣṇan wanted to be a son of yasōdai, and he went into tiru āyppādi.

Like how a dog cannot digest wonderful ghee, kamsan and his asuras were not fortunate enough to have kriṣṇan in their place even for a night. kriṣṇan could not stay in his birth place even for a night.

***ōr*** *iravu – That was some night! – There was no such night before, no such night after; the birth of kriṣṇan, and what he went through.*

*The night that was so wonderful because of His birth; the night that was so cruel that a new baby had to be taken out of the house of birth without anybody’s knowledge.*

ōr iravil – from the place where there is no use of time, what a wonder that he has come born here in this ‘night’ (implies this world being controlled by time).

ōr iravil – We samsāris would keep a new child safely inside the house for at least 10 days and not take anywhere. kriṣṇan did not get that safety…

**orutti maganāi**

One woman wanted Him to be born as her son, and He gave her his avatāram, and to this other woman he is giving the enjoyment of his leelai.

magan**Ai** – like how there is nothing wrong in his being born as that woman’s son, there is nothing wrong in his growing up as this woman’s son, since the latter also thought of him as her son; and he could not get his milk there and got it from this woman only. **aitīhyam:** Someone asked [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) whether yasōdā would have produced milk since she is not his actual mother; [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) replied, “as soon as she saw kaṇṇan she might have produced milk and he might have drunk it, why do you have to research this / how does it affect you?”)

Since we surrendered to you, you made it unnecessary for us to have mother’s milk (since we would get mōkśam).

\[ [ācāryas](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/)/siśyas : They are born when they learn tirumantram, they grow reciting dvayam all the time.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> his next ‘birth / mother’ was when he underwent samāsrayaṇam from [periya nambi](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/periya-nambi/).

\]

**oḷittu vaḷara**

Growing without being sighted by kamsan’s poisonous eyes.

kriṣṇan was not able to do everything that the people of this world could; he was going through what thieves would go through (hiding). Even when he was here as an avatāram, he was being like antaryāmi!

When he is inside itself as antaryāmi, people do not agree about his presence/control, would they agree if they are able to see him? Reason for his being as antaryāmi – if a mentally unstable son would hit her mother if he sees him, she would hide herself but be somewhere around keeping him in her sight, because otherwise if he is alone he might kill himself; she would be around (but out of sight) so that if at any time he consents her and calls ‘ammē!’ (O mother!), then she would be there to ask ‘What?’. Likewise, when we don’t consent that we are His, He hides himself within our ātmā/heart but does not go away since we would be nothing if He is not there within everything; and whenever we consent (இசைவு) to receive his grace, he is there waiting and ready to protect us.

orutti – instead of saying the name of the mother, [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) is saying ‘orutti (one woman)’;  after all, [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) is the daughter of the one [who sang](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-periyazhwar.html) ‘*attattin pattā nāḷ*‘ (baby that was born 10 days near hasta nakśatram (star) – this was to create confusion whether it was plus 10 days or minus 10 days, so that kamsan or others would not be sure of the exact day if they try to cast some evil spell (sūniyam) on kriṣṇan; and they would be not sure whether the song is about kriṣṇan. [āḻvār](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-periyazhwar.html) was born very long after kriṣṇan’s avatāram. Here they don’t say the name so that kamsan won’t be able to cast some spell on the name { not very long ago, people won’t say their names or dear ones’ names easily, in fear of things such as this }.

\[ācāryan/siśyas: They teach/learn rahasyams (oḷittu) and grow (vaḷara) in their jñānam\]

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> yādavaprakāsar who was an advaiti was not happy with [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s objections which were raised when he was teaching [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) – yādava prakāsar planned to kill [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) by pushing him in to the ganges by taking him on a yātra to north. [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) came to know this and he had to hide himself (oḷittu) from yādava prakāsar to travel back (pēraruḷāḷan and perundēvi tāyār came as a hunter couple and helped [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) reach kānci (orutti maganāi oḷittu vaḷara)) .

**darikkilān āgi**

(kamsan) who could not tolerate the news that kriṣṇan is growing in some place when nāradā informed him. If someone tells him that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) is inside him as antaryāmi, then he would not accept that.

\[ ācāryan cannot withstand it when a siśyan is not getting jñānam/samāsrayaṇam; [emperumānar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-emperumanar.html) could not tolerate when one of his siśyas stopped coming to  his kālakśēpam; [emperumānār](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-emperumanar.html) went to the place where he was gambling in bad company, and told him “Even if you leave me, I would not leave you. Come and join us”.\]

**tān tīngu ninainda**

These gōpikās fear for the safety of his beauty and well-being and sing mangaḷāsāsanam to Him, but kamsan being the kind described above is creating danger to Him, due to his bad prakruti.

tān – kamsan, who is astiran (mortal), like any being, that is, he doesn’t know any trouble that he might go through the next minute. (such a person is trying to create trouble for Him).

tīngu ninainda – [āṇḍāḷ](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-andal-anubhavam.html) is just saying ‘tīngu (danger)’ since it is unbearable to talk about the exact danger of asuras like pūtanai who came to Him, and about the danger of kamsan inviting him to his palace for the bow festival (வில் வழவு).

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> yādava prakāsar could not withstand the greatness of [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/), and like the ‘tīngu’ that kamsan wanted to do to kriṣṇan, he wanted to do to [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/).

**karuttaip piḻaippittu**

pūtanai and others’ troubles were averted by kriṣṇan, and he gave himself to us; what kamsan thought of doing to kriṣṇan, kamsan himself went through that danger – got killed. Kamsan was planning to play the role of uncle and pretend cry when he would hear his plan about kriṣṇan would succeed; but kriṣṇan played the role of nephew.

**kanjan vayiṟṟil neruppenna ninṟa**

(kriṣṇā! you stood in front of kamsan and scared him to death)

All the pāpams that kamsan had accumulated in all the births – he went through the effects of those pāpams just by the sight of you in front of him.

neruppu – fire – fear – The fiery fear created by kamsan in kriṣṇan’s devotees and his parents (vasudēvar, dēvaki) was transferred to kamsan by kriṣṇan when kamsan saw kriṣṇan.

**nedu mālē** (māl – love)

You went through all that because of your love towards all your adiyārs; also because of your love for vasudēvar and dēvaki.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> As [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s greatness grew in all directions, yādava prakāsar’s mother advised him to go surrender to [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) and join him as a siśyan. yādava prakāsar felt bad for his mistakes and told his mother that he too would like to do that, but his pāpams might make him ineligible – his mother advised that if he fell on [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s feet and went around rāmānuar to do pradakśinam, then all his pāpams would go away – he did that and [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) was very glad to take him as his siśyan.

**unnai**

You, who do not have any needs from anyone, came down to this place from srīvaikuṇṭam and begged (for land, for butter..). Upon whose needs did you do all these? You came by yourself to help us. So you (who came by your own will) would help us.

nedu mālē **unnai** **aruttittu vandōm**

aruti – pray/plead. We have come here to pray to you; actually we have come here to pray and get you – we are greedy: we want you; and we pray you for that! But we know that our praying/pleading is not the means for getting you – only your kindness and love for us will get you for us.

unnai – You are already love sick upon us, and by coming and asking you, we are creating more love sickness on your love sickness (பிச்சின் மேல் பிச்சேற்ற வந்தோம்).

When you see us you would understand the level of our pleading.

**paṟai tarudiyāgil**

**īF** you would benefit us by giving paṟai : kainkaryam, kriṣṇan, mōkśam;

āgil – īF you did – our begging or pleading is from our side but it is your blessing and your independent wish which would make it happen; please do not make your beauty an obstacle and distract us, please help us to reach you.

\[ [rāmānuja!](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-emperumanar.html), we surrender to you and you recommend us to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) to give us mōkśam (paṟai).\]

**tiruttakka selvamum**

(we will sing) about your wealth because of being the lord of [tāyar (tiru)](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/);

About the wealth (which is yourself) that [pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) herself loves!

**sēvagamum**

about your power to protect and enjoy such wealth.

**yām pādi**

we, who did not mention, not qualified enough to mention, your name and about your wealth, are here today to sing about them

**varuttamum tīrndu**

Our suffering due to separation shall go away because of singing about you;

You don’t know how much that suffering would be because you have never been separated from yourself.

Not only removal of suffering, but also:

**magiḻndu**

**–** We will sing your names and become happy (too);

Unlike kaivalyam (enjoying only the [jīvātama](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and not [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) which just removes the suffering of this samsāram, we will not only remove our suffering of this samsāram (varuttamum tīrndu), but will also have the way to enjoy with you (mōkśam); not just removal of suffering but also enjoy.

[rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) =\> We wish to be associated to [rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/); he accepts us when we do our saraṇāgati through our ācāryan; and the count-down starts – for our leaving this samsāram at the end of this life, and reaching srīvaikuṇṭam and do uninterrupted kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and become happy to see him happy.

**ēlōrempāvāi** – think through and follow our path.

In this pāsuram, after getting kaṇṇan to wake up and be seated in his throne, gōpikās pray to him to give them opportunity to do kainkaryam to Him;  
to Him who was born in this world out of his own wish and love and performed so many wonders;  
to Him who was born for one, and grew up under another, by transferring himself the same night he was born;  
to Him who can bless us out of his own wish and love, so that our sorrows (samsāram) would end and we would be happy too (in mōkśam).

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 26. Male manivanna

*mālē maṇivaṇṇā mārgaḻi nīrāduvān mēlaiyār seivanagaḷ vēṇḍuvana kēttiyēl jñālattai ellām nadunga muralvana pālanna vaṇṇattu un pānca canniyamē pōlvana sangangaḷ pōippādu udaiyanavē sālap perum paṟaiyē pallāṇḍu isaippārē kōla viḷakkē kodiyē vidānamē ālin ilaiyāy aruḷ ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

mālē – Oh you who is loving (of your devotees)!; maṇi vaṇṇā – having color like a blue diamond!; ālin ilaiyāy – (during praḷayam) slept in a new banyan leaf!; kēttiyēl – if you inquire about the; vēṇḍuvana – needed items; seivanagaḷ – for actions done; mēlaiyār – by preceptors / earlier generations; mārgaḻi nīrāduvān – for bathing/nōnbu of mārgaḻi; sangangaḷ – conces; pāl anna vaṇṇattu un pānca canniyamē pōlvana – like your pānca janyam (conch) that is white in color like milk; muralvana – which can sound (such that); jñālattai ellām – whole of the world; nadunga – (would) vibrate/be scared; paṟai – paṟai instrument (drum); pōi pādu udaiyana – with enough space; sāla peru – (and) very big; pallāṇḍu isaippār – people who sing tiruppallāṇḍu; kōlam viḷakku – (lamps) auspicious mangaḷa dīpam; kodi – dvajams (flags); vidānam – clothes (covering us) above; aruḷ – kindly give them

**Перевод:**

**mālē – māḷ: loving/affectionate (to His devotees)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*{ We see very interesting conversation between kriṣṇan and gōpikās, in this pāsuram }*

Whereas earlier they said ‘*nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān*‘ (only nārāyaṇan would give us mokśam / opportunity-to-do-kainkaryam) they sang about his greatness; now after seeing him, they conclude that His nature is to be kind to us; that he is defined by his kindness toward us.

pirātti referred to Him as “saraṇāgata vatsalan” as the main aspect from the essence of srī rāmāyaṇam. For mahābhāratam, they are looking at kriṣṇan’s ‘*mām* ēkam..’ in gītā, and his desire to be with us; his love for us adiyārs (āsrita vyāmōham) as his main guṇam.

How did they come to that conclusion?

When they said ‘yām vanda kāriyam ārāindu aruḷ’, they saw that kriṣṇan’s face became brighter and glowing than before, and he walked to his simhāsanam with eagerness to help and be with the gōpikās, and being much more madly in love than before.

‘***māl****āip piranda nambiyai….*‘ in nāciār tirumoḻi.

**maṇi vaṇṇā –** beautiful sapphire like blue colored form of Him

Even though he is supreme, he has made himself easily available to gōpikās and is following their orders. Similar to how we could handle a sapphire or pearl, he is being very easy to have him in the tip of their sarees (*mundānaiyil mudindu āḷalāmpadi irukkai / முன்தானையில் முடிந்து ஆளலாம்படியிருக்கை*), wear, or even sell!

His desire for adiyārs they referred to is showing in his form too.

Even those who don’t like him are mesmerized by this beauty.

Even if he does not have his kindness, this beauty would not let them leave him;

His beauty which is the reason for these gōpikās’ craze towards him.

‘*mālē seiyyum maṇaḷanai…..*‘ (nāciār tirumoḻi)

nōnbu and such would be the means for kāmyārtis (who do it for getting some other benefit); it would be just the following of what is required in sāstram for niśkāmar (those who do not do it for any benefit), and would be a kainkaryam for prapannas.

==\> “Why have you come here?”

They tell him what they have come here for:

**mārgaḻi nīrāduvān**

We came here to request the items needed for mārgaḻi nīrāttam (nōnbu / kriṣṇa sambhōgam).

Even though they got what they wanted (kriṣṇan) as the result of nōnbu, they are asking him about the items needed for nōnbu – to help the elders get rain.

“What is this mārgaḻi nīrāttam?”

“īsn’t it well known?”

Even though elders have decided that the gōpikās shall do nōnbu and kriṣṇan shall help them, He is asking these just for the enjoyment of conversing with these girls, because conversing with them is His goal.

“Is it as famous as what vēdas say about what one should do for various benefits?”

**mēlaiyār seivanagaḷ**

(what people have been doing for eons)

“No not like that, dear kriṣṇa. This is even more familiar to everyone, as what one does learning from their elders – siśtācāram.  [*seyyādana seyyōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289) (we would not do what they have not done).

vēdas say – “*darmajñasamaya: pramāṇam vēdāsca*” – the ācāram (behaviour, activities) of those who know darmam is (the main) pramāṇam (root for true knowledge); (then) vēdas are also pramāṇam.

“*yadyadācarati srēśta:* (whatever a noble person follows, other people also would follow that; how much ever he does, that is how much the others would do)”

“If so, tell me what items you need”

**vēṇḍuvana kēttiyēl**

If you want to listen to what we need, if you can stop staring at us, then we will tell you what we need. Your focus is somewhere else.

Why is he having some other focus?

Since all the girls from 5 lakhs houses are in front of him, he is dwelling on their breasts, waist, eyes, and face, and sitting motionless like a chess player. So they pat him to get him out of his trance.

In svāpadēsam it is talking about jñāna, bhakti, vairākyams. These are the ones that are apt for an adikāri (eligible person);  
since it helps to see things, “eye” is meant for jñānam;  
since it is for enjoyment “breast” is meant for bhakti;  
since it is very slim, “waist” is meant for vairākyam; If asked whether slimness imply vairākyam, then, – slim due to virakti which is about not being interested in worldly things.

“Your form are feast to my eyes, likewise, aren’t your words sweet to my ears? So tell me.”

“Are we here for your enjoyment? If you will listen without seeing parākku (looking around at things), then we will tell you”.

And they list the items they came for:

**jñālattai ellām nadunga muralvana**

(first they ask for conch)

This whole world should hear its (sangu) sound and prosper.

They are the ones who sang “[*valampuri pōl ninṟu adirndu*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=356) (4)”.

**pālanna vaṇṇattu un pānca canniyamē pōlvana sangangaḷ**

The sangu (conch) that is white like the milk collected together; we need many of them.

They said ‘*maṇi vaṇṇa*‘ since it is what they like (his color), so it goes without saying that they would like the color of the conch that is touched by him.

**poippādu udaiyanavē**

sangu that is having the fame of being with you, having the fame of helping the ones like rukmiṇi pirātti (when she was waiting for kriṣṇan to come and take her, kriṣṇan used the sound of sangu as a sign to let her know of his arrival, when she was desperate and was ready to take her life).

==\> Since the sound of conch is equated to praṇavam, asking for conch is about asking for ananyārha sēśatvam that is highlighted in praṇavam.

“Found one. what else?” (ஒ*ன்று கண்டோம், பின்னையோவென்ன*,)

**sālap perum paṟaiyē**

For starting if we used conch, don’t we need to do ‘kottu’ (mrdangam like drum) for the purappādu?

A big paṟai (percussion instrument like drums), so big that it makes sound not for a small gathering, but that which reaches everywhere; everywhere where the sound of conch reached. paṟai that makes such a sound when hit in the ‘middle’ of it.

==\> Since paṟai makes the sound from the ‘middle’, and the middle word ‘nama:’ in tirumantram is about pāratantriya jñānam (knowing about subservience) which helps remove svasvātantriyam (thinking we are independent of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) which is a bhagavat-ananyārha sēśatva virōdi (i.e., it prevents the above mentioned anayārha sēśatvam to [peruman](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), and helps remove svādīna kartrutvam & bhōkyatvam (doing it for own benefit or own enjoyment) when doing kainkaryam, which is the symbol of sēśatvam.

“what else?” (*பின்னையோவென்ன*,)

**pallāṇḍu isaippārē**

and we need ones who will be going with us singing tiruppallaṇḍu

==\> we need sat-sahavāsam (association with adiyārs).

“what else?” (*பின்னையோவென்ன*,)

**kōla viḷakkē**

Beautiful lamp; auspicious mangaḷa dīpam.

One who is singing would be able to see us and we would be able to see him.

==\> need bhāgavata sēśatva jñānam which makes it enjoyable for [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) when we go with bhagavat-ananyārha sēśatvam.

**kodi**

We need flags to wave, to go in front of us and which would be visible from afar.

==\> need kainkaryam that is the indication of sēśatvam.

**vidānam**

(cloth) covering (us) above (*கூறாளம்*) from the mist.

==\> in that kainkaryam we need removal of doing it for our own enjoyment.

These are the things we need when we go for nīrāttam.

\*\*\*\* He starts replying about these items:

“I don’t have many sangams, you take pānca janyam”.

Only if there is someone like him would there have been multiple sangams that are like his pānca janyam.

“When all the people were clapping around me when I was dancing while balancing the pots (kudak kūttu), I was making the beats with a paṟai tied to my hip (*pārōrgaḷellām magiḻap paṟai kaṟanga / பாரோர்களெல்லாம் மகிழப் பறை கறங்க* – siṟiya tirumadal) – take that one for paṟai; for singing tiruppallaṇḍu take [periyāḻvār](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/10/archavathara-anubhavam-periyazhwar.html) (because I cannot find anyone who cares about me like he does); for lamp take nappinnai pirātti (they too sang, ‘*kula viḷakkē*‘) (nappinnai pirātti would also be too happy to help her adiyārs as it would help them reach [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)), for flag take the one that has got periya tiruvadi (garudan), for vidānam on the top take my uttarīyam (white or colored cloth tied in the hip).

For vidānam why not give tiru anantāḻvān who covered him on the day of his birth (when going to tiruVaippādi)? Even though He asked others to go, since tiruvanantaḻvān would not take even one step without [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with him (*oḻivil kālamellām udanāi manni vaḻuvilā adimai / ஒழிவில் காலமெல்லாம் உடனாய் மன்னி வழுவிலா அடிமை* – tiruvāimoḻi), He gave his uttarīyam instead.

His uttarīyam is also something we enjoy. (*vāttam taṇiya vīsīrē* / *வாட்டம் தணிய வீசீரே*)

After getting all of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s dear items, the are like equivalent to perumān having all these items.

“One each is not enough; we need many of them like these”

“will I search for what is not there?” (*இல்லாததைத் தேடப்போமோ*?)

“Is there anything that is impossible/hard for you?”

“Is it easy for me?”

==\> How can I create what is not there?

**ālin ilaiyāy..**

You became very small, got all the worlds into your stomach, and you slept in a banyan leaf – is there anything that is not possible for you?!

For us, you can create even something that does not even exist in this world.

You possess agadita gadanā sāmartyam (smartness to connect/make things that cannot be connected/made).

**aruḷ**

Even if we do not ask you might still help; you are not without ability to help; for our goals your blessing is the means.

By ‘*mālē maṇivaṇṇā*‘, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) talks about ‘*mām*‘ (‘me’, the kind me); by ‘*ālin ilaiyāy*‘ she talks about ‘*aham*‘ (‘I’, the only one who can help you get mōkśam (not other means)) – from carama slōkam.

Since [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gives them his pānca janyam etc., it goes with what muṇḍakōpaniśad 3-1-3 says: *“niranjana: paramam sāmyamupaiti”* – in short: when a jñāni sees [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (brahmam) then the jñāni (gains mōkśam and) gains jñānam similar to that of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**ēlōrempāvāi** – commit and follow our ways

{ In this pāsuram, gopikās take this great opportunity to talk to kriṣṇan and to praise his kalyāṇa guṇams (divine qualities); they ask him to give the items needed to do their nōnbu; those items would in fact help go in a procession and sing kriṣṇan’s nāmams.

Doing nōnbu, and asking those items, are part of what the elders wanted them to do. Their own personal agenda? Destination kriṣṇan, be with Him as long as possible, play with him, tease him, enjoy him, and do a lot of kainkaryams to him. }

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 27. Kudarai vellum

*kūdārai vellum sīrk gōvindā unṟannaip pādip paṟai koṇḍu yām peṟu sammānam nādu pugaḻum parisināl nanṟāga sūdagamē tōḷ vaḷaiyē tōdē sevippūvē pādagamē enṟanaiya palkalanum yām aṇivōm ādai uduppōm adan pinnē pāl sōṟu mūda nei peidu muḻankai vaḻi vāra kūdi irundu kuḷirndu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

vellum sīr – having the guṇam of winning; kūdārai – those who are opposed; gōvindā – Oh gōvinda!; pādi – (we would) sing (till we are content); un tannai – about you; paṟai koṇḍu – and receive paṟai (instrument); yām peru sammānam – and the price we are going to get is; nādu pugaḻum parisināl – that the villagers would appreciate/celebrate us; yām nanṟāga aṇivōm – we would wear (the ornaments) very well (as we are decorated with them by you and nappinnai pirātti), (ornaments such as); cūdagam – ornament for the arms; tōḷ vaḷai – ornament for the shoulders; tōdu – ornament for the ears; sevip pū – ornament for the upper part of ears; pādagam – (pāda kadagam) – ornament for the legs; enṟu anaiya pal kalanum – and more such ornaments; adan pinnē – after that; pāl sōṟu – rice (in the) food made with milk; mūda – (which) would not be visible (due to); nei peidu – (lots of) ghee added; muḻangai vaḻi vāra – such that it would drip through the elbows; kūdi irundu – (we would) be together (and eat that); kuḷirndu – and be happy

**Перевод:**

**kūdārai vellum**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*After getting him they are talking about the rewards they would get for completing the nōnbu (getting him). During union with emperumān it would appear that the ornaments are their aim.*

kriṣṇan wins those who don’t want to befriend Him; He will try as much as possible to include everyone with Him. He would win the ones like rāvaṇan who did not want to bow his head till the end.

He loses to those who join/accept him.

parasurāman tried to fight with cakravarty tirumagan, but after losing to Him, he gave his bows and arrows and did anjali to Him and left; if he had done anjali even when holding the bows in his hand it would have been cakravarty tirumagan who would have lost to that.

“We won’t fall on the feet of kriṣṇan who is having friendship with arjunan” said duryōdanan, and you won him.

“We will do whatever you say” said pāṇḍavas, and you did everything they said – walking for them – to ask for a few small villages, riding a chariot in the war, bathing the horses of the chariot, and so on.

\[

[ācāryas](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) try hard to correct those who do not follow the sampradāyam. They try whatever is required to help siśyas understand bhagavat viśayams.

**==\>** Once an elderly person, who helps with tasks related to writing copies of grantams**,** came to [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) and requested him to teach him some meanings (do kalakśēpam). [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) agreed to teach him. But that person put a condition that nobody should know that he is learning from him – he probably thought it is low of him to be learning because others might think that he does not know enough; [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) agreed not to tell anyone that he is teaching this person, since [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) thought that it would any way help one person to gain bhagavat viśayams.

So, [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) took that person to an isolated place and started doing kālakśēpam, one on one. That person would hold the grantam in his hands and read a phrase and [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) would explain its meanings (as was the custom of the time). This went on for a few days. One day when this was going on, [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) saw some people walking toward their location. That elderly man did not notice those people. [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) was concerned that this person’s condition of anonymity would be in jeopardy if they came to know about this kālakśēpam.

As those people came closer to the location, [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) quickly took the srīkōsam from that person’s hands and kept it in his hands, and as those people crossed them, [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) loudly said to the student, ‘please continue (teaching me)’ – pretending to be learning from that old man. Those people might have thought, “we thought [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) knows everything, but looks like he is learning all of these secretly from this old person”

[kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) is jñāna nidi – seeing his jñānam [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) himself has wondered whether ālavandār has born in the form of this [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/); there is no end to talking about the greatness of [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) — that [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/) is sitting here pretending to learn from this person; he did not care what others would think of him. That old person realized his mistake and fell on [kūrattāḻvān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/10/05/kurathazhwan/)’s feet.

They try whatever is required to help siśyas understand viśayams.

**sīr**

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) wins all by his guṇams.

perumāḷ won enemies just by the supreme way he held his bow. (*cacāla cāpanca mumōca vīra:*) He won them by his guṇam of valor.

He wins gōpikās and adiyārs by showing his beauty and kindness (guṇams).

He won [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) by showing his svarūpa (true nature) rūpa (forms) guṇa (auspicious qualities) vibhūti (wealth).

He wins people like us who don’t show interest in any of his qualities, by using our sambandam with [āḻvars](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) as the front.

**==\>** One could take care of the target of his arrows by applying some medicine; there is no medicine for the target of his beauty and kindness. “Wow! How can you be like that?” say the jñānis when looking at his simplicity (*ettiṟam*! – tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-1 / *எத்திறம்!* (திருவாய்மொழி 3-1-1)).

**gōvindā**

You are so fond of the cows and you go behind them even though they don’t say they want to be with you, nor do they realize that you are going behind them.

You are just happy that they did not say *No* to your relationship; they enjoy and grow by your fond touch.  
\[ Another meaning for govindā – capable of speaking about great things. Our ācāryan \]

**unṟannaip pādi**

We are singing your names for the purpose of just singing your names.

You are ‘*gōpi jana vallabhan*‘; singing your names is our benefit of doing the nōnbu; benefit of having a tongue.

**paṟai kōṇḍu**

They are talking about paṟai (drum instrument) which is prāpakam (means for nōnbu) when they are already on to prāpyam (kriṣṇan); they are doing this is to help the elders.

\[Singing ācāryan’s greatness, doing saraṇāgati through him; ‘*kurugūr nambi pāvin* (tiruvaimoḻi) *innisai pādit tirivanē*‘, said [madurakavi aḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/17/madhurakavi-azhwar/). (I will go around singing kurugūr nambi’s tiruvāimoḻi); When others said “Since tiruvāimoḻi is about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), you are singing about [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), not ācāryan”, he replied, “I don’t know what is there in tiruvāimoḻi, I only sing that because [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) wrote it”.\]

**yām peru sammānam**

the prize we get – is the flower garland from your shoulders, for you to place it in our shoulders… like perumaḷ gave to pirātti when going out of his palace to meet cakravarty during the day of his pattābhiśēkam.

yām – we, who think that we are always under the blessing of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and all that we have to do is accept that uninterrupted blessing.

**nādu pugaḻum parisināl**

you give… such that it is celebrated by everyone as “this one gave and all the girls got the prize!”.

\[ The award we get is what we learned from ācāryan’s gatākśam, our association with our ācāryan. ‘tiruvadi sambandam’. ‘adiyen [rāmānuja](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) dāsan’\]

**nanṟāga**

(give us prize in the best way) like how perumāḷ and pirātti together gave tiruvadi (hanumān) the chain given by indiran. Unlike those going to srīvaikuṇṭam from here who are decorated by apsara women, we want you and nappinnai pirātti to decorate us; we don’t want to be in the situation of decorating and waiting for your arrival; we want you to decorate us and enjoy the beauty.

They ask kriṣṇan the following five ornaments.

**\[** We get from ācāryan the ‘five’ – [panca samskāram](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html), during [samāsrayaṇam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html) – those five are: poṟi (tāpa mudrā) in shoulders, nāmam (puṇḍram) in forehead and other places, a new name (nāmam) for us – ‘dāsan’, rahasya **mantram**, and, about tiruvārādana kramam(yāgam).**\]**

**cūdagamē**

Jewels worn in hands – the hands of gōpikās that kriṣṇan would first hold when touching; he would fondly keep their hands in his head and chest;

\[\[ tiruvārādanam, done by hands, is our ornament. ācāryan explains this to us during [samāsrayaṇam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html) \]\].

**tōḷ vaḷaiyē**

with that touch he would want to hug our shoulders – we need jewels for such shoulders.

\[ Jewels for our shoulders – tiru ilaccinai – the cangu chakra poṟi by our ācāryan. \]

**tōdē**

even if we have already worn in our ear lobes, it vouldn’t be same as what you would put on us.

our ear studs would be a decoration to your shoulders when you hug our shoulders.

\[==\> The rahasyam we get in our ears from our ācāryan – *tirumantram*.

During [panca samskāram](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html), this is dāsya nāmam from ācāryan; visible outside like tōdu. \]

**sevip pūvē**

unlike other jewels worn by them which bother when He hugs them, these ornaments and flowers worn in the upper part of their ear lobes would help him enjoy his hug.

\[ ==\> sevip pū – is bhōgam (enjoyment), that is, for our every-day recital, that we got from our ācāryan : *dvayam* \]

\[\[ rahasyams received during [samāsrayaṇam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html), not so visible outside, like sevip pū. \]\]

**pādagam**

Worn in their legs; after hugging and losing to them, he would press their legs – ornament for those legs;

cūdagam for hands, pādagam for legs – pressing their hands and legs is what He does and accomplishes other related things from them.

\[==\> [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi is what we surrender to – he gave *carama slōkam* for that. That is our ornament.

==\> So, the above three are about rahasya trayam. They are also about ‘jñāna, bhakti, vairājñam’ that one would show towards [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). How? Since tirumantram is about the jñānam of our relationship with Him, dvayam’s 2nd line of kainkaryam is based on our bhakti towards him, and carama slōkam is based on vairākyam to not use other means to reach Him, they are about jñāna, bhakti, vairājñam.

pādagam – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi is what we wear as nāmam in our forehead -\> one of [panca samskārams during samāsrayaṇam](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html). \]

**enṟu anaiya pal kalanum**

We will wear all types of ornaments that you know; you should know, you wear so many ornaments; we will wear many of these and more like these.

**yām aṇivōm**

They had said ‘[*malar ittu nām mudiyōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289) (2) (won’t wear flowers (as they were doing nōnbu)), but now they are saying they will wear all these jewels.  Only till coming to him were they to follow those things; now they want him to decorate them; he will do it if they say they agree to wear it.

\[==\> without bhagavat sambandam, the jñāna, bhakti, vairājñam would not benefit but would only create danger. For a baudda (follower of budda) too there would be jñāna, bhakti, vairājñam, but what is the use?

As acaryan does [panca samskāram](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/%20http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/07/srivaishnava-lakshanam-2.html), we will wear those as our ornaments.\]\]

**ādai uduppōm**

Will wear clothes.. were they not wearing before?

They did not wear the ones which He wore first – so they don’t consider them as clothes or as wearing.

His tiruppariyattam (cloth) worn in his head is what they want for their waist. In privacy they would wear each other’s clothes; that is the kind of clothing they wish for. As they are finishing nōnbu, they want a good tiruppariyattam.

They belong to the clan that sang ‘*uduttuk kaḷainda nin pīdaga ādai uduttu*‘.

His smell in what he wore first is what they enjoy when wearing it themselves.

gōvindā, unṟannaip pādi, ādai uduppōm : Even when you were not near her she said gōvindā raising her hands and she got unlimited length of saree; we are near you here, will there be any dearth of getting our clothes?

(But, when kriṣṇan was near gōpikās, he stole their clothes when they were bathing; when he was away from draupati he gave her enough saree when she said his name. It looks like his name is better than him!). \[*mumukśuppadi: draupatikku ābattilē pudavai surandadu tirunāmamiṟē / முமுக்ஷுப்படி: த்ரௌபதிக்கு ஆபத்திலே புடவை சுரந்தது திருநாமமிறே* \]

==\> sēśatva jñānam; one who does not have any jñānam/awareness (like a baby, or a mentally challenged) would not worry about clothes; so jñānam is equated to having clothes worn. We get jñānam from ācāryan.

**adan pinnē pāl sōṟu mūda nei peidu**

adan pinnē – After wearing clothes worn by you, we want to eat prasādam eaten by you.

adan pinnē – If one goes to someone other than Him, they would not get those items and they would not get Him. If they go to Him, they will get everything (Him).

We said ‘[*nei uṇṇōm pāl uṇṇōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)‘, we did not eat ghee or milk all these days- seeing that, kriṣṇa! you also avoided those things; so there is so much of milk and ghee in all of our houses. We will cook rice with milk, and will add a lot of ghee that covered its top; one would have to search for rice in between ghee.

tiruvāippādi that is having bhagavat sambandam with kriṣṇan is wealthy and loving.

==\> *pāl sōru:* kainkaryam**;**   *mūda* – but we will not think that it is we who did the kainkaryam;   *nei peidu* – thus we will stick to our pāratantriyam.

**muḻankai vaḻi vāra**

==\> ṇambi tiruvaḻudi vaḷanādu dāsar asked [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/): “Wouldn’t the ghee go inside their mouth? Would it only pour out in their arms?”  [bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) replied, “kaṇṇan is their food, water, and betel leaf (*uṇṇum sōru, parugu nīr, tinnum veṟṟilai ellām kaṇṇan* (tiruvāimoḻi) / *உண்ணும் சோறு பருகு நீர் தின்னும் வெற்றிலை எல்லாம் கண்ணன்* (திருவாய்மொழி). These gōpikās thinking about kriṣṇan and his leelais vouldn’t put food in their mouth, so there is no question of ghee flowing down in their arms.”.

For nōnbu they had said ‘[*nei uṇṇōm, pāl uṇṇōm*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289) (2)’, but now, after joining kriṣṇan, they love to eat like this; enjoying food made of so much ghee and other tasty items, which kriṣṇan ate first.

===\> \[ When standing in a line to get water, if someone is extremely thirsty he would drink the water pouring from the elbow of the person in front of him who is drinking water; we are also drinking water (jñānam/mōkśam) from our ācāryan’s elbow who is drinking from the elbow of his ācāryan, and so on. *muḻankai vaḻi vāra*.\].

**kūdi irundu**

They were separated from kriṣṇan all these days; now they are with him, around him, and they start feeling better after touching him, talking to him, and talking about him. This is better than eating. Eating is not their goal – being together with kriṣṇan and touching the adiyārs is their wish.

\[==\> ādiyārs being together for talking, singing, thinking, and doing things about [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), [ācāryas](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/), [pirātti, and perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – this is what will make them more dear to kriṣṇan. ‘*andam il pērinbattu adiyārōdu irundamai’* (tiruvāimoḻi).

*aham annam – aham annāda:*  :  I am like food for [paramātmā](http://ponnadi.blogspot.com/2013/03/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god.htm). I the food of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), am enjoying [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who enjoys me as a food;

Here too they are bhōjñam to each other.

**kuḷirndu**

Be with kriṣṇan, and cool down the hearts that were aching all these days due to separation.

\[==\> Like how they conduct ‘namperumāḷ tirunāḷ’ to get all the srīvaiṣṇavas together and enjoy that sight.

***kūdi irundu kuḷirndu:***   *sōśnutē sarvān kāmān saha brahmaṇā vipasstitā* – muktātmā gets all kalyāṇa guṇams and enjoys them together with brahmam ([perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) in srīvaikuntam.

(with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)) perumānudan kūdi irundu kuḷirndu (in here as well as in mokśam).

(with adiyār) adiyārgaḷudan kūdi irundu kuḷirndu (in here as well as in mōkśam).

Together with other adiyārs, we enjoy the wonderful prasādam we cooked.

That will be our sammānam (gift).

**ēlōrempāvāi –** Enjoy this and follow our path.  
In this pāsuram, through our ācāryan, through the link to [srī rāmānujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/), we get rahasya trayam, jñānam/bhakti/vairākyam, after samāsrayaṇam we get mōkśam and be with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) forever.

Previous pāsuram ‘[*mālē maṇi vaṇṇa*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=805)‘ highlighted “sāmyāpatti mōkśam” – gaining equivalent kalyāṇa guṇams as [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). There, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) asked many of the things that [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) holds – his cangu, flag, muktātmās (pallāṇḍu isaippār), etc., gaining what he has got.

This pāsuram highlights ‘sāyujya mōkśam’ – being together with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (kūdi irundu) and doing uninterrupted kainkaryams to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (kuḷirndu) in srī vaikuntam.

With sēśatvam (servitude) we do kainkaryams to you for the sake of Your happiness, and without any thoughts that it is we who did all the kainkaryams – everything is yours, so whatever we give you is not created by us.

Doing kainkaryam is our nature; loving and blessing us is your nature.

It also highlights cakkarai pongal made with/of lots of ghee, milk, and some rice. When we take it in our hand to eat it (or not), the ghee will flow in our muḻankai (arm; all the way down to our elbow). We are happy to share the food with other adiyārs – other adiyārs are happy to enjoy it together with us.

It is only because of our ācāryan we get sammānam of sāmyāpati and sāyujya mōkśam where we can be with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), and along with all muktātmās together do kainkaryam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) for ever**.** The help of our ācāryan. *ācāryan seida upakāram / ஆசார்யன் செய்த உபகாரம் (upadēsa rattinamālai).*

English translation raghurām srīnivāsa dāsan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 28. Karavaigal pin cenru

*kaṟavaigaḷ pin cenṟu kānam sērndu uṇbōm aṟivu onṟum illāda āikkulattu unṟannaip piṟavi peṟuntanaip puṇṇiyam yām udaiyōm kuṟaivonṟum illāda gōvindā unṟannōdu uṟavēl namakku ingu oḻikka oḻiyādu aṟiyāda piḷḷaigaḷōm anbināl unṟannaic ciṟu pēr aḻaittanavum sīṟi aruḷādē iṟaivā nī tārāi paṟai ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kuṟai onṟum illāda gōvindā – Oh gōvinda! who does not have any shortcomings!; yām – we; kaṟavaigaḷ pin cenṟu – go behind the cows; kānam sērndu – reach the forest/fields; uṇbōm – (we walk around while) eating; aṟivu onṟum illāda – (we are) not having any jñānam; āykkulattu – (belonging to) the covherd clan; un tannaip piṟavi peṟum tanaip puṇṇiyam udaiyōm – we are blessed (have good karmā) to have you born; iṟaivā – Oh the almighty!; untannōdu uṟavu – (our) relationship with you; ingu namakku oḻikka oḻiyādu – cannot be removed here by you or by us; aṟiyāda piḷḷaigaḷōm – we, the innocent little girls (who don’t know the norms of the world); unṟannaic ciṟu pēr aḻaittanavum – called you with an insignificant name; anbināl – due to affection; nī – you (who loves your adiyārs); sīṟi aruḷādē – without blessing us your anger; paṟai tārāi – give (us what we) required

**Перевод:**

**“Before I give you all the things you have asked till now and anything more you are going to ask (their real requirement), can you gōpikās tell me what you have done to gain any qualification for getting something from me?”. He is trying to play with them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*===\> āṇḍāḷ is doing saraṇāgaty in this pāsuram. After doing saraṇāgaty here, in the next pāsuram she talks about doing kainkaryam to him forever. (dvayam)*

If there are any other means that we followed, you would leave us to that and let us continue on that path. We don’t have any such means.

([āḷavandār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/01/alavandhar/) asks ’emperumān! sisupālan got mōkśam because he said your names many times, kākāsuran got respite as he fell on your feet, etc.. – they did some upāyam (means) and still you gave them mōkśam, whereas I have not done anything, so why can’t you give me mōkśam?’).

**karṟavaigaḷ** – cows, which don’t have any knowledge.

**pin cenṟu**

We go behind cows. Our living is with the cows and going behind those cows.

![⚠ картинки нет на сайте: abhimana\_sthalam\_mannargud\_rajagopalan](<https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/01/abhimana_sthalam_mannargud_rajagopalan-768x576.jpg>){ kriṣṇan too loves to go behind cows. Cows and calves look at His divine and mischievous face and grow happily. In the accompanying picture see kriṣṇan standing like this as srīvidyā rājagōpālan in the abhimāna stalama of mannārgudi, and see how the calves are looking at him!}.

To gain jñānam one has to go live with a guru. We follow the cows. In narasimha purāṇam 16-13 it says ‘*jñānēna hīna: pasubhi: samāna:*‘ (one who doesn’t have jñānam is equivalent to a cow). Such is our jñānam learned from these cows.

Do we look like we have got some qualifications? Are the cows equivalent to sage vasiśtar? Even the cows imitate and laugh at our covherd behavior.

**kānam** **sērndu**

We go to forest; unlike people with jñānam who with their ability live in villages, towns, and cities who would be able to gain some knowledge, we live in this small place and go behind cows to forest for getting them fed; this forest is not where many saints lived like in sāḷagrāmam etc., – this is one with just grass for the cows (so we don’t have that benefit too).

sērndu – Our legs won’t stay in the village; they would go and be in the forest. (so we don’t have any qualification related to knowledge).

**uṇbōm**

“for vysyas, taking care of cows is the varṇāsrama darmam, so you are all doing that? īsn’t that a qualification?”

“No we didn’t go to the forest with any vairākyam (to follow the darmam) – we don’t go there to do tapas like riśīs; we go there and we take care of ourselves–we eat. (Can you count this as karma yōgam?)”

**aṟivu onṟum illāda**

“You may not have any such jñānam now, but in your previous lives you might have gained jñānam at the level of vidurar or others”

‘onṟum’ – (not even one/none of it) – no we don’t have any qualification in any life whatsoever. We don’t have any ātma jñānam that leads to bhagavat jñānam, nor do we have bhakti which comes after gaining such jñānam.

īsn’t the gōpikās’ eagerness to mix with kriṣṇan a form of bhakti?  It is their nature to be desparate to be with Him – they don’t consider it as a means for getting him.

=\> So, with these, they say they don’t have any karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgam which can be used as a means for reaching him in srī vaikuntam.

[nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) said the same thing in the same order, “*nōṟṟa nōnbu ilēn, nuṇ aṟivu ilēn*‘ (I don’t have any karmas(actions) that is karma yogam, or any jñānam (and so nor any bhakti which is the next stage after jñānam).

[toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/08/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) too sang, ‘*kuḷittu mūnru…*‘ to say that he does not have any karma/jñāna/bhakti yōgams as means.

“You are also talking like those [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), so you do have such jñānam”

**āykkulam** (covherds)

The covherd community that you can be sure of that we won’t have any jñānam;

Our kulam is such that all the lack of qualifications mentioned above are our strength.(*srīvacana bhūśaṇam – utkruśta janmamē srēśtam / ஸ்ரீவசந பூஷணம்****:*** *உத்க்ருஷ்ட ஜன்மமே ஸ்ரேஷ்டம்*)

“Even after seeing us you are saying that we have brains – this is *your* lack of jñānam”.

“You don’t have any qualifications, but you still want mōkśam?”

**un tannaip piṟavi peṟum tanaip puṇṇiyam yām udaiyōm**

kriṣṇan continues, “Those who don’t have any puṇṇiyam (good karmā), cannot wish for mōkśam”

“How can that be? We have got You. We feed food for the puṇyam (you). That is, we don’t say No to your accepting us as your adiyār; so you feel fulfilled. You are the means”.

piṟavi peṟum tanai – people go to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) and pray to him. Are we like that? You were born among us, and mingled with us – that is what we have got.

puṇṇiyam yām udaiyōm – All jñānis say that kriṣṇan is The darmam himself; we have got you, we play with you, do what we want with you. (*நினைத்தபடி விநியோகம் கொள்ளலாம்படி கைப்புகுந்த உன்னை உடையோமன்றோ?)*

kriṣṇan catches them: “You said you don’t have any jñānam (so no puṇyam (good karmā), but now you are saying you have got puṇyam. You are contradicting yourselves.”

“See?! We don’t have jñānam (to talk without contradicting!) \[ [periyavācān piḷḷai](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/09/periyavachan-pillai.html)‘s hāsya rasam\].

“You don’t have any of the yōgams; only a lot of disqualifying aspects, so why should I give you mōkśam?”

**kuṟaivu onṟum illāda gōvindā**

We talked about lack of everything in us, did we say anything is lacking in you? You are everything. Our lack of everything would match with your having everything. We are like a deep pit and you are like a mountain that could perfectly fit into that pit; so we have got a good match.

We will have to lose our prize due to our lacking everything, only if You lack anything.

We don’t have to lose you because of not having anything else. (no other means, only you are our means to reach you). Thus this shows their upāya-nairapēkśyam.

**gōvindā**

**“**Since you are saying that I am everything, then I can give you mōkśam if I want to, or I can deny if I don’t want to”.

“Did we come to paramapadam to ask you all these? You could not stay there without us, so it is you who were born here to be with us, to be teased by us, and to do all the chores we tell you to do – we are asking you only in such a place (here)”.

This name of gōvindā shows [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s saulabhyam (being able to mix with small level people (all of us)), which is one of the main guṇams of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) that one considers when doing saraṇāgati.

\[ [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) used the name ‘nārāyaṇā’ three times in prior pāsurams. In these three pāsurams (previous one, this, and the next) she calls him ‘gōvindā’. When she said nārāyaṇā, [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) did not seem to be happy – she realized that He is born here in order to enjoy us and not be reminded of his greatness – so to make him happy she called him as gōvindā three times, but the meaning is same – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with saulabhyam for us to do saraṇāgati, and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) with svāmitvam for us to do kainkaryam\].

“only if there is any rule that the one with everything should give to ones who don’t have anything”

“O, that?”

**un tannōdu uṟavu**

“āren’t you our relative? Don’t you have to take care of what we need?”

“Because of our relationship? That will end with a pot of water (last rites)”.

*un tannōdu uṟavēl* **namakku ingu oḻikka oḻiyādu**

( namakku ) Our relationship cannot be broken either by us or by you. It cannot be broken even if we tried together with an understanding between us.

“How can you say for sure?”

“[tirumaḻisai āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/16/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) sang ‘*nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāi nāraṇanē! nī ennai anṟi illai*‘ (I am nothing without you nāraṇā!, and you are nothing without me) – that is our proof”

“But they are your parents, (they would only talk like that)”

“Also see in what you (in the form of our ācāryān) gave in our hands as the first pramāṇam – the source of all references (tirumantram) – see its first word (praṇavam)”.

That is why we said ‘[*nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)‘. You are the savior, we are the ones to be saved – that is our relationship, forever”.

“You are talking about our relationship, with you as gōpikās, and me as your gōvindan; but you called me as ‘narāyaṇan’ instead.

“Is it wrong to call you**,** who is *(in)* everything**,** as nārāyaṇan?”

“My purpose of coming here was to be with you all; so I like it when you call me gōvindan”

If a prince and a common man were in a prison, and after getting released if the common man reminds of those days to the prince it would hurt the prince’s feelings. [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was also having the same emotion.

**ariyāda piḷḷaigaḷōm**

“we did that because of ajñānam due to being ignorant and small”. Sorry that we pushed you about our relationship with you and reminded you of your paratvam.

\[When we do saraṇāgati to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), we accept all our mistakes and shortcomings. For example [nātamunigaḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/22/nathamunigal/)‘s and [rāmanujar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s slōkas related to their saraṇāgati\]

**anbināl**

“We called you nārāyaṇā because of our ajñānam due to our love. So please bear with us.

It is like when we are (wife is) in the same bed, you please do not complain that ‘the legs hit me, arms hit me’ “.

arjunan pleaded to kriṣṇan to forgive him (during bhagavat gīta) for having called him ‘hey kriṣṇa!’, ‘hey yādavā’, ‘hey friend’ without realizing kriṣṇan’s greatness.

**un tannaic ciṟu pēr aḻaittanavum sīṟi aruḷādē**

When you have come to this world to enjoy earning your saulabhyam, we called you with the name of nārāyaṇan instead of gōvindan by mistake due to ajñānam, small age, and love. Please do not be upset with us.

**iṟaivā**

We are asking Not based on thinking about the god of the dēvas, but the god of us covherds.

Would anyone say that their legs and arms did mistakes and be angry with those things? Since we are your property, if you don’t bless us then would it not be a loss for you?

Since you have not learned from a teacher sitting under his seat, (as a sarvajñan (who knows everything)), you do not know that it is not correct for you to be angry with us?

Your saulabhyam does not match with you anger.

**nī**  (you**)**

kriṣṇan tries to back out saying that you have to go through tāyār when doing saraṇāgati.

They say that they have done all of that, and they are here only with [tāyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s (nappinnai’s) blessings and recommendation.

So it is now You who have to act.

nī **tārāi paṟai**

You, who hope that people would not say No when you bless them, please give us paṟai. Please allow us to surrender to you – please give us mōkśam.

**ēlōrempāvāi** – O girl, think, commit, and follow our ways.

This pāsuram is related to first half of dvayam. Through ācāryan and [tāyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) we surrender to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); we learn not to prevent him from pouring his love on us. First half of dvayam is about saraṇāgaty. Next pāsuram is related to second half of dvayam which is about requesting to doing kainkaryam after saraṇāgaty.

This pāsuram talks about doing saraṇāgaty – 1) accepting that we are not capable of doing karma/ jñāna/ bhakti yōgams,  2) understanding that we don’t have any qualifications, and don’t want any qualifications to be the means, 3) praising [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who is complete in everything, 4) understanding our relationship with him, 5) accepting and feeling bad for our mistakes of thinking that there are other means other than Him for reaching Him, and 6) surrendering to Him through our ācāryān;  This thought is saraṇāgaty.

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 29. Cirram cirukale

*ciṟṟam ciṟukālē vandu unnaic cēvittu un poṟṟāmarai adiyē pōṟṟum poruḷ kēḷāi peṟṟam mēittu uṇṇum kulattil piṟandu nī kuṟṟēval engaḷaik koḷḷāmal pōgādu iṟṟaip paṟai koḷvān anṟu kāṇ gōvindā eṟṟaikkum ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum unṟannōdu uṟṟōmē āvōm unakkē nām āt ceivōm maṟṟai nam kāmangaḷ māṟṟu ēlōrempāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**
