gōvindā! – Oh kaṇṇā!; vandu – (we) came (here); ciṟṟam ciṟukālē – very early in the morning; unnai sēvittu – and prayed you; kēḷāi – please listen to; un pon tāmarai adi pōṟṟum poruḷ – the meaning/benefit of doing mangaḷāsāsanam to your golden lotus feet; peṟṟam mēittu uṇṇum kulattil piṟanda nī – you who has born as covherds, who herd the cows and then eat; engaḷai koḷḷāmal pōghādu – cannot be without accepting us in your mind (tiruvuḷḷam); kuṟṟēval – (our) very personal kainkaryam (antaranga kainkaryam – confidential service); iṟṟaip paṟai koḷvāṇ anṟu kāṇ – we did not come here for getting (this) paṟai (drum instrument) given today; eṟṟaikkum – for ever; ēḻ ēḻ piṟavikkum – how many ever births we go through; uṟṟōmē āvōm – (we) shall be related; untannōdu – to you; nām – we; āḷ seivōm – shall be subservient; unakkē – to you only; māṟṟu – kindly prevent; nam – our; maṟṟai kāmangaḷ – interest in other matters

**Перевод:**

**ciṟṟanj ciṟu kālē**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In this pāsuram āṇḍāḷ gives the reason for their coming to kriṣṇan (which she said she would do later, when in the pāsuram of orutti maganāi … yām vanda kāriyam).*

Very early in the morning, the cold morning in which it is hard for little girls to wake up. Due to our angst we did not mind that.

==\> In brahma muhūrtam when we carry more satva guṇam (tranquility); this guṇam would help us have a clear mind to do kainkaryam to [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

In [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/)‘s gośti a question came up: ‘what should we say/request when standing in front of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)?’. Some people said, we should say what kśatrabandu said (*mūdōyam..* viṣṇu darmam 98-33) – ‘I pray to you. please give me mōkśam’. [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) said that these words imply that our praying would be an upāyam (means). That should not be the case. We will start thinking that I did something and I got his grace because of that. More appropriate would be what kāḷiyan said, ‘*sōhamtē* ..(srī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-7-10)’ where, even while he was holding kriṣṇan tight in his body, he requested Him to do all the help to him, that is, even releasing kriṣṇan from him also to be helped by kriṣṇan. So asking for nirhētuka krupai like kāḷiyan is what we need to consider, said [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/).

The time when darkness in minds got cleared and our mind got enriched with bhagavat viśayam.

kālē – in the morning

ciṟu kālē – early morning

==\> ciṟṟam ciru kālē – Very very early in the morning.  ‘kālē’ instead of pure tamiḻ ‘kālai’ is due to the slang of their covherd clan.

***kriṣṇan’s dinacaryai (daily schedule):***

– Early morning (ciṟu kālē) kriṣṇan would go out for herding his cows ([periyāḻvar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) imagining himself to be yasōdai in ‘periyāḻvār tirumoḻi’, feels bad for sending him like that instead of letting him go around to other people’s houses for doing mischief).

– He would enjoy herding cows during the day; play with them; his body would be covered with dust.

– At lunch time he would wait for riśi patnīs to bring him food – he will be eagerly looking in that direction (*nedu nōkkuk koḷḷum patta vilōcanan*), with his big stomach yearning for tasty food;

– In the evening he will casually walk back coming behind all his cows and along with other friends (*kālip pinnē varuginṟa kadal vaṇṇan*), enjoying the nice sunset; all the gōpikās would come out to their door steps to see his beautiful body covered with dust,

– Reaching home, without taking bath, he would eat food along with his father (*appanum uṇḍilan, unnōdu udanē uṇbān*).

– Immediately after filling his stomach, he would go around to all the houses and try to see which girls are available to play with. He would see one girl here, talk to one girl there while giving signals to another, then would play with another, dance with yet another, and so on.

– Very late at night (about 2 or 3am) he would reach home and go to bed.

So when can one meet him in his home??

The best time to get hold of him is very very early in the morning, about 3 or 4 am – that is, *ciṟṟam ciṟu kālē*!!

**vandu**

Our mistake is that whereas You should have come to us to bless us, but due to our desperation to see you we came here first. Please bear with us.

kriṣṇan was so pleased and felt bad that he made these little girls get up so early; and  they cleverly caught him at the right time when he was not busy with other things.

perumāḷ praised guhan for walking from his place to see Him in srungibērapuram; (never mind he had walked all the way from ayōdya to see guhan).

**unnaic cēvittu**

He is enjoyable not only when we have achieved the destiny – even during the time we consider him as upāyam (means). [āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) sang ‘*pala mundu sīril padimin* (tiruvāimoḻi 2-8-4), that is before getting Him, enjoy the kalyāṇa guṇams (divine qualities) of [emperumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) during upāya dasai – while considering him as upāyam.

You would want to come to us and lose yourself in us (like how you walked for dēvas, pāṇḍavas, and you did everything for them; you wanted mahārājar (sugrīvan) as your nātan (master) because he was so dear to you), but instead we came here and we fell on your feet, and not somebody else’s feet – **unnai** sevittu.

Not only that, we came here and did anjali too.

(( cēvittu – anjali – nama: –  If one does anjali to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (by hand, in mind, etc.), He feels He is indebted to that person. How much ever He gives to that person, He does not feel he has done enough. That person, if a srīvaiṣṇava, also would be satisfied only if [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) gives the kainkarya prāpti (both here and in srīvaikuṇṭam). )).

**un poṟṟāmarai adiyē**

Your tiruvadi – divine golden lotus feet – which is priceless, enjoyable, and appropriate – is the means to reach you,  adiy**E –** we don’t get into any other means/destiny – other than your lotus feet.

{

**===\> One of the** reasons we say [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) has done something beyond other [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)**:**

As we know, only [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s feet (tiruvadi) is the means to reach Him. So we surrender to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi. After surrendering, we do kainkaryam –  which is again for his tiruvadi.

We know that dēvas and others made [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) walk with that soft tiruvadi to all places, for their own benefits. What have we, as saraṇāgatas, done? same thing? For our saraṇāgaty we made use of his feet?

We had to, because we don’t have any other means. So it is difficult for us also to be reminded that we made use of his feet for doing our saraṇāgaty. So when we go to next step of doing kainkaryam to his tiruvadi we don’t want to be reminded about our previous deed of using his tiruvadi (as the means).

So, in dvaya mantram’s first half:  “*srīman nārāyaṇa caraṇau saraṇam prapadyē*“, it talks about his tiruvadi (caraṇau) when doing saraṇāgati (prapadyē);  but the second half of dvayam, “*srīmatē nārāyaṇāya nama:*” which is about doing kainkaryam, the mantram does not remind us about his tiruvadi, so there is no ‘caraṇau’ or something in the 2nd half. But it is understood that the kainkaryam mentioned in the 2nd half is for his tiruvadi only.  (dvayam too is finding it difficult to be reminded of our using tiruvadi (as the means)).

So, following the same way of dvayam, whenever all the [āḻvārs](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) sing about doing saraṇāgaty, they too would mention [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi at that time (*un adik kīḻ amarndu pugundu*, and so on), but whenever they sing about doing kainkaryam, they would mostly not mention about his tiruvadi (and it is understood that there also they are talking about doing kainkaryam to tiruvadi).

But, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) did not follow such a pattern. She is *āḻvārgaḷ tam seyalai vinji niṟkum tanmaiyaḷ*. She did not explicitly make use of the word tiruvadi when doing saraṇāgati (even though that is the only option, and that is what is actually used by her as the means), but she wanted to explicitly make use of the word tiruvadi when doing kainkaryam pāsuram only:

In the previous pāsuram of ‘*kaṟaviagaḷ pin ceṟru*‘ which is related to first half of dvayam,  that is, doing saraṇāgaty, there is no mention of tiruvadi. But in this pāsuram related to 2nd half of dvayam, that is, doing kainkaryam, she is confidently using the word tiruvadi, in the phrase of ‘*un pon tāmarai* ***adiyē*** *pōṟṟum poruḷ kēḷāi*‘.  Nobody else has done it this way.

**}**

**pōṟṟum**

pray, and do mangaḷāsāsanam.

**poruḷ kēḷāi**

Why are they saying ‘kēḷāi’ (listen) to the Him who is standing in front of them?

It is because he is looking around at all the gōpikās.

Please stop your parākku looking at us, and listen to us.

He too thinks “it is my dear girls’ talk, so let me listen to them”.

Even if we ask for other benefits/goals from you because of our rajas/tamas, you don’t have to listen to that and you don’t have to give us those things.

**peṟṟam mēittu uṇṇum kulattil piṟandu nī**

Whereas you are known only through sāstrams / vēdās, you came and be born amidst us who cannot eat without herding the cows. (who do not eat unless our cows’ stomachs are filled). You are one among us, so you cannot avoid our subservience to you. We want to achieve our goal because of your birth here too.

**kuṟṟēval**

subservience – which is liked by us, which is our nature. Personal kainkaryams like pressing your legs, you telling us rahasyams.

**engaḷai**

Cows may get some other people to save them, but we cannot be saved by anyone else – we have got only you as our savior.

**koḷḷāmal pōgādu**

This is a command for you. We were enjoying all material things and focusing on samsāram, and you showed your beauty and changed us to think ‘*uṇṇum sōru, parugu nīr, tinnum vēttilai ellām kaṇṇan emperumān*‘ – now after providing us jñānam related to our svarūpam, you cannot avoid giving us the opportunity to do kainkaryam.

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is forcing him saying ‘you don’t have any other option; you have to give us paṟai’  ([*balātkrutya bungtē*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=231) – in taniyan).

As they said this, He says “For doing your nōnbu you take these items and instrument; why are you talking about new things like being subservient and all that”, and he tries to give them paṟai instrument.

After listening to them, he thinks ‘They would not let me go. They would stop and surround me and get what they wanted’, and so he says ‘take the paṟai (instrument)’. (இவர்கள் நம்மை விடார்களாயிருந்தார்கள்; நம்மைத் தடுத்தும் வளைத்தும் கொள்வர்கள்)

**iṟṟaip paṟai koḷvān anṟu kāṇ**

See dear, we did not come here to get the *instrument* paṟai or other things for ourselves.

kāṇ – look, don’t you understand our intention?

koḷvān anṟu – you have to get us;  that is what we are here for. (not for getting things from you).

You are just following the sounds/words (and not the intentions) of what we are saying.

**gōvinda** (you don’t have jñānam, as you have been going behind the cows?)

Your janmam (birth as a covherd boy) does not have the jñānam about the intentions? Can you not see the inner meanings of our requests? You go behind cows; you don’t understand girls’ hearts?  You were away from us for four days and you have become like this.

[bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) said ‘*keduvāi! nāngaḷ iṟṟaip paṟai koḷvān anṟu kāṇ! / கெடுவாய்! நாங்கள் இற்றைப் பறை கொள்வான் அன்று காண்*‘, where “keduvāi” implies ‘you are not having the brain to understand what we intended’.

āren’t you the one who upon hearing ‘*pōgu nambi* / *போகு நம்பி*‘ (go away dear), would come and stand around? (tiruvāimoḻi 6-2-2 / திருவாய்மொழி 6-2-2).

“I tried to use the word in the sense that is understood by most people, but you are saying I don’t understand the intention. okay, I will spend the time with you today if that is what you wanted”.

**eṟṟaikkum ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum**

We want to do kainkaryam to you forever.

When you are in srīvaikuṇṭam we want to be in srīvaikuṇṭam with you;

When you take avatāram, we want to be born during the same time period and be with you in the samsāram.

Like iḷaiya perumāḷ who was with you when you were in the forest, and who was with you when you were back in your kingdom (without wishing to enjoy as a prince).

Like how pirātti is with you there in srīvaikuṇṭam, and does a suitable avatāram when you do avatāram.

Any day, during any number of births, we want to be with you.

**untannōdu uṟṟōmē yāvōm**

This relationship of ours with you should be there forever.  Can the body and its owner be separate? Like how the body listens to its owner, we want you to take our services.

You be the svāmi and we be the belongings of you – this is all we wish for.

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) does not specifically say which relationship – she wants *all* relationships to be Him.

“Is that all? What else are you praying for?”

**unakkē nām At cēivōm**

This relationship is not all. We want to do kainkaryam to you too based on the relationship.

Unlike bharatāḻvān who had to suffer because of separation, we would like to be with you always like iḷaiyaperumāḷ and do kainkaryam.

“So that is it?”

“unakk**E** – not for the enjoyment of both ourselves and you, but, just for you. (like how we tell kids when we give them something ‘idu unakkē unakku’ (this is for you and you only)). It should not be just for our enjoyment, or for both our enjoyment – it should be to make you enjoy it.  Subservience that is mixed with ahankāram would not be puruśārttam (destiny)”.

“Is that it? Let that happen. What next?”

**maṟṟai nam kāmangaḷ māṟṟu**

Please remove any thought in us that we do the kainkaryam for our own enjoyment, āṇḍ remove any thought in You that you accept the kainkaryam for these people’s enjoyment. These two are obstacles, so they ask Him to remove such thoughts in both.

kāmam maṟṟu – they are devoted to kriṣṇan, always looking at his face to see what he will want them to do next. Their kāmam is not like ours, the samsāris. Their kāmam (hindrance) is what the elders wanted them to do – that is nōnbu – for material things. They are praying that they want to do kainkaryam to him without any other agenda from the elders or others.

“Sounds good; is that it?”

“That is not it”

maṟṟai kāmam mārru – When we are with you in srīvakuṇṭam amidst the nitya sūris, you would have to talk to us respecting your office; instead it should be an exchange like how it is here in our verandahs where you call us **“***gōpikās! / பெண்காள்!***“** and we respond “*kriṣṇa! / க்ருஷ்ணனே!*” and we jump at each other and fall on each other.

As they talk about him as the means, him as the destiny, this place’s friendliness, and preference to be in srīvaikuṇṭam and so on,

“Instead of one wish, your hearts are wishing for many things?”

“Since it is you who is prāpyam, prāpakam, and saraṇyan, we wish for all of that. We talked about this place because of this closeness; about that place because it would be without any interruption – our hearts which is fixed on getting your tiruvadi won’t be broken in srīvaikuṇṭam”.

maṟṟai kāmam – maṟṟu is similar to what is said in ‘*maṟṟōnrinaik kāṇāvē (amalanādipirān)*‘.

govindā-maṟṟai-kāmam-māṟṟu : What else are you going to achieve without giving us mōkśam?

In previous pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) said we don’t have any other ways/means to reach you; you are the only means; she also apologized for all the mistakes of ours; it talked about first half of dvayam.

In this pāsuram, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) talks about the beauty of prāpyam that is [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) tiruvadi, our being desperate to reach His tiruvadi;  she destroys any of our thoughts that are against our nature of being a dāsan of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); This pāsuram talks about second half of dvayam – we are forever inseparable from [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s relationship; we are His dāsan; He always blesses us.

gōvindā unakku – ‘akāram’;  unakk**E** – ‘ukāram’;  nām – ‘makāram’   (praṇavam).

‘unṟannōdu uṟṟōmē āvōm’ – means ‘nārāyaṇāya’.

‘maṟṟai nam kāmangaḷ māṟṟu’ – refers to nama:

together, it is tirumantram.

srī vaiṣṇavas who have the taste of understanding their nature of doing kainkaryam, would enjoy the beauty of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi as their destiny; thinking about its safety they would feel the angst, and would avoid thinking of anything other than Him itself as the means; would have strong faith in the blessing they have got for doing kainkaryam, without thinking of any benefits for themselves;

srīvaiṣṇavas – Waking up very early in the morning, going to bhagavat sannidi, doing mangaḷāsāsanam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), confessing to [tāyār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) our mistakes, talking about the importance of [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s avatārams, telling him how we desperately want to be with Him, letting Him know that we don’t want any other benefits, wanting the same kind of conversation with Him that nityasūris enjoy (in srīvaikuntam), and praying Him that He should help destroy those guṇams or thoughts that make us act against our nature of being subservient to Him.

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) and gōpikās wish kriṣṇan to understand all of their thoughts above, and so they look at kriṣṇan’s divine, beautiful, mischievous, and attractive face, and request him for all these so that they can achieve the goal of doing saraṇāgaty: uninterrupted kainkaryam in srīvakuṇṭam.

**ēlōrempāvāi –** Dear girl, think and commit, then follow our path.

This pāsuram īś tiruppāvai, (*tiruppāvai āgiradu ippāttu*) say our [ācāryas](https://guruparamparai.wordpress.com/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/).  In previous pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) performed saraṇāgaty; in this pāsuram she says that all we want to do is kainkaryam to your lotus feet for ever and ever.

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

## 30. Vangak kadal kadainda (and vali tirunamam, etc.)

*vangak kadal kadainda mādavanaik kēsavanai tingaḷ tiru mugattuc cēi iḻaiyār senṟu iṟainji angu ap paṟai koṇḍa āṟṟai aṇi puduvaip paingamalat taṇ teriyal pattar pirān kōdai sonna sangat tamiḻ mālai muppadum tappāmē ingu ipparisu uraippār īr iraṇḍu māl varait tōḷ sengaṇ tiru mugattuc celvat tiru mālāl engum tiruvaruḷ peṟṟu inbuṟuvar empāvāi*

**Пословный перевод:**

tingaḷ tiru mughattu cē iḻaiyār – gōpikās having beautiful face like the moon, and wearing rich glittering ornaments; cenṟu – reached; iṟainji – prayed/prostrated; mādavanai – sriya:pati (tāyār’s husband); kēsavanai – kaṇṇan; kadainda – who churned (for dēvas); vangam kadal – the tiruppārkadal with ships in them (milky ocean); angu – in tiruvāippādi; ap paṟai koṇḍa āṟṟai – and about the way in which they got the (their) well known goal (puruśārttam), (as explained by); pai kamalam taṇ teriyal pattarpirān – (the daughter of) periyāḻvar with freshly blossomed lotus flowers; kōdai – (who is) āṇḍāḷ; aṇi puduvai – (who) rose in the lovely place of srīvilliputtūr; sonna – (aruḷic ceida) and gifted; sangam tamiḻ mālai muppadum – these thirty pāsurams, which are to be immersed in by groups of groups; tappāmē – without fail; uraippār – those who recite; ingu – in this world; ipparisu – in this way; tiru aruḷ peṟṟu – would get the blessings of; īr iraṇḍu māl varai tōḷ – one having four big mountain-like shoulders; selvam – one having all the wealth; tirumālāl – (that is) emperumān who is srīmān; engum – and everywhere (in all the worlds); inbuṟuvar – (they would) be joyous

**Перевод:**

**vangak kadal kadainda mādavanai**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous 29 pāsurams āṇḍāḷ pretended to be one of the gōpikās in tiruvāyppādi, and followed the ways of them (anukāram – enacting) for reaching kriṣṇan. She is singing this last pāsuram as self, as she was living in the great puduvai (srīvilliputtūr) as the daughter of great pattar pirān (periyāḻvār).*

When churning the ocean in earlier avatāram he cleverly did that without shaking any boats; he himself was like a boat; when he rotated while churning, he covered the whole ocean; or when he churned, the whole ocean was pulling all the boats/ships around and the whole ocean was full of it.

Are there boats/ships in tiruppāṟkadal? It is only to show the equivalence of the salty ocean near us with that ocean; it is to say how well he churned that ocean.

He was helping dēvas who wanted the lowly amudam (nectar), with his body aching from the churning; they put the great [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) through this for their viśayāntaram (interest in other matters).

āren’t these gōpikās about singing kriṣṇan? Would it match for them to sing about the one who churned the ocean?

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) had a different agenda when churning the ocean; from the ocean not only did the nectar come out; it also brought out his dear [periya pirātti](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) – he churned it only for getting her, but used as front getting nectar for dēvas (clue: why did he keep churning even after nectar came out?).

==\> So these gōpikās are saying that this is nothing new to him. Like how he used churning of ocean as the front for getting pirātti, kriṣṇan is born here and churned *curd* to get these *gōpikās*.  Similarly, He used the elders’ nōnbu request as the front for getting what he really wanted **–** all these innocent gōpikās. So she sings *kadal kadainda mādavanai…*

\[\[ kadal kadainda: vēdas are like ocean; upaniśads too; meanings in [prabandams](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2013/02/dhivya-prabhandha-pramanya-samarthanam-1.html) are like an ocean; ācāryan churns these oceans and brings out beautiful meanings;

vanga: [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/); so ācāryan shows us [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) by churning the ocean of vēdas, upaniśads, and [prabandams](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2013/02/dhivya-prabhandha-pramanya-samarthanam-1.html).

\]\]

mādavanai – Husband of pirātti. What is available for us to surrender to, and to enjoy too, is available with pirātti; he is near the one who will make Him forgive us, our mistakes, and make Him realize His greatness to accept us.

We feel confident when pirātti is there to support us.

kadal kadainda mādavanai – one who churned the ocean and got pirātti from that.

\[ mā-tavan – ācāryan, who has the great tapas of having [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in his noble heart;  it refers to jñānam as well; *jñānam anuttānam ivai nanṟāgavē udaiyanāna kuruvai adaindakkāl*… said [maṇavāḷa māmunigaḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/) in upadēsa ratinamālai.\]

**kēsavanai –** who gets rid of whatever is not good for his devotees; he who made us get rid of our thinking that we are girls who should not get out of our house (to see kriṣṇan); he who makes all of us get rid of anyasēśatvam (sēśatvam (servitude) for something other than naṟāyaṇan).

kēsavanai – one with beautiful hair;  one who troubles the enemies of his adiyār; one who killed the asuran kēsi;

kadal kadainda kēsavan – When [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was churning the ocean, his hair was waving in the air and it was such a beautiful divine sight. [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) feels terrible that dēvas were focusing on the lowly nectar and missed what was really really important – seeing [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s divine face and his hair and enjoying that, and forgetting other things. To feel better now, she sings, kēsavanai….

[bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html) says: He ate the actual nectar (pirātti) from churning ocean (mādavan), and gave the residue (*சக்கை / cakkai* ) to those dēvas.

mādavanaik kēsavanai – you got pirātti, and you were kind to those who needed your help (dēvas).

mādavanaik kēsavanai – you are mādavan (pirātti married you), because you are kēsavan (have got beautiful hair).

kēsavanai – [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) referred to His tiruvadi in [2nd pāsuram – ‘](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)[*paraman adi pādi*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289)[‘](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=289) and in this last pāsuram is singing about his head, thus singing from His tiruvadi (lotus feet) to tiru*mudi* (lotus head).

{ srīvaiṣṇavas always start mangaḷāsāsanam to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) starting from tiruvadi, and going up to tirumudi, when in temple, etc. }

\[ kēsavan – [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) who killed the horse asuran kēsi.  In bhagavat gītai [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) said that our indriyas (senses) are like horses; ācāryan helps us control our indriyas, so kēsavan as well refers to ācāryan. \]

Now [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) talks about the adiyārs who want [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**tingaḷ tirumugattu**

Like a pleasant moon, gōpikās’ faces have blossomed due to joining kriṣṇan,

His – [***‘***](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)[*kadir*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)[***‘***](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272) [***‘***](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)[*madi*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)[***‘***](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272)[*yam pōl mugam*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=272) – because he sees both puṇyams and pāpams of ours, his face is like sun and moon.

gōpikās’ faces – just nice like the moon. That is because they are with their kriṣṇan.

**==\>** Everyone who enjoys Him will have such a nice and happy face. So happy that others would wonder where they have come from.

As [nammāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) sang, those who enjoy him in srīvaikuṇṭam are too ‘*madi muga madandaiyar / மதிமுக மடந்தையர்’*! (truvāimoḻi 10-9-10 / திருவாய்மொழி 10-9-10).  (women with beautiful faces like the moon).

**cēi iḻaiyār**

They had asked for [*‘cūdagamē*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=819)‘ (27), etc., ornaments, now they are here wearing all the ornaments put on them by Him, while being together with kriṣṇan.

They have got the ornaments that are kriṣṇan’s fondness and his katākśam. Because of this they got the briskness and looks like wearing one container *(ஒரு படி)* full of ornaments.

\[ tingaḷ tiru mugattu sēi iḻaiyār – we siśyas are too; moon does not have its own light. We siśyas get jñānam from ācāryan.  Moon is called ‘madi’, which also means ‘jñānam’. With our ācāryan’s katākśam we would be filled with jñānam.  Our ācāryan puts the ornaments on us which are ātma guṇams.\]

**cenṟu**

gōpikās went to him, without being able to do all these decorations with the ornaments.

\[cenṟu – we siśyas *go* to our ācāryan \]

**iṟainji**

They did ‘*tū malar tūvi tōḻudu, vāyināl pādi, manattināl sindittu, anṟu ivvulagam aḷandānai adi pōṟṟi, sēnṟu sēvittu*, and all that mangaḷāsāsanam.

**angu –** In tiruvāyppādi

**ap paṟai koṇḍa āṟṟai –** the way they used ‘paṟai’ instrument for elders as the front, and surrendered and did kainkaryam to kriṣṇan;

koṇḍa – they asked ‘*nī tārāi paṟai*‘, and he gave himself to them for *eṟṟaikkum* (for ever).

āṟṟai – the way in which they did these.

**\[  ‘ap’ paṟai  – ‘that’ paṟai –  Going even beyond doing kainkaryam to** [**perumān**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)**,** [**āṇḍāḷ**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) **refers to ācārya kainkaryam, which is the ultimate kainkaryam – siśyas to do ācārya kainkaryam (kuṟṟēval) while living here, and to continue the same after going to srī vaikuṇṭam. \]**

**aṇi puduvai –** the main place for the people of this world, the beautiful srivilliputtūr where vada perungkoil udaiyān is also present for us;

Unlike for pirātti or nappinnai, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)‘s birth place is also her husband’s place.

The place decorated with [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/), [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) and vata perum kōyil udaiyān (vatapatrasāyī – presiding emperumān of [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/)).

**paingamalat taṇ teriyal** pattar pirān

pain taṇ kamala teriyal – lotus garland that is cool and nice to the core

pattar pirān – [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) (who is a brāhmaṇōttamar with the beautiful garland), who gave love and jñānam to all the learned people (pattar) by singing tiruppallāṇḍu to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) himself, and hence was named the pirān (leader/king) of pattars.

[periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) who gave her daughter in marriage to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).

**pattar pirān kōdai sonna**

What [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)‘s daughter [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) said pretending (anukāram) to be a gōpikā living in tiruvāippādi.

pattar pirān kōdai – If kriṣṇan’s greatness is that he is ‘*āṟṟap padaittān magan (21)*‘ (son of nandagōpa), [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) is ‘pattar pirān kodai’ (daughter of [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)).

sonna – as [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) listened to the upanyāsakar (vidvān) who came to [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) explaining about nōnbu, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) imagined everything, and after it filled her heart with kaṇṇan, the [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) came pouring out due to bhagavat katākśam.

sonna – [*sruti satas siddam*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=231) – what is told in vēdās, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) sang them in simple tamiḻ for everyone to understand.

**\[ pattar pirān kōdai – she says about her ācāryan – her father –** [**periyāḻvār**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)**. Shows her abhimānam towards her ācāryan\]**

**sangat tamiḻ mālai**

This prabandam which has to be recited and enjoyed together in bigger and bigger groups of adiyārs (devotees); (sangam); īsn’t this the prabandam that the gōpikās from 5 lakh houses got together and enjoyed? ([‘sangam iruppār pōl vandu talaippeidōm’](https://vedarama.com/en/books/tiruppavai/divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=690))

tamiḻ mālai – like how bhūmi pirātti came as [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) to help us, upaniśad became tamiḻ.

tamiḻ – amiḻdu (amrutam) – sweet; simple. (sweet like kriṣṇan; simple like gōvindan).

When [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) came himself to the level of human form as kriṣṇan, bhūmi pirātti came herself as [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/), but she went one step further as usual – as part of [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) she pretended to be a covherd girl. vēdas wanted to do their part, and became tamiḻ [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68).

mālai : [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68). [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) like the flower/garland, is not only our medicine, but it is also for enjoyment, to be carried in our head (sirōbhūśaṇam).

mālai – [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) named [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) as ‘kōdai’ to mean mālai (garland), as He was into doing puśpa kainkaryam (flower) to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). So, [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) *herself* is mālai. And she put together the mālai that is this [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68).

==\> mālai kattina mālai ([tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) constructed by [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/))

==\> māl-aik-k-kattina mālai (māl -\> [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/).  [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) was tied down ([koḷḷāmal pōgādu](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=887)) by [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)).

‘[sevip pūvē](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=819)‘ – kriṣṇan gave one flower to her ears; [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) gave this big tamiḻ mālai to him and to us. With this mālai she tied him and demanded paṟai (mōkśam/kainkaryam).

**muppadum tappāmē  (**muppadum tappāmal) – all 30 without missing any pāsuram.

Like how when even one stone is missing in a priceless necklace it would not look beautiful, it would not be complete. It would be a big loss.

**ingu** – āṇY time in the future; in this samsāram (of ours);

**ip parisu uraippār**

**–** whoever would recite these pāsurams

– ip parisu – with the same mind

– In actual tiruvāippādi, gōpikās got Him and doing kriśnānubhavam; [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) got the same by anukāram (pretending and following what the gōpikās did earlier); So, we who would learn these pāsurams also would get Him in the same way and at same intensity.

**īr iraṇḍu māl varait tōḷ**

kriṣṇan does not have to see our level, but as we sing following the ways of [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/), kriṣṇan’s shoulders would grow big out of love for her.

Those who don’t have love towards him will see only two shoulders;

For us who love [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) and kriṣṇan, his shoulders would appear to grow as we recite [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) and other prabandams.

māl varai – big like mountains; where pirātti and gōpikās can enjoy their krīdai (playing).

**sengaṇ tirumugattu**

The one with the divine face with the eyes that became red because of eagerness to give katākśam and love as we sang [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68).

Whose is this tiru mugam?

**selva tirumālāl**

Lakshmi’s husband.

with the katākśam of husband of lakshmi (tirumāl);

[perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/), the one who is the owner of all worlds (his selvam);

Like in dvayam where pirātti is mentioned in its first half and second half, this pāsuram also mentions pirātti in ‘mādavanai’ in first half, and here in ‘tiru mālāl’ in second half.

**engum tiruvaruḷ peṟṟu**

Both in this world and in srīvaikuṇṭam, pirātti and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s katakśam will be there (for those who recite all the 30 pāsurams of this prabandam)…

**inbuṟuvar**

.. and they will become happy because of getting together with [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) (and do kainkaryam in srīvaikuṇṭam).

**empāvāi –** Think about our ways, commit in your mind, and follow our path.

*empāvāi inbuṟuvar* – like how we girls were happy because of tirumāl, those who recite all the tiry pāsurams of this [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) will also be happy in the same way.

[bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html): wake up very early in the morning and recite all the 30 pāsurams; if not doing that, recite ‘ciṟṟam ciṟu kālē’; if not doing that, at least think about this gōśti (of [bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html), with [bhattar](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/11/archavathara-anubhavam-bhattar-anubhavam.html) reciting [tiruppāva](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68)i (and its greatness).

In this pāsuram [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) as [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)‘s divine daughter says that those who would recite all the thirty pasurams that she sung following the ways of gōpikās of tiruvāippādi for reaching kriṣṇan, would also get the same benefit that the gōpikās and [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) got: receive tāyār and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s katākśam and will live happily in this world and in srīvaikuṇṭam. tāyār and [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) shall take turns to treasure us. *தாயாரும் பெருமானும் மாறி மாறி பரியக் கடவர்.*

**Recited along with tiruppāvai:**

**kōdai piranda Ur** – where kōdai was born  
**gōvindan vāḻum Ur –** where there is kōdai there is gōvindan  
**sodi maṇi mādam tōnṟum Ur** – the place with beautiful gōpuram  
**nīdiyāy nalla pattar vāḻum Ur** – the place where honest devotees live  
**nān maṟaigaḷ ōdum Ur** – where the four vēdas are recited all the time  
**villiputtūr vēdak kōn Ur** – It is [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/), that is the place of king of vēdas.

**padagangaḷ tīrkkum** – will make all the karmas vanish  
**paraman adi kāttum** – will show us [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/)‘s tiruvadi for surrendering and doing kainkaryam  
**vēdam anaittukkum vittu āgum** – the one that is seed/extract of all vēdas, upaniśads, and purāṇas  
**kōdai tamiḻ ai aindum aindum** – [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)‘s tamiḻ prabhandam 5\*5 + 5 ([tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68))  
**ariyāda mānidarai vaiyam sumappadum vambu** – (those who don’t know those 30 pasurams) are a trouble to this world since they would misunderstand vēdas’ meanings and would debate or follow in wrong ways.

**tiru ādip pūrattu segattudittāḷ vāḻiyē** – Her tiru nakśatram is great ādi pūram. bhūmi pirātti was hidden away by hiraṇyākśan; [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) assumed kūrma avatāram, dug and brought out his wife bhūmi pirātti and kept her in his lap. pirātti found herself to be safe, but she was concerned about us the samsāris; she asked [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) a simple way in which we all could reach [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) in srī vaikuntam. He gave varāha carama slōkam. bhūmi pirātti wanted to simplify that further, so she came to [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) as [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/), and sang [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) which explained in simple tamiḻ about the simple way to reach [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/) : saraṇāgaty.  
[**tiruppāvai**](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) **muppadum cēppināḷ vāḻiyē** – She told us [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) having 30 pāsurams  
[**periyaāḻvār**](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/) **peṟṟēdutta pēṇ piḷḷai vāḻiyē** – The girl child of [periyāḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/01/20/periyazhwar/)  
**perumpūtur māmunikkup pinnānāḷ vāḻiyē** – [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) who was born in [srīperumbūtūr](http://sriperumbuthur.koyil.org/) is like her elder brother because he fulfilled her wish of giving ‘nūṟu tadā veṇṇai, akkāra adisil, etc.’ to [perumān](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/divya-dhampathi/). [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) came in front of him and called him aṇṇar (since he fulfilled her wishes like an elder brother), even though she was born much much earlier than [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/). So [udaiyavar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) got another name ‘kōyil aṇṇar’.  
**oru nūṟṟu nārpattu mūnṟu uraittāḷ vāḻiyē** – [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) sang [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) and also nāciār tirumoḻi which contains 143 pāsurams.  
**uyar arangarkkē kaṇṇi ugandu aḷittāḷ vāḻiyē** – who gave herself, that is who got married, to tiru arangan and increased his status.  
**maruvārum tirumalli vaḷa nādi vāḻiyē** – Glory to [srivilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) that is filled with fragrance of malli flowers everywhere.  
**vaṇ puduvai nagark kōdai – malarp padangaḷ vāḻiyē** – glory to one from [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) – Glory to [āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/)‘s lotus feet.

[āṇḍāḷ](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/16/andal/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[uyyakkōṇḍār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/24/uyyakkondar/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[parāsara bhattar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/11/parasara-bhattar/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2012/09/periyavachan-pillai.html) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/12/15/azhagiya-manavala-perumal-nayanar/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[tirunārāyaṇapurattu āyi jananyācāriār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/04/24/thirunarayanapurathu-ay/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[vānamāmalai jīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/30/ponnadikkal-jiyar/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
sudda satvam doddācāryar tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[piḷḷai lōkam jīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2013/04/08/pillai-lokam-jiyar/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
[āḻvār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/18/nammazhwar-english/) [emperumānār](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/06/emperumanar/) [jīyar](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/) tiruvadigaḷē caraṇam  
ācāryan tiruvaidgaḷē caraṇam  
[maṇavāḷa māmuniyē](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/09/23/azhagiya-manavala-mamunigal/) innum oru nūṟṟāṇḍu irum.

Note: Thanks so much to srī U.Ve. Vinjimoor S. Venkatacariar svāmi of [srīvilliputtūr](http://srivilliputhur.koyil.org/) for providing initial guidance when adiyen did the translation. As mentioned in the beginning, our [pūrvācāryās](https://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/2012/08/17/introduction-contd/) have provided so much of wealth related to [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) that they all came in handy in doing the translations, in addition to upanyāsams on [tiruppāvai](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/?p=68) by many great swamis.

A lot of thanks to śaraty ṭhotatri swami for encouraging adiyen to maintain accuracy, consistency, and time. Every day he read through the whole text, made corrections for above reasons, and then published to the group. His tireless efforts made this much more readable than the original.

Finally a lot of praṇāms to the adiyārs who read this translation everyday, and also to those who provided comments and feedback for these posts.

In addition to the vyākhyānams and upanyāsams, there are a couple of other resources which were helpful in doing the translation.

**– tiruppāvai viḷakka urai** – by ‘āḻvārgaḷ amuda nilaiyam’ which is a simple tamiḻ translation of the 3000padi vyākhyānam for the most part.

**– vaiṇava urai nadai varalāṟṟu muṟait tamiḻp pēr agarādi** – by sānti sādana – publishers, 125 Chamiers Road, Chennai 600 028, which is a Gṟēāṭ resource to know the translation of samskrutam words seen in our [srīvaiṣṇava grantams](http://granthams.koyil.org/). It is in tamiḻ. (If interested in purchasing the 3-volume set, publisher’s contact number is *9940019804* in Chennai. (we are not associated with them in any way other than as buyers))

English translation ṟaghuram śrinivasa ḍasan.

archived in https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org
